# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1998

## Introduction by Prahlādānanda Swami

The Vyāsa-pūjā book, with offerings from the followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda, is an opportunity for them to crystallize their transcendental realizations through writing about Śrīla Prabhupāda. Each year these realizations become newer and fresher as the devotees experience how Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON and teachings, by ushering in a new culture of spiritual enlightenment and values, are saving humanity from the degradation of unrestrained materialism and confusion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda often instructed his disciples to write their appreciations. He even stressed the value of writing to a man who couldn’t understand or appreciate the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When Śrīla Prabhupāda visited Buffalo, New York, in 1969, I listened while he repeatedly tried to explain to an Indian gentlemen that Lord Kṛṣṇa is like our father and the *Vedas* are like our mother. As our mother is the authority for understanding who our father is, so the *Vedas* are similarly the authority for understanding who the supreme father is. They will reveal to us how Lord Kṛṣṇa is that supreme father. Unfortunately, the Indian gentleman, being an impersonalist, could neither understand nor appreciate what Śrīla Prabhupāda was saying.

The man then mentioned that he had written an article for *Back to Godhead* magazine but that it had not been published. Śrīla Prabhupāda then began to explain that devotees should express their realizations in writing, and that these writings should be published for the benefit of others.

As Śrīla Prabhupāda said in a lecture delivered in 1972 in Los Angeles:

Realization means you should write, every one of you, what is your realization. What for this *Back to Godhead* is? You write your realization, what you have realized about Kṛṣṇa. That is required. It is not passive. Always you should be active. Whenever you find time, you write. Never mind, two lines, four lines, but you write your realization. *Śravaṇaṁ kīrtanam,* writing or offering prayers, glories. This is one of the function of the Vaiṣṇava. You are hearing, but you have to write also. Then write means *smaraṇam,* remembering what you have heard from your spiritual master, from the scripture.

In the pages of this Vyāsa-pūjā book, many devotees elegantly express their gratitude to and appreciation for their dearest friend and ever well-wisher, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The spiritually uneducated may find some devotees’ offerings trite or repetitive. But when a sincere devotee composes an homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda that is philosophically in accordance with the teachings of the disciplic succession, then Śrīla Prabhupāda and Lord Kṛṣṇa undoubtedly appreciate it despite its literary flaws, just as a father appreciates the incoherent stammering of his young child as he tries to express his affection for the father.

During Janmāṣṭamī devotees around the world have the opportunity to meditate upon Lord Kṛṣṇa’s appearance and activities. The Lord Himself says this meditation helps one become eligible to return to the spiritual world. By Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, the day after Janmāṣṭamī is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance day, a day when we in the International Society for Krishna Consciousness have the opportunity to enhance our meditation on the Lord by increasing our meditating on and praying for the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda. When we receive the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, our understanding and appreciation of Lord Kṛṣṇa develop easily. The offerings in this book are sublime meditations on Śrīla Prabhupāda and inspirations to pray for his mercy. Such meditations and prayers assure our success in life.

Prahlādānanda Swami

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Trivikrama Swami

The Vaiṣṇava tradition celebrates the appearance of the spiritual master in a very special ceremony called Vyāsa-pūjā. This name indicates that every bona fide spiritual master must be a representative of Vyāsadeva, who is known as the original spiritual master because, as the literary incarnation of God, he compiled the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and other Vedic literatures, containing the realized knowledge every genuine guru imparts to his disciples.

The guru-disciple relationship lies at the heart of all the Vaiṣṇava *sampradāyas* and forms the essence of the *paramparā,* the disciplic succession. Thus the guru-disciple relationship is not a detail but is the most vital principle of spiritual life. Kṛṣṇa (God) Himself has established this system of receiving transcendental knowledge, and when this knowledge appeared to be lost He personally came to reestablish the *paramparā* by speaking the *Bhagavad-gītā.*

Every sincere disciple carefully observes the yearly Vyāsa-pūjā festival in the association of other disciples. Together they offer prayers and glorification to the spiritual master. They also offer gifts and cook special preparations for his satisfaction. The bona fide disciples do this in a mood of awe and reverence because they know that in this world of the cheaters and the cheated, by some extraordinary good fortune they have found a person who is actually worshipable because he embodies the Absolute Truth in word and deed. The *śāstras* explain that we may wander for innumerable lifetimes in the material world before meeting such a genuine spiritual master; therefore Vyāsa-pūjā is a real occasion for celebration.

The disciple worships the spiritual master just as he would worship the Lord because the guru is the unalloyed servant of the Lord. The spiritual master never thinks of himself as a master, but on the order of his own spiritual master he takes on the service of training new candidates for devotional service. In this way the spiritual master always considers himself first and foremost a disciple, and this is his real identity—a servant of the servant. By maintaining this consciousness, he can train his disciples in the proper method of honoring the spiritual master, including how to properly conduct the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony.

To an outsider, the Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony—in which the spiritual master sits peacefully and listens to himself being praised by a congregation of devotees—may appear to be the grossest expression of arrogance. As we have seen, however, it is not. Rather, only out of a sense of duty to his own spiritual master and compassion to his disciples does the spiritual master take the risk of being falsely accused in this way, for he knows he must give his disciples an opportunity to praise him if they are to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As the *Gurv-aṣṭakam* teaches:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**

For every member and friend of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, then, Vyāsa-pūjā is a special opportunity to immerse oneself for an entire day in remembrance and praise of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the foremost representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Trivikrama Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Sitting down to write my annual Vyāsa-pūjā offering is inevitably a bittersweet experience. On one hand, the occasion obliges me to confront my many short-comings. Again I am forced to take stock of how little I have done with the mercy received from you.

At the same time, these thoughts are leavened by the sweet and sublime pleasure of remembering you.

Actually, taking inventory of my life is quite simple. Whatever is good, true, pleasing, and valuable, I have learned from you. As it is said, “Your mercy is all I am made of.”

Mahārāja Rahūgana, appreciating how Jaḍa Bharata had opened his eyes, prayed:

It is not at all wonderful that simply by being covered by the dust of your lotus feet, one immediately attains the platform of pure devotional service to Adhokṣaja, which is not available even to great demigods like Brahmā. By associating with you just for a moment, I am now freed from all argument, false prestige and lack of discrimination, which are the roots of entanglement in the material world. Now I am free from all these problems. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* 5.13.22)

Addressing my condition in particular, you write in your purport, “Association with pure devotees certainly frees one from the material clutches.… The arguments offered by pure devotees to their disciples are so convincing that even a dull-headed disciple is immediately enlightened with spiritual knowledge.”

One of my senior Godbrothers once said in a class,

We may well have thought that the descriptions of Kṛṣṇa consciousness found in the *Bhāgavatam* were a fictitious or unattainable state, but there was Śrīla Prabhupāda. He was so obviously Kṛṣṇa conscious, and more amazingly, he confidently stated that we could be also, if we would simply follow him.

You gave your association unlimitedly and unceasingly. I remember when I was a *brahmacārī* in Los Angeles, you used to sit in the afternoons in your little garden. We *brahmacārīs* could peek through the windows of the second floor *āśrama* and watch you as you spoke with guests or heard from *Kṛṣṇa* book or *The Nectar of Devotion*. At times there would be as many as twenty plus young men, all jockeying for position in the window to hear and see you.

Our temple commander concluded that this was a distraction for you, and in no uncertain terms he made it clear that we were not be in that window in the future. The next afternoon you came to take your seat, looked up toward the *āśrama* window, and inquired from that same temple commander, “Oh, where are the *brahmacārīs*? Go and get them.”

Later on, in India, after you had traveled around the world a dozen times, opened scores of temples, initiated thousands of disciples, preached on a scale unprecedented in the history of the world, when you were presented with an opulent plate of *prasādam* you commented, “I am now an old man. I have no appetite and I have no teeth.” Then you considered further, “No eating, I sleep very little, and I work all day and night. Minimal cost and maximum labor; very good investment for my disciples.”

You gave and gave, and only asked in return that, as we felt relief and satisfaction by following you, we do our best to try to give this same knowledge and experience to others. You said, “As you have been delivered, now go and deliver others.”

Once, in Māyāpur, one of your *sannyāsī* disciples saw you looking over the railing at something. Coming up alongside you, he saw that you were watching a poor woman and her son picking through the trash, looking for some morsels of food. The disciple said to you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, sometimes I feel so sorry for these people.” You turned to him and, with a deep and penetrating look, asked, “Why just sometimes?” (You began your “Food for Life” program from this point, giving us the goal that no one should go hungry within a twelve-mile radius of a temple.)

Your wonderful association and its efficacious effects are not limited to those who were able to meet you personally. The *ācāryas* are not personalities limited to mundane time and space. You explained this principle in reference to your own spiritual master:

So my Guru Mahārāja will be very, very much pleased upon you and bless you with all benefits. So he wanted this, and he is not … It is not that he is dead and gone. That is not spiritual understanding. Even ordinary living being, he does not die. *Na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre*. And what to speak of such exalted, authorized personality like Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. He is seeing. I never feel that I am alone. Of course, when I came to your country without any friend, without any means… Practically, just like a vagabond I came. But I had full faith that “My Guru Mahārāja is with me.” (Vyāsa-pūjā lecture, 1975)

Again you made this point on a morning walk in San Francisco:

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. My Guru Mahārāja passed in 1936, and I started this movement in 1965, thirty years after. Then? I am getting the mercy of guru. This is *vāṇī*. Even the guru is not physically present, if you follow the *vāṇī*, then you are getting help.

**Disciple:** So there’s no question of ever separation as long as the disciple follows the instruction of guru?

**Prabhupāda:** No. *Cakhu-dān dilo jei…* What is that, next one?

**Disciple:** *Cakhu-dān dilo jei janme janme prabhu sei*.

**Prabhupāda**: *Janme janme prabhu sei*. So where there is separation? He who has opened your eyes, he is birth after birth your *prabhu*.

I mention this principle of timeless association for two reasons. One is that your mercy and association are still available to anyone who sincerely tries to follow your instructions. The other is that your mercy and association are still flowing through your institution, ISKCON, and your steady hand is still there to guide your preaching mission.

Your disciple Rādhānātha Swami related a mystical pastime giving support to the above—an event in relation to your New Vrindaban. The history is that this farm community had drifted away from your standards and teachings. After a difficult period, those discrepancies have been rectified, and recently New Vrindaban has come back to your lotus feet. The story told by Rādhānātha Swami is as follows:

In February of this year the devotees in New Vrindaban received a box in the mail. When they opened it, they found a pair of your shoes—the same shoes that had mysteriously disappeared from your *vyāsāsana* years ago. At the time of the shoes’ disappearance, the general conclusion was that out of misplaced sentiment some devotee must have taken the shoes for his or her own worship. However, inside the box with the newly returned shoes was a letter with the real explanation.

The letter was from a middle-aged housewife. Seven years earlier she had come to your Palace with some friends, on one of the many bus tours. As she entered the Palace, she heard a voice declare, “Take my shoes.” She looked around, and not seeing anyone speaking, disregarded the voice. But again she heard the voice: “Take my shoes.” No one else seemed to be hearing the voice, and she became quite unnerved. Again she heard the voice: “Take my shoes.” By this time she was in a cold sweat, thinking that she was having some kind of psychotic event. Those around her were taking no notice, oblivious to the voice she alone was hearing. Again came the order, “Take my shoes.”

Seeing your shoes lying on a cushion in front of your *vyāsāsana,* she made her move. She related in her letter how she was a moral Christian and had never stolen anything in her life, but she saw no alternative to ending this disembodied calling. She went up to your *vyāsāsana* and slipped the shoes in her purse, and the voice stopped.

In her letter, the lady went on to explain that she had kept the shoes in her closet all these years. Now, the night before she had written the letter, she had had a dream. In that dream she recognized (from seeing your *mūrti* in the Palace) that you had come to her. When you spoke to her in that dream, she realized that it was your voice she had heard so many years ago at the Palace. You said to her, “Send my shoes back; I have returned.”

Later on, when I related the story to some *saṅkīrtana* devotees, one of them told me of a person they had recently met. The devotee was at a book table, dressed in devotional attire, when a typical American tourist came up to him and asked, “Do you have the *Bhagavad-gītā*?” When the devotee asked the man if he wanted hard-bound or soft, with Sanskrit or not, the man replied, “I don’t know anything about it. All I know is that an elderly monk keeps coming to me in a dream and saying, ‘Buy my *Bhagavad-Gita.*’ You are the first person I have seen who is dressed like him, so I was hoping you would know what was meant by all this.”

When the devotee handed the man your *Gītā,* he turned the book over and, seeing your photo on the back, went white as a sheet. He called his wife over: “Come and see this! Come and see this! This is the man who has been coming to me in those dreams.”

The above accounts are just a taste of the many confirmations that those who never met you while you walked the earth can still experience your presence and mercy. It is not just a sentiment. After hearing of the results of an especially powerful Christmas marathon, you wrote how the grandfather is even more merciful than the father, and therefore Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura would pour his mercy down on the *saṅkīrtana* devotees.

At the same time, the above does not suggest jumping over the principle of disciplic succession. If these experiences are to be sustained and bear their full fruit, they must be honed, focused, and built upon by serious association with and surrender to your devotees.

Physically, you left us over twenty years ago. In your purport describing Queen Vaidarbhī’s lament after the death of her husband, you reveal how the disciple should feel upon the departure of the spiritual master.

A devoted disciple of the spiritual master would rather die with the spiritual master than fail to execute the spiritual master’s mission. As the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes down upon this earth to reestablish the principles of religion, so His representative, the spiritual master, also comes to re-establish religious principles. It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master.

To go on without your presence would be impossible. To not have your association is worse than death. But when I am reminded that you are still with us, that you are still the *senapati bhakta,* that great general leading the Vaiṣṇava army in the fight against Māyā, I am revived and I have hope.

Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna in the Eleventh Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* “Even if you don’t fight, the outcome of the battle is fixed. The only question now is whether you become part of the glory.”

To achieve this victory over the forces of Kali, ISKCON is the vehicle you so carefully crafted, and which your powerful hand still guides. Success is guaranteed by the *śāstra,* the previous *ācāryas,* and your own bold declarations. May I always remember that you are still with me and thus become a surrendered servant in your hands.

Your insignificant disciple,  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

Today we are gathering together to celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā day, to glorify you as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. There may be many Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations of many advanced devotees during the course of the year, but yours is special, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva is the literary incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and so he is the greatest authority on Vedic knowledge. Therefore, among devotees he is special. In the *Padma Purāṇa* it is stated:

*dvaipāyanena yad buddhaṁ brahmādyais tan na budhyate*  
*sarva-buddhaṁ sa vai veda tad buddhaṁ nānyagocaram*

**“Vyāsa knows what even Brahmā and the others do not know. He knows all that can be known, while what is known to him is beyond the reach of others.”**

Śrīla Vyāsadeva was the only person who could reorganize the Vedic literatures for the audiences of Kali-yuga. And as his empowered representative, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taken his writings, translated them into English, and made them understandable and available to the fallen souls of this age. Therefore you are clearly his special ambassador to the people of these times, and anyone who wants to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this day and age should recognize your unique position and work under your shelter.

The *Skanda Purāṇa* states:

*vyāsa-citta-sthitākāsad avacchinnāni kānicit*  
*anye vyāvaharanty etāny urikṛtya gṛhād iva*

**“Others have borrowed bits and pieces from the ethereal realm of Vyāsa’s mind for their own use, just as one would remove objects from a house and use them.”**

Your contribution to the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is very special, and should be recognized as such. As Śrīla Vyāsadeva was able to establish the truth of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a way that no one else could, similarly your ability to establish his message in this day and age is something no one else can emulate. We as your followers are just taking bits and pieces from your mind and trying to present them as they are to the conditioned souls, and we see the results of doing that are most wonderful. There is immense transcendental potency in your words.

Unfortunately, some people who are less than completely scrupulous try to borrow bits and pieces from your teachings, or from your ISKCON, but without giving you the credit. However, we are convinced that without giving you the respect you are due, they will not be able to be successful for long.

You are the empowered *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* sent by Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Lord Caitanya to fulfill their mission of delivering the fallen souls. No words of praise can actually do justice to your glories and inconceivable mercy. You are unique among devotees.

At the same time, you set the perfect example for all of us by being the ideal disciple. Your dedication to the mission of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is beyond compare, so we have had the opportunity to learn from you how to continue the disciplic succession. If we are absolutely faithful to you, we will be able to play a part in furthering the mission of the Lord, and we can help transform this iron age of Kali into a golden age of pure devotional service.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being unique. Thank you for giving us the unique gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as it is. Thank you for everything.

Begging to remain engaged in your service,  
Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

All your sincere followers are spreading your glory in so many wonderful ways. Inspired by their dedication to Your Divine Grace, I embarked on a project to broadcast your glory through a video epic called “Abhay Charan.” On this most auspicious day I seek your blessings to successfully complete that project, so that the entire world can watch it and recognize your greatness. Let that be my humble offering at your lotus feet.

Your insignificant servant,  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano- ’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to all your dedicated servants, who are working relentlessly to help you realize the vision you have given us, a vision which encompasses the whole world and is meant to usher in a new age and a new social order.

Glimpses of a glorious future not so far away are beginning to appear, and the holy land of Bhāratavarṣa, befittingly, is taking the lead in giving us some amazing insights into things soon to come. Although I have not personally seen the magnificent temples recently built in Bangalore, Ahmedabad, and New Delhi, it is obvious that the desires of Lord Caitanya and the previous *ācāryas* are beginning to manifest through such offerings of devotion. Every time your ISKCON society takes a tangible step in more firmly establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world, this directly reflects on you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the inspiring and mobilizing force behind all such accomplishments

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura predicted that one day high-court judges would wear *tilaka* in court as Kṛṣṇa consciousness became more and more accepted in society. News of the recent inauguration of the New Delhi Temple and the Glory of India Vedic Cultural Center, the powerful devotional speech delivered by the prime minister of India, the Honorable Śrī Atal Behari Vajpayee, strengthened our already firm conviction that Lord Kṛṣṇa is masterminding and unfolding before our very eyes our bright Kṛṣṇa conscious future. How wonderful it is to hear such eloquent glorification of your ISKCON society! How pleasing to hear the message of the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* promoted globally by the leader of the world’s largest democratic country! And how satisfying and inspiring to hear you praised—you, our spiritual guide and father, who are boldly and vigorously leading your army of spiritual soldiers against the faltering and ailing agents of Kali-yuga! We cannot but feel extreme pride, gratitude, and indebtedness to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making all of this possible. We are told that the prime minister of India is himself a Vaiṣṇava devotee who always wears Rāmānuja *tilaka* and the Vaiṣṇava dress. In this way India is showing the way to the future reforms, which are in the process of taking place under our very eyes.

Just a few days ago we were most fortunate to get a glimpse of the soon-to-be-released ISKCON interactive video presentation. All of your dreams and aspirations are being vividly portrayed and conveyed in this breathtaking and moving presentation. The most awesome and most daring of all your dreams and aspirations is now beginning to take shape as plans for the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium in Śrīdhām Māyāpur begin to take shape. This is not simply some building but the tool and medium by which all of your dreams and that of the previous *ācāryas* will gradually unfold. The prediction of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu and the *ācāryas* must come true and is coming to be at this very moment.

As predicted in the *śāstras,* Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement will remain for ten thousand years, and the focal point of the movement, the heart and inspiring force, will be situated at the birthplace of Lord Caitanya, Śrīdhām Māyāpur. Every year now hundreds and thousands of devotees from India and around the world converge on Śrīdhām Māyāpur during Gaura-Pūrṇimā to receive the *darśana* of Lord Caitanya and get a glimpse of how the *saṅkīrtana* movement is expanding more and more in all parts of the world. We have seen how the completion and opening of your *puṣpa-samādhi* has attracted literally thousands of people daily to take your *darśana*. We cannot begin to imagine or estimate how many hundreds of thousands of devotees will converge on Śrīdhām Māyāpur once the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium is completed. This means that your glories will be sung more and more all over the world as people come in contact with your devotees who will visit Śrīdhām Māyāpur, and the flood of love of God will gradually inundate every town and village.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your complete faith in and dedication to the instructions of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, are the cause of your astounding and unparalleled success. I can only pray that one day I may also develop such firm faith and conviction. All good qualities are always manifest in such a surrendered soul as yourself. Lord Kapila very nicely describes the characteristics of the pure devotee in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.25.21), and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the embodiment of such a rare and exalted preacher devotee:

*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥ śāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

**“The symptoms of a** ***sādhu*** **are that he is tolerant, merciful, and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime.”**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me this opportunity to engage in your devotional service. I am not always a very good servant, and I am not very eloquent in glorifying you. I have many faults and shortcomings, but I do have that one desire to serve you always and somehow try to assist you in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I sincerely pray that you will be kind upon me and continue to shower your mercy although I remain so undeserving and unqualified. May your fame and glories continue to be spread and sung in all the three worlds.

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As I recollect and gather your divine instructions to me during a span of eight years from 1970–77, I begin to realize the significance of making Manipur a “Center of Scientific Vaiṣṇava Culture.” You mentioned several times that Manipuris are descendants of Babhruvāhana, the son of Arjuna, and that it will be possible to make this state a completely Vaiṣṇava state. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said,

So … make Manipur a Vaiṣṇava state … There must be some state, ideal state. Just like Russia is a Communist state… Similarly we must have a Kṛṣṇa conscious state. \[Room Conversation, Māyāpur, February 28, 1977\]

Let us have a small ideal state. If respectable gentlemen take it, oh, it will be a great success, an ideal state throughout the world, Vaiṣṇava state… Show there policemen, all with *tilaka,* and marching, “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.” \[Room Conversation, Māyāpur, February 20, 1977\]

Now Manipur, make a very enlightened state. I have got very good respect for your state because Arjuna is there, Arjuna’s son. So you make an ideal state, Indian culture. I wanted to go only for this reason, that you take it very seriously, make a Center of Scientific Vaiṣṇava Culture. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda Conversations—Extra\]

Under your divine guidance, ISKCON Manipur is launching a **University of Bhāgavata Culture** to revitalize the Vaiṣṇava tradition of Manipur. This university will be based on the essential instructions of Śrīla Nārada Muni to Śrīla Vyāsadeva contained in the *Bhāgavatam śloka* 1.5.22, which you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have quoted many, many times in connection with preaching to the intellectuals and scientists:

*idaṁ hi puṁsas tapasaḥ śrutasya vā*  
*sviṣṭasya sūktasya ca buddhi-dattayoḥ*  
*avicyuto ’rthaḥ kavibhir nirūpito*  
*yad-uttamaśloka-guṇānuvarṇanam*

**“Learned circles have positively concluded that the infallible purpose of the advancement of knowledge, namely austerities, study of the Vedas, sacrifice, chanting of hymns and charity, culminates in the transcendental descriptions of the Lord, who is defined in choice poetry.”**

In the center of the university campus will shine a jewellike Maṇimandir, the divine home of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇacandra. Other departments at the university will include a Śukadeva School of Vaiṣṇava Vedānta, a College of Sanskrit Studies, a Bhaktivedanta Institute of Science, Philosophy and Culture, a prestigious Vedavyāsa Library, a Babhruvāhana School of Martial Arts and Administration, a Dhanvantari School of Āyurveda and Alternative Medicine, a Chitrāṅgada Women’s College of Vocational Training, a Caitanya School of Saṅkīrtana, a Bhāgyachandra Rasa Maṇḍala Auditorium, and a Vedic Museum. The project is well underway, and when completed it will serve as a center for cultivation and propagation of Bhāgavata culture.

At this point Manipur is facing a great influence of the symptoms of Kali—the age of dissent and quarrel—and everyone is in great anxiety. By your divine blessings, when the university starts functioning next year, we’re confident that all these troubles and difficulties will be overcome. So on this most auspicious day we seek your divine blessings so we can complete this project timely.

Your servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

*śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala bhakati-sadma,*  
*vando mui sāvadhāna mate*

My Dear Spiritual Father,

Please accept my humble obeisances on this most honorable day of your appearance. I am intoxicated with gratitude!!!

I would first like to thank you for all of your wonderful books, which are not only the daily guides for your disciples and grand-disciples but which are offered as the spiritual law books for humanity at this critical period in world history.

Thank you for your BTG and HKW, which your wonderful disciples are maintaining to help the world to learn more about how your devotees are applying the Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for arranging for Kṛṣṇa to descend in His *arcā-vigraha* form all over the planet so we can be freed from our attachments to *nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi*.

Thank you for allowing us to have your own *mūrti* in our presence so that we can personalize our meditations and prayers to you daily while we gaze upon your transcendental form overlooking all of our worship activities and ceremonies.

Thank you for giving us *japa*, *kīrtana,* and *harināma* so we can vigorously call on the names of God in private as well as in public so we can advance while sharing with everyone the medicine that can revive their dormant consciousness.

Thank you for giving us the Food for Life program. You have ordered us to distribute *prasādam* profusely to millions of souls who are imprisoned in their material bodies so that they can begin to be stimulated about their eternal freedom.

Thank you for introducing us to the *gṛhastha-āśrama* so we can understand the difference between the *gṛhamedhī* and the *gṛhastha* lifestyles. Now we have so many wonderful householders all over the world who are struggling to fulfill your request to bring Kṛṣṇa conscious children into the world, offering themselves and their children to guru and Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you for introducing us to the *sannyāsa-āśrama*. Now so many young men all over the world are constantly traveling, having become civically dead becoming more and more spiritually alive as they try to surrender their entire essences to your mission.

Thank you for establishing a GBC. These men (and now women) have no private life or vacation; they brainstorm day and night trying to see how to remain loyal to you and fulfill your vision for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness at this very challenging time in ISKCON as well as in the world.

Thank you for sharing your vision of a spiritual city in Māyāpur, which will serve pilgrims from all over the world and help millions to have a better appreciation of transcendence.

Thank you for ordering us to establish spiritual rural communities so as to aid us in giving better care and protection to our devotees, assist the materialists, and show the world a viable system for simple living and higher thinking.

Please forgive us for being slow in this area, and please empower us to become so much more appreciative of all you have given and all the direction you have supplied that we harness this enthusiasm to expand all of the existing projects and complete all of the outstanding ones. Without your additional mercy, this will be impossible. But as you are like the magnanimous *surabhi* cow who constantly pours out mercy, there is great hope for all of us because your mercy constantly proves to be unlimited. Above all things we thank you for being consistently merciful even when we do not deserve it.

Your thankful son,  
Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I was blinded by darkness—you made me see.  
I saw no light—you brought the candle.  
I was locked behind doors—you handed the key.  
I was forsaken by others—you appeared like an angel.

I was stumbling and falling—you caught me in time.  
I was hurt and injured—you healed my wounds.  
I am hearing your voice—so sweet and divine,  
Soothing and cooling—like millions of moons.

In desperate hours—you stood by my side.  
In fear and danger—you made me bold.  
I was weak and forlorn—you gave me the might:  
“Don’t give up,” “Don’t give up”—you’ve told.

When I couldn’t hear—you were my ears.  
When I couldn’t think—you were my mind.  
In pain and sorrows—you uttered cheers.  
Oh, I am missing you—who are so kind.

When I couldn’t walk—you were my feet.  
When I couldn’t speak—you were my voice.  
Your shelter so kind—forever I seek.  
Your Divine Grace—I’ve no other choice.

The world is in danger—your message is clear.  
Your merciful glance—forever I yearn,  
Delivering your words—make them hear.  
The only hope—sensibleness to return.

My lord and master—you are my life.  
I beseech you—do not cast me away.  
Cut through my nescience—as sharp as a knife.  
Save this soul fallen—take me away.

This day so sublime—so dear to all.  
Let your fame shine—as bright as the sun.  
Bowing to your feet—your name we call,  
“Srila Prabhupada!”—your worthless son.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine and powerful lotus feet, which, although appearing to be of this world, are far beyond it. Your full and ecstatic life in the selfless service of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa proves the *prema* that you carry. You care only for their pleasure, and their service is natural and effortless for you. Your preaching success, executed alone, also bears witness to your love, as it cuts a channel of mercy westward that seems to gain more and more strength as it continues to circle the globe.

All I have in this world and in my spiritual life has truly come by your divine grace. I feel such a relief to have such a kind and powerful spiritual master as you, and I wonder when I will actually become your servant. I am awed by your tolerance: after all these years, though I am who I am, you still accept service from me and allow me to continue my attempts to become a devotee. You thus prove what all say: you are an ocean of mercy.

You asked me years ago, during my first visit to Vṛndāvana, in 1974, “So, how do you like Vṛndāvana?” The vibrant look in your eyes expressed your own enthusiasm for the holy *dhāma*. I would like to take this opportunity, twenty-four years later, to thank you for bringing me to that holy land. Although my view of Vṛndāvana is limited by my failings as your disciple and my lacks as a servant of Kṛṣṇa, that gift of yours nevertheless means everything to me. Practically speaking, whatever I do glimpse of Vṛndāvana plus your other gifts of the *aṅgas* of *pañcāṅga-bhakti* are my only possessions.

*sādhu-saṅga, nāma-kīrtana, bhāgavata-śravaṇa*  
*mathurā-vāsa, śrī-mūrtira śraddhāya sevana*

**“One should associate with devotees, chant the holy name of the Lord, hear** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*** **reside at Mathurā, and worship the Deity with faith and veneration.”**

*sakala-sādhana-śreṣṭha ei pañca aṅga*  
*kṛṣṇa-prema janmāya ei pāṅcera alpa saṅga*

**“These five limbs of devotional service are the best of all. Even a slight performance of these five awakens love for Kṛṣṇa.”**

*aṅgānāṁ pañcakasyāsya pūrva-vilikhitasya ca*  
*nikhila-śraiṣṭhya-bodhāya punar apy atra śaṁsanam*

**“The glorification of these five items \[association with devotees, chanting the holy name, and so on\] is to make known the complete superiority of these five practices of devotional service.”**

*durūhādbhuta-vīrye ’smin śraddhā dūre ’stu pañcake*  
*yatra svalpo ’pi sambandhaḥ sad-dhiyāṁ bhāva-janmane*

**“The power of these five principles is very wonderful and difficult to reconcile. Even without faith in them, a person who is offenseless can experience dormant love of Kṛṣṇa simply by being a little connected with them.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your lowly servant Bhūrijana begs you, his exalted master, to kindly continue his tolerant treatment despite my testing your affection. Please award me the causeless mercy of the shelter of *pañcāṅga-bhakti*. I pray also for the ability to carry these sparkling jewels to others, as your servant.

Most fallen, but yours,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to you! All glories to your dedicated servants!

I know that a disciple is not supposed to ask anything from the spiritual master, but I am so audacious that I have the following requests to make from you:

I beg you for the potency to change others’ hearts and give them pure devotional service.

I beg that you give me compassion for all living entities so that I can give up my selfish desires.

I beg that you engage me in your service life after life, and never kick me away from your lotus feet.

I beg that you give me a taste for hearing, chanting, and the other processes of devotional service.

I beg that you always give me the association of your sincere servitors.

I beg that you give me dedication and loyalty to your society, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

I beg that you allow me to serve the other members of ISKCON.

I beg that you eradicate all the lust, anger, greed, illusion, envy, and other *anarthas* that continue to plague my consciousness.

I beg that you give me full faith in the holy names of Kṛṣṇa.

I beg that you continue to allow me to render personal service to your lotus feet.

I beg that I will have unflinching loyalty to your lotus feet and never harbor any doubts that by just serving you all perfection will be there.

I beg that I be empowered to do some service that you will be proud of.

I beg that you allow me to appreciate the amazing service that your followers are rendering.

I beg that you allow me to be detached from my senses, particularly my voracious tongue.

I beg that you give me insight into your desires so that I can become a first-class servant.

I beg you give me the strength to be always enthusiastic in your service.

I beg you to allow me to chant offenselessly.

I beg that you always allow me to have your association.

I beg that my mind will always be fixed at your lotus feet.

I beg that you give me the strength to be strict in the process of *sādhana-bhakti* so that I may always set a first-class example for others.

And I beg that you allow me to continue begging for these boons and other boons that will please you.

Your eternal servant,  
Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

**ISKCON: Five Potent Forms of Devotional Service**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your Divine Grace on this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day.

We have all heard your well-known saying “ISKCON is my body.” Most ISKCONians take this statement literally and believe that ISKCON, like your spiritual body, is transcendental. You created and guided ISKCON so that its followers could progress from *śraddhā* to *prema* and beyond.

But some devotees, both inside and outside of ISKCON, view this stand as fanatical or at least sentimental. “After all,” they say, “the institution is only a mundane organization. And how can a mundane organization bring its followers to the stage of advanced Kṛṣṇa consciousness?”

But you saw ISKCON in a different light. Often referring to yourself as a *rūpānuga,* a faithful follower of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, you based your Society on his teachings:

“The present Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is also based on the authority of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda. We are therefore generally known as *rūpānugas,* or followers in the footsteps of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda.” (NOD Preface)

Therefore, it comes as no surprise that you chose five items of devotional service from Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s list of sixty-four as the foundation of ISKCON’s spiritual program. You write:

“Rūpa Gosvāmī has stated that five kinds of devotional activities—namely residing in Mathurā, worshiping the Deity of the Lord, reciting *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, serving a devotee and chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra*—are so potent that a small attachment for any one of these five items can arouse devotional ecstasy even in a neophyte.” (NOD Chap. 13)

Indeed, ISKCON rests on these pillars of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, daily *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, opulent Deity worship, *guru-pūjā* (serving a devotee), and the creation of expansive facilities in Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana.

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* quotes a statement by Rūpa Gosvāmī that these devotional activities have extraordinary potency:

*durūhādbhuta-vīrye ’smin śraddhā dūre ’stu pañcake*  
*yatra svalpo ’pi sambandhaḥ sad-dhiyāṁ bhāva-janmane*

**“The power of these five principles is very wonderful and difficult to reconcile. Even without faith in them, a person who is offenseless can experience dormant love of Kṛṣṇa simply by being a little connected with them.” (*****Madhya*** **22.133)**

Although these five items are mentioned previously among the sixty-four items of *sādhana-bhakti,* Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī again focuses on them as the last five of the sixty-four to underscore their special nature. He says that these principles can evoke “dormant love of Kṛṣṇa.” Paraphrasing Rūpa Gosvāmī in your *Nectar of Devotion,* you say, “a small attachment for any one of these five items can arouse devotional ecstasy even in a neophyte.” Devotional ecstasy is a characteristic of *bhāva-bhakti,* not mere *sādhana-bhakti*. Therefore, these activities will arouse *bhāva-bhakti,* or ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa, even in a neophyte, if he does not commit offenses.

You have also explained how each of these five potent activities evokes *bhāva-bhakti:*

\[Residing in Mathurā:\] Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī has described Mathurā-maṇḍala: “I remember the Lord standing by the banks of the Yamunā River, so beautiful amid the *kadamba* trees, where many birds are chirping in the gardens. And these impressions are always giving me transcendental realization of beauty and bliss.” (NOD, pg. 111)

If simply thinking about the Lord’s *dhāmas* evokes *bhāva-bhakti,* we can hardly imagine the benefit for one who visits them. You established wonderful facilities in Vṛndāvana and Māyāpur for all to be purified by the transcendental atmosphere of these holy places. We have faith in your words: “Such transcendental feelings are aroused immediately and without fail after one arrives in Mathurā or Vṛndāvana” (NOD pg. 111). When visiting the *dhāma,* our main business should be to hear, chant and remember the pastimes of the Lord and His associates, without committing offenses.

\[Worshiping the Deity:\] Rūpa Gosvāmī has written the following verse: “My dear friend, if you still have any desire to enjoy the company of your friends within the material world, then don’t look upon the form of Kṛṣṇa, who is standing on the bank of Keśī-ghāṭa… He is known as Govinda, and His eyes are very enchanting. He is playing upon His flute, and on His head there is a peacock feather. And His whole body is illuminated by the moonlight in the sky.” (NOD, pg. 109)

To break our attachment to mundane society, friendship, and love, you taught us to worship Śrī Mūrti with love and devotion. Now, in ISKCON temples worldwide, many thousands “look upon the form of Kṛṣṇa” every day and are attracted by His enchanting eyes.

But you tell us that Deity worship isn’t meant just for devotees in the temples:

“Thus it is the duty of every householder to install Deities of the Lord at home and to begin the process of worshipping along with all of his family members. This will save everyone from such unwanted activities as going to clubs, cinemas and dancing parties, and smoking, drinking, etc. All such nonsense will be forgotten if one stresses the worship of the Deities at home.” (NOD Chap. 13)

\[Reciting *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*\] Rūpa Gosvāmī further writes, “My dear foolish friend, I think that you have already heard some of the auspicious *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, which decries seeking the results of fruitive activities, economic development and liberation. I think that now it is certain that gradually the verses of the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* describing the pastimes of the Lord, will enter your ears and go into your heart.” (NOD Chap. 13)

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the cream of the Vedic literature because it simply glorifies Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, right at the beginning you established the daily *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class on the following principle:

*naṣṭa-prāyeṣv abhadreṣu nityaṁ bhāgavata-sevayā*  
*bhagavaty uttama-śloke bhaktir bhavati naiṣṭhikī*

**“By regular attendance in classes on the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed, and loving service unto the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact.” (SB 1.2.18)**

You sum it up by saying, “By the process of hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* one can attain the highest perfection of life, namely going back home, back to Godhead, without failure” (SB 1.15.51 purport).

\[Serving a Devotee:\] A devotee once said, “It is very astonishing that since I have seen this Personality of Godhead, who is washed by the tears of my eyes, there is shivering of my body, and He has made me a failure in executing my material duties. Since seeing Him, I cannot remain silently at home. I wish to go out to Him always.” (NOD Chap. 13)

In your explanation of this passage, you tell us how we can attain the state of purity necessary for seeing the form of the Lord: “The purport of this statement is that as soon as one is fortunate enough to contact a pure devotee, one must be anxious immediately to hear about Kṛṣṇa, to learn about Kṛṣṇa, or, in other words, to become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious” (NOD Chap. 13).

By serving pure devotees and hearing about Kṛṣṇa from them, one will develop an intense eagerness to serve the Lord always. Our days start with offering you *guru-pūjā* and are filled with executing your instructions. That is your mercy upon us.

\[Chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra:\] “It is said that saints have been able to hear the vibrating strings of the *vīṇā* in the hands of Nārada, who is always singing the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Now this same sound vibration has entered my ears, and I am always feeling the presence of the Supreme Personality. Gradually I am becoming bereft of all attachment for material enjoyment.” (NOD Chap. 13)

Kṛṣṇa and His name are non-different:

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ*  
*pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto ’bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ*

And Narottama dāsa says, golokera *prema-dhana harināma-saṅkīrtana.* “The holy name of Kṛṣṇa comes from Goloka Vṛndāvana.” Thus the holy name has unlimited spiritual potency.

Everything begins and ends with the chanting of the holy name. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays, “There is nothing but the name to be had in the fourteen worlds.” By your example, you taught us to take shelter of the holy name. Even in the midst of Kali-yuga, with all its faults (and ours), we take solace in the benediction offered in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*kaler doṣa-nidhe rājann asti hy eko mahān guṇaḥ*  
*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya mukta-saṅgaḥ paraṁ vrajet*

**“My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: Simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom” (SB 12.3.51).**

“In these statements about devotional service, sometimes it may appear that the results have been overestimated, but actually there is no overestimation.… And for a person who is already in contact with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the described results are not unusual.” (NOD Chap. 13)

There will always be those who say that ISKCON is only a mundane institution meant for neophytes. But ISKCON is your mission, and we are confident that serving your mission includes following the principles of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, which evoke the highest loving sentiments for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, service to your mission means service to Your Divine Grace, to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and to the eternal *paramparā*.

Always begging for your mercy,  
Your fallen servant in the Far East,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāja Swami

**Our Best Friend**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and causeless mercy.

When I first read, in your Concluding Words to *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* how you referred to your Guru Mahārāja as “my beloved eternal spiritual master, guide and *friend*,” I was surprised. But now I also realize, “My Guru Mahārāja is my best friend.”

In one lecture you mentioned that everyone in the material world is looking for a good friend who can help him in his struggle in material existence, someone who can understand him and help him when he is in need. But in this material world, even when a friend tries to understand us, he may misunderstand. And even if he wants to help us, he may be incapable. So our real friend is Kṛṣṇa. He is most close to us. He will always understand us, and He is always able to help. We should make Kṛṣṇa our best friend.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I do not know Kṛṣṇa; I know you, and only through you do I know something of Kṛṣṇa. You are my best friend. You always understand me and are always there, willing and able to help.

From the very beginning you understood me perfectly. I remember when I first met you at the Boston temple in 1969:

You asked for questions. I was burning to ask mine. “There are so many different swamis and yogis, and each one advocates a different process of self-realization and says his process is best. So how do I know which is actually best?”  
You replied, “What is your goal? Do you want to become God, or do you want to serve God? If you want to become God, you are not God now. And how can not-God become God? God is God. He is always God. He doesn’t have to become God by yoga or meditation. Kṛṣṇa is always God, whether He is playing on the lap of Mother Yaśodā or speaking *Bhagavad-gītā* to Arjuna.  
“If you want to serve God and sow the seed of devotional service, and water it by chanting, God will provide the sunlight and all other favorable conditions to make it grow. But if you want to become God, why should God help the competition? God is in your heart. He is ready to help you. So you can become godly—not God. If you want to become God, you are only cheating yourself.”  
While you were speaking I felt you knew everything about me. I felt you were looking right into me, into the town of Waltham to where I stayed, into my apartment, into my bathroom, and onto the wall where I had inscribed in beautiful ornate handwriting—“You are God.”  
“So what do you think?” You asked. “You want to serve God or you want to become God?” I felt so ashamed. My abominable desire to become God had been exposed. Still I didn’t want to admit it. “I want to serve God,” I replied. But I knew that you knew everything and I could not cheat you. Within a fraction of a second I said, “I want to serve God, but actually I realize that I wanted to become God.”  
“*Yes!*” you replied emphatically.  
I offered my obeisances. I knew you were my perfect master.  
\[Adapted from *In Search of the Perfect Master*\]

And in the end, when you were not well in your new quarters in Juhu, Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami and I were having a discussion in the secretary’s room outside. Goswami Mahārāja was asking me to go to the bank and I was arguing with him, saying I could do better things. In the midst of the discussion, you rang your bell and we rushed into your room. You began to preach and quickly came to the point: “If the spiritual master orders, ‘You should go to the bank,’ and you argue, that is not surrender.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you could not possibly have heard our discussion. We were some distance from your quarters and the doors in-between were closed. And always conscious not to disturb you, we had spoken softly.

Thus from beginning to end you understood me perfectly—and still do.

You are so unfathomably, unlimitedly merciful that you have stayed with me all along. Even when I became offensive, when I fell into sense gratification, when I failed miserably, you never gave up on me. You were always with me, waiting for the opportunity to intervene and save me. Who could be a better friend than you?

So many times I have faced difficult problems which seemed impossible to solve, but you understood the problem and arranged the solution. Sometimes you acted from within the heart, or through your disciples and followers, your books and lectures. But many times, directly from the heart you revealed the solution to a problem or brought me back when I had strayed.

You are always there to help—not only me but all who attempt to serve you. You are there when we need to be rectified and when we need to be encouraged. You are there to take away our attachments and to give us facility for service. You perfectly understand exactly what we need and administrate it expertly.

But you are very careful and cautious before you give facility. You test us first to be sure we can handle it—that we will sufficiently value the facility you give us and nicely take care of it and engage it in your service. As you wrote soon after I joined: “Actually, the whole universe is Kṛṣṇa’s property. And Kṛṣṇa’s devotees are his bona fide officers or sons. If He likes, he can entrust the whole charge to His devotees immediately. But He wants to see how His devotees are developing unalloyed Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

Although you fulfill our desires, you first put us through ordeals to test and chasten us. You want to see us rise to the plane of transcendental service, above the dualities of material pain and pleasure, free from material selfishness and sense gratification. If we pass the test and remember you and serve you when we are in difficulty, we may similarly remain fixed in our service to you when you give us facility.

Whatever you give us is extremely precious—Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s service—and you want us to appreciate it fully. To insure we do not take your mercy cheaply, you make us work for it.

But in the end, Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you give us everything, we feel so much love for you and so indebted to you, we just want to serve you more and more. But what can we offer you in return? You have instructed us,

If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. \[SP letter, August 14, 1976\]

And if at all we are successful in preaching, it is only by virtue of your causeless mercy:

A Vaiṣṇava always feels himself incapable to repay his debt to his spiritual master, therefore he works very hard in order to try and repay that debt. He knows that without the mercy of the spiritual master one cannot preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore he always tries to act in such a way that he may please his guru. \[SP letter, August 9, 1976\]

When we endeavor to preach you give us even more mercy—to serve you, the disciplic succession, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus in the attempt to reciprocate and “repay” you, we become even more indebted. Unable to repay our debt to you, we shall have to serve you life after life.

And when, by your mercy, we go to the spiritual world to serve you, there you will be—our best friend.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Yours eternally,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā occasion, I want to offer my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Again and again, the Vyāsa-pūjā comes, reminding us to resolve to take full shelter at your lotus feet. Before writing my present offering, I read and reflected on the offerings written by your other sincere followers. My conclusion is that they are all far more sincere and dedicated to your instructions than I am. They have dedicated their lives to your mission, whereas I am only pretending to do so.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have offered the entire world a way in which to live peacefully under the banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have done what no one else could have done—converted thousands of *mlecchas* into devotees of the Lord. You created an institution, ISKCON, and left behind transcendental literature that will go on guiding humanity for the next 10,000 years. Due to your purity and causeless mercy, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is continuing to expand. The transcendental books that you have planted are bringing newer and newer results all over the world. The Holy Name of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is popular all over the world.

In the past twelve months, three major temples were inaugurated by your society in India. This achievement has surprised most people. In 1997 major projects were inaugurated in Bangalore and Ahmedabad. On the auspicious occasion of Rāma Navamī, April 5th, 1998, your Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārthasārathi Deities were finally installed in their permanent home. It was your desire that a major preaching facility be established in Delhi, which at one time was known as Hastināpura. You once stated that Delhi was the most important city in Asia. When you were living at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana, you used to come to Delhi to preach and distribute your books on the streets and in shops and libraries. The Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is gradually fulfilling all your desires. It was only due to your pure desire and causeless mercy that we were able to obtain a three-acre plot from the government at a very prominent location. By your causeless mercy, difficult things become easy, and impossible tasks become possible. You sent a professional team to help realize the Delhi project. You inspired the top architect of the country to offer his services free. You gradually provided the funds. In what was initially conceived as a US$2 million project has already cost over US$7 million.

A major victory for your movement was the inauguration of the Delhi project by the Honorable Prime Minister of India. This was a milestone achievement. The speech of the Prime Minister was truly impressive. He publicly praised your ISKCON society and Your Divine Grace’s contribution in spreading Vedic culture to every corner of the world. In India, most newspapers reported the inauguration on the front page with a picture of the Prime Minister at your project. The major television channel in India gave twenty minutes of coverage to the inauguration. The inauguration was also covered by the media all over the world. Besides the Prime Minister, many other prominent dignitaries were also present. Here are few extracts from the Prime Minster’s speech.

Distinguished guests, it is my privilege to be present at today’s pious function to mark the opening of this magnificent temple and also the Glory of India Vedic Culture Center in Delhi. The piousness of today’s function is further enhanced by the fact that it is taking place on the auspicious occasion of Rāma Navamī.

I would first of all like to express my deepest sense of appreciation for the vision, dedication, and achievement of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness (ISKCON) and all those who have collaborated with it to present this unique spiritual gift to the nation’s capital.

The ISKCON movement has few parallels in the world in terms of its rapid global spread, its trans-national, trans-ethnic, trans-professional appeal, its outward simplicity, and the devotional energy of its followers. In the less than three and half decades since its inception, it has established temples in practically all parts of the world, and many of them are marvels of beauty, such as the one that is being opened in Delhi today.

The Mahā Mantra of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rāma reverberates to the dancing feet of ISKCON devotees each morning and evening, in temples from Stockholm to Sao Paulo and from Miami to Māyāpur.

For this accomplishment alone, Indians should be eternally grateful to the devoted spiritual army of Swami Prabhupāda’s followers. The arrival of Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda in the United States in 1965, and the particular popularity his movement gained in a very short span of twelve years, must be regarded as one of the greatest spiritual events of the century.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the doer and the ultimate guide for all of our activities. With the passage of time, your fame is increasing, and a day will come when your books will guide the leaders of the world. In recognition of your literary accomplishment, the government of India recently released a commemorative stamp in you honor. Getting the stamp released was not easy. Some influential people tried to sabotage the release, but the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa did not let them succeed. The Lord wanted His pure devotee to be publicly recognized. I do not think any Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* has received such a high honor in recent history from the government of India.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you visited Kurukṣetra in 1976 and manifested a desire that a Kṛṣṇa-Arjuna temple be built at Jyotisara in Kurukshetra. Jyotisara is the location where the *Gītā* was imparted five thousand years ago. After twenty-two years of effort, Kṛṣṇa fulfilled your desire and gave ISKCON a six-acre plot at this auspicious location. Now that the land has been obtained, I am confident that by your causeless mercy the project will also get built. Your mercy and potency are inconceivable.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless me so that I can go on serving along with other devotees in your ISKCON movement. Kindly bless me so that I can please you with my insignificant service.

Your servant,  
Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who has given the misguided civilization of Kali-yuga the implements to fulfill the purpose of human life and achieve ultimate success.

In your *Bhāgavatam* purports you define *ācārya* as he who has assimilated the essence of all *śāstra* and who teaches the same to his disciple. The *ācārya* knows the purpose of the Vedic injunctions, can employ the principles laid down in the *Vedas,* and teaches his disciples to do the same.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have performed the most amazing task of introducing the purest and most complete comprehension of devotional service to the entire world. You where never distracted by floundering disciples and their feeble attempts to serve. You where never attracted to having followers, yet you meticulously took care of everyone, both by instruction and personal care. But in the midst of so many demands from your public, you maintained the vision of the world *ācārya,* and the legacy continues.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you have defined the short-sighted vision of foolish persons:

The word *jīva-hiṁsā* (envy of other living entities) actually means stopping the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Preaching work is described as *paropakāra,* welfare activity for others. Those who are ignorant of the benefits of devotional service must be educated by preaching. If one stops preaching and simply sits down in a solitary place, he is engaging in material activity.

I am one such fool. I humbly request that you give me one drop of your vision, so that I can be useful in your preaching mission. I understand that I must preach, although I have no real desire to do so. But your example is so powerful that I cannot resist, and in spite of my envy, a slight taste permeates my consciousness.

On the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, as I contemplate a little more deeply your transcendental attributes, I feel so insignificant because I have contributed nothing to your movement. By your mercy this fallen soul has remained, trying to fulfill your instructions. By the mercy of your serious and dedicated servants I can fathom what needs to be done. Thus patiently but anxiously I await an injection of transcendental mercy to become more functional, although you have already given me more than I deserve in a hundred lifetimes. Still I beg another chance to become your steady servant.

Your aspiring servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances, thrown in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who have given us this most sublime process of devotional service, full of the potential of love of Godhead. There is simply no way that I can thank you enough for what you have done for me and for countless others in this world, now and in the future.

Each year, a different realization arises in my mind when it is time to write an offering to you. This year I thought, Why should I write you merely some words of praise? I am sure that you will find this common, and I know that I have engaged in praising you many times in the past. In Sanand, Gujarat, you once told me that it is all right that I go on praising my Guru Mahārāja, but the real thing was to preach. Praising one’s spiritual master is an easy thing to do, for it does not imply any commitment to the preaching mission of Lord Caitanya, which is difficult to seriously take up. One can speak many words of glorification, which, although meaningful and proper, dissipate after a few moments, never to be recaptured. Although this is true, such words are not at all meaningless, for they are actually the absolute truth. Kṛṣṇa is happy when we glorify you, for you are His most beloved devotee. Further, praising you is a transcendental sound vibration that purifies us as well.

However, I feel that the time has come when I must offer you something more than my words. I must offer you my life, for without surrendering myself fully to you, I cannot become a valuable part of your preaching mission. It is easy to say this, but to actually do it is quite another story. Anyone can present himself before you and declare that he is giving you his life, and I have seen it done time and again throughout the years. But many who have so declared are no longer to be found in your institution. Offering the life means just what it says. One must keep the life within your movement, fully engaged in your loving devotional service. Indeed, more than that, one must dedicate that life to fulfilling your deepest desires. There is no question of doing anything other than what you have requested, for when one delivers himself at your feet, you take charge of him and demand a very exalted level of surrender with high expectations. When one surrenders his life, he chooses to become a puppet in your hands—a very special puppet that seems to have a life of its own yet is totally pliable to the will and direction of the puppeteer.

When your will is my will, when your desire is my desire, when your words are in my heart and mind, then I can actually claim to be your surrendered soul. As a dog is helpless without a strong and capable master, so I am helpless without your direction. I need your order, for that order determines my thoughts, my words, my movements, and thus my life. Whatever you command, I will accept, even though I may be unable to fulfill such commands instantaneously. If your command means that I must reorganize my life in all its multifarious aspects, from the subtlest platform of my soul down to the nails of my toes, I shall endeavor to do so regardless of the cost.

It is not that I do this out of blind faith. I do this because I now have direct experience that fulfilling your desires is my deepest and most cherished desire. I am embarrassed to admit this, for it seems that such a supplication is a self-motivated action, but there is no other choice. You have so mercifully arranged that my total surrender to you is also the means for me to attain perfect satisfaction. It is such a sublime reciprocation of such unfathomable depths, that I can only thank you unlimitedly for capturing me in the network of the lotuslike stems of your mercy. It is for my own self-interest that I surrender utterly unto your desire, and yet you are so kind you make it seem as if I have done you a favor. What else can one do who has tasted the flavor of service to your lotus feet? How else could one live his life? There is nothing else in this world than your service. One of my Godbrothers told me that he did not even desire to return to the spiritual world because he was fully satisfied and, indeed, ecstatic serving you here in the material world where there were so many thousands of opportunities to serve your mission. Such realizations by your advanced disciples are not unusual, for your potency as our spiritual master is realized when we actually display that we have learned what you have taught us.

It is impossible to squeeze ecstasy out of our hearts by some external understanding of even the most exalted subject matters, for spiritual emotion cannot be forced without falling into the realm of the *sahajiyās,* who have no proper realization, yet who think that merely by desiring ecstatic symptoms they will attain them in fact. You have taught that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not an external imposition on the mind but something we will realize in truth in due course when we fully accept the mercy that is flowing from the devotional service of the Lord as it has been revealed by you. I do know that the more I surrender unto your mission, the more I am overwhelmed by your love, and I thank you for capturing me and never letting me go.

What more can I say than “Thank you,” and what more can I do than give you everything I have? I can offer you this body, although it is less than useless for all practical purposes, as well as all its possessions, which may have some use to you. But if I offer you my total energy, will you promise to protect me and care for me, who am nothing but your helpless child in your arms? Hardhearted as I am, I cannot cry for you, and thus I cannot call out to you to alert you to my precarious condition. Please understand that I will always remain your dedicated son, and if I ever try to deviate from your wish, please chastise me severely and correct me immediately.

Someday I will really please you by fully developing my eternal love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and engaging in Their loving service without interruption in Their holy abode of Śrī Māyāpur. Till that time, do not neglect me, even though I may be worthless according to all spiritual calculations. Allow me to come to the platform of giving my life to you without reservation. Lift me up, who am fallen. Save me, who am a surrendered soul. Do not neglect me. Please. I beg of you.

Your totally good-for-nothing-but-making-you-trouble servant,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

On the occasion of the divine appearance day of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, let us all offer our most respectful obeisances at his lotus feet.

We purify ourselves by glorifying Śrīla Prabhupāda. And when we induce the world to acknowledge his glories, the world will also become purified. The basis for harmonious relations among people is to give up envy and enmity toward one another. This can be done by learning to worship the bona fide *ācārya,* who comes to this world as the intimate agent of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

The world must come to understand that Śrīla Prabhupāda led a sinless life fully devoted to the loving service of the Lord; that he exhibited unprecedented compassion upon the fallen souls and gave his life so that they might have life; that he fearlessly and boldly presented the highest knowledge of God, the soul, and this world, based on the most sublime and holy scriptures, the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* that he created a wonderful worldwide mission to engage all the fallen souls in practical works of devotion, for their own salvation, and so that others might also be saved; that he implored us to work together in a spirit of selfless devotional service to the Lord, Kṛṣṇa.

Our perpetual duty is to spread the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda, for those who appreciate Śrīla Prabhupāda can easily understand the glories of the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and thus they will gain an eternal life of bliss and knowledge in the highest abode of the Lord.

Falling at his lotus feet,  
His servant,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

**Great, Greater, Greatest**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You described yourself perfectly in that prayer.  
Delivering the words of Caitanya Mahāprabhu,  
Proving the Absolute Truth is personal,  
Driving the Māyāvādīs to their caves.  
You are teaching everyone just how Kṛṣṇa is the GREATEST.  
You have perfect faith in the order of your Guru Mahārāja,  
And in the *Vedas;*  
So you are the greatest Personality of Servitor Godhead.  
You gave Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words, *Bhagavad-gītā* (As It Is),  
Your have full faith,  
You didn’t change or interpret any of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words,  
Therefore you are changing the world.  
Surprising things are happening.  
Scientists are seriously discussing the matter of consciousness.  
Of course, they are bewildered and will have to take shelter of you.  
All kinds of preachers are using your sublime examples and analogies.  
Drunkards and *mlecchas* are becoming *brāhmaṇas.*  
So many things are happening, all due to your preaching.  
Your books are being distributed more and more.  
New temples are opening everywhere.  
We must mention the grand “Glory of India,”  
Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārthasārathi’s new temple in New Delhi;  
The Prime Minister of India strongly glorified you and your ISKCON.  
He said that millions of *Bhagavad-gītās* should be distributed.  
That has always been your order.  
Crowds are rushing for *darśana*.  
Your love for Kṛṣṇa is greater than anyone else’s.  
Therefore you are GREATEST.  
Those who follow you are great, greater.  
So you are becoming greater than the greatest.  
Your books are the greatest.  
What is the need of any other?  
You should get all the Nobel, Pulitzer, and whatever prizes there are—  
Peace, Poetry, Literature, Science.  
At everything you excel.  
You should be named the man of every year.  
I am like a fly trying to follow Garuḍa.
