Only your compassion maintains me.  
On my own I can do nothing.  
Your devotees have learned compassion from you,  
And they are maintaining me.  
That was demonstrated last year when I fell very ill.  
So many devotees prayed and intervened with death.  
You have empowered so many to be merciful to the fallen souls.  
So *kīrtana* is going everywhere.  
Books are going everywhere.  
Russian Vaiṣṇavas are flooding Māyāpur at Gaura-pūrṇimā,  
And even filling up temples all over the world.  
What can I say?  
You are the Greatest.  
Some more qualified servants can tell you of the glorious progress  
of the Māyāpur *adbhuta-mandira.*  
That is most inconceivable.  
But your desires are all being fulfilled by the Supreme Lord.  
You are having the only positive influence on the world.

Aspiring for service at your feet, praying for the honor of representing you and assisting your servants,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance day!

On this most auspicious occasion, I would like to offer you a song that I wrote soon after becoming a devotee. It is still apt.

Jaya Prabhupāda!

In praise of my Spiritual Master,  
I would like to sing a song.  
He’s so great and I’m a disaster,  
but he saved me all along.  
’Cause if I hadn’t had his grace,  
I would have left the human race,  
and if he hadn’t been so kind,  
I could be born a dog or swine.

The grace of my Spiritual Master  
is what keeps me going on.  
His words and his bodily lustre  
exposed Māyā’s tricky plans,  
and if it weren’t for his grace,  
I would have lost this human face.  
Long pointed nose, hooves and a tail—  
which kind of birth, no one can tell.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!

For pleasing my Spiritual Master,  
more convinced I have to become.  
The mind is not easy to master—  
only by his love can she be overcome.  
But if I don’t secure his grace,  
I will be lost and be disgraced,  
amongst the crowds another face,  
going to hell at a breathless pace.

Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!  
Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for picking me up twenty-five years ago in Kathmandu, on a dark night of a dark life. You gave me incontrovertible proof of your being the true representative and very self of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, whose glorious power is unimpeded by any form of time, place, or circumstance. I look shelter of you then; thank you for accepting me. I want to remain eternally with you. Thank you also for giving me the opportunity to witness the wonderful exchange of love amongst the devotees this year at Māyāpur. This ISKCON movement is your very self: Śrīla Prabhupāda Dhāma.

Please allow me to remain a resident of this Dhāma and to offer eternal, however menial, service to its inhabitants.

Grasping your lotus feet, I beg to remain  
Your humble servant,  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humbly offered obeisances again and again at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your devotees who have dedicated their lives to your sacred service!

In the future, in some distant lifetime, I pray to be one of your reliable agents, assisting your soldiers in spreading the holy name. You are our (my) lord and master, a rare gem of love and devotion bringing us back home to Godhead. Every day we (I) sing of your glories, yet who can sufficiently chant your glories? Just as Brahmā told Devahūti that her son would increase her fame, your glorious sons and daughters are advertising on your behalf, increasing your fame through rare achievements of devotional service, which highlight your ever-increasing presence as a true avatar of mercy in our lives.

What is it that I can do for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? How can I serve you and your true devotees? I have fallen … I am so far behind … I simply hope and pray for the continued mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas to keep this lost soul within the dust of your lotus feet. I have no right to assign myself to Your Divine Grace’s mercy; in the past, due to offenses, I became grossly negligent. My heart begs to tell you how sorry I am; my heart begs that I will never again offend and displease you or the Vaiṣṇavas. I am your lost property, now returned. It is with both pain and relief that my heart is crying at the sight of your lotus feet. May the entire world take shelter at these lotus feet.

Whatever service is allowed becomes my life and soul, offered to you. This is how I now live, waiting for more. Once you told me that I would slit my throat for you—Oh, yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Every day now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with the sharp knife of your merciful love and instructions I again am cutting through the nonsense of worldly attachments, *kāma-krodha …* and, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you told me that you would do the same for me … actually you already had done so. Your gift was too great and I did not catch it properly at that time, so again you came to me … cutting, cutting, even in your so-called absence, saving my life by giving me yours. You said of your spiritual master, “He was no ordinary person. He saved me.” Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda … what then can be said of you? What did you need to be saved from? (Was it from going back home before saving us /me?) From the first eternal glimpse of your shining effulgent body there was never any doubt that you were anything less than extraordinary, and still you continue to capture our hearts and amaze us. Just as your wonderful devotees keep on performing amazing acts of devotion fired by the fuel of your love, you also performed incredible activities under the sublime order of your Guru Mahārāja by encircling the globe and building the foundation of an international spiritual empire meant to reign for the next ten thousand years.

You saved me, you saved all of us, and you continue to save conditioned souls who enter the shelter of your lotus feet. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda; may I also remain in the shelter of your service, assisting your sublime mission in one way or another as it pleases you.

Your immature servant and daughter,  
Mālatī-devī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Affection. That is what I remember most about our first meeting. It was the conversation, not the lecture, that remains etched in my mind.

Memories are fleeting and ethereal, like impulses on a computer disc. They are very real but hard to keep for a long time. Their substance fades rapidly with the passage of time. Early recollections of names, places, and objects become blurry and faint, shadowy and unreal. What color was the rug? Who sat in the room? What did he say? As time inevitably erodes mountains and stones, it seems to push memories into the outer darkness, relegating them ultimately to oblivion.

But feelings have more staying power. What did it *feel like* to be there?

Well, it was very focused, and focused on one person, that summer night in 1966, in the top-floor loft at 105 Bowery, in New York City. The others in the room were like the galaxy’s flickering stars, and you were the moon. I was curious, but I also felt strangely secure. It felt reassuring to hear, close up, a “great man” who seemed genuinely concerned about humanity’s plight. You spoke deep, penetrating thoughts that were hard to grasp yet somehow easy to assimilate. You were distinctly different from anyone else I had ever seen or heard.

The setting was casual, and yet a curious formality was about. You were the lecturer on a raised dais and I was a member of the audience, sitting on the barely carpeted floor. I thought, “I must approach him after the lecture—just say something, *anything*! It may be my only chance.”

It didn’t take that much courage, but some was definitely needed. After the final *kīrtana,* members of the audience began to talk among themselves and the group began to break up. I didn’t have far to walk, only a few steps. I didn’t know what I was going to say; I just knew I had to say something!

I found myself standing, above you, as it were. You were sitting, looking up at me, smiling broadly. Your smile was more than reassuring—it was accepting, inviting. Since I hadn’t really prepared anything to say, and since your demeanor at that time didn’t seem to call for philosophical inquiry, something strange happened in the time that the brain’s motor mechanism sends signals to the vocal cords, tongue, lips, and teeth. I heard myself speaking *before* I knew what I was going to say. It was truly impromptu!

I said, “I have a *tamboura*”—my first words to my spiritual master. Without missing a beat, you responded resoundingly, “Then bring it!” My mind raced. “Of course I would bring it. Then I could participate. What more could I ask? I would be actively involved in the great happening that was taking place three nights a week. I would accompany the chanting of the sacred mantra of the great man with my Indian instrument. He wasn’t just requesting me; he was *summoning* me to take part!”

After that, I couldn’t really think of anything else to say. I guess I didn’t want or need to say anything else. Now I had a firm invitation to return any Monday, Wednesday, or Friday evening with my instrument and be part of the new “band.” There was really nothing more to do or say. I would return with the *tamboura* and be part of the new, exciting “inner circle.”

You just kept looking up at me with the warmest smile with a look that reminded me of my grandfather. It is said that grandfathers are more affectionate to their grandchildren than fathers are to their children. You even *looked* like my grandfather. I felt that you accepted me for what I was, and to my mind that was momentous. It was a very strange and wonderful feeling. I muttered something about coming back with my *tamboura*—and I did, two days later. Your fond expression is what sustained me. That was the meditation that brought me back to your lotus feet for the second time a few days later. That meditation has stayed with me.

Over time, I often found the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness littered with thorns and sharp pebbles, but it is that feeling of affection pouring from you that empowers me to carry on during the turbulent and testing times that so often stand in the way of my spiritual journey.

Mukunda Goswami

## Nava Yogendra Swami

My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are very merciful upon fallen souls like myself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your appearance day. This day is most auspicious for me, for without your causeless mercy I had no hope of spiritual life. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my only hope.

As you know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am the most fallen of all of your disciples and I have no qualifications, material or spiritual. But I have an intense desire to become your true follower. This will be possible only by your causeless mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, make me your true follower; only you can do that—you have the power to do so. Please don’t ignore me by seeing me as too fallen. If you will ignore me, then I will have no hope. You are my only hope.

Please take care of your ISKCON, which is very dear to you because ISKCON is meant to save the fallen souls. You are a *mahā-bhāgavata* Vaiṣṇava. You are always concerned about the fallen souls. I have seen personally how much you were concerned for others.

Please make me a good instrument to serve your ISKCON and all the devotees of ISKCON, so that the world can be saved.

Please free me from the six passions—those of the mind, anger, the tongue, the belly, and the genitals.

Please correct the six faults—overeating or collecting more monetary funds than required, over-endeavoring for material objectives, unnecessary talking about mundane affairs, being too attached to or too neglectful of scriptural rules and regulations, associating with worldly-minded persons, and lusting after mundane achievements.

Please bestow upon this servant the six good qualities—enthusiasm, patience, confidence, the determination to perform activities favorable to devotional service, the resolution to abandon the association of nondevotees, and the discipline required to always follow in the footsteps of the predecessor *ācāryas*.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servant,  
Nava Yogendra Swami

## Param Gacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.23.57) it is said:

*etāṁ sa āsthāya parātma-niṣṭhām*  
*adhyāsitāṁ pūrvatamair maharṣibhiḥ*  
*ahaṁ tariṣyāmi duranta-pāraṁ*  
*tamo mukundāṅghri-niṣevayaiva*

**“I shall cross the insurmountable ocean of nescience by being firmly fixed in the service of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. This was approved by the previous** ***ācāryas,*** **who were fixed in firm devotion to the Lord, Paramātmā, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

My dear master, your life is naturally the very personification of truthfulness because you were always dependent on Kṛṣṇa, who is Himself the embodiment of the Absolute Truth, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By your personal example you showed that our only friend in this world is the Lord. In your heart you always meditated on Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Their mercy. In your life you never deviated from truthfulness; therefore your character was always sublime and free from material contamination.

Your mission was completely successful because of your absolute dedication to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You teach us that our preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness should be in the mood of complete surrender to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Since the Lord knows and controls everything, the result of our endeavors depend on His good will.

We cannot bring this message to the people in general and make them accept the science of *bhakti* unless we are ourselves properly acquainted with it and successfully immersed in it, and therefore free from the tendency to cheat others.

In your practical life, you showed how it is crucial to be free from duplicity and the tendency to cheat others and to be completely fixed in truthfulness. Therefore your sublime character and your behavior are to be glorified by the whole human society and all the demigods.

I humbly pray to you to give me a drop of determination and mercy, by which I can always be fixed at your lotus feet and never deviate from your instructions.

Your humble servant,  
Param Gacit Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace! In Kali-yuga, life in this material world is like a ferocious whirlwind. People all over the world are rushing hither and thither in all sorts of motor vehicles, trains, and planes. They are communicating at high speed by telephone, fax, or Internet. And their minds are rushing all over the place at even higher speed. They are constantly searching for newer and newer ways to gratify their senses, and, with the help of modern technology, they keep coming up with more and more alluring alternatives. Although all of these are just variations on the same old theme of material sense gratification, nevertheless, by Māyā’s potency, the conditioned souls are becoming ever-increasingly captivated.

In the Sixth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* Arjuna says that it is more difficult to subdue the mind than to control the wind. That which was so difficult for such an exalted devotee as Arjuna, who lived in such a pious age and associated so closely with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is definitely much more difficult for us fallen souls, who are drowning in the material whirlpool in this impious Age of Kali. There is practically no hope for such persons to become rectified and freed from Māyā’s influence, so as to go back home, back to Godhead.

Nevertheless, despite the anomalies of this age and the disqualifications of people in general, Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared with his brother Balarāma in the forms of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, like the sun and the moon arising simultaneously. They brought with them the powerful *saṅkīrtana* process, by which all the degraded persons of Kali-yuga can be reformed. Furthermore, they have sent you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as their empowered emissary, to bring their message to conditioned souls in every nook and cranny of this planet. This you have successfully accomplished with the utmost humility, precision, and potency. For you, old age was not a deterrent, neither was lack of material facilities or unfamiliarity with the various circumstances in different countries. In any case, due to your firm faith in him, Śrī Kṛṣṇa provided you with everything required to successfully accomplish the task. You transcended all material barriers, for you were directly linked with the Supreme Godhead through the *paramparā* system, and thus you acted fully on the spiritual platform. This enabled you to attract followers like a powerful magnet, pulling them right out of material bewilderment and situating them firmly in the pure activities of devotional service. In addition, you created a huge spiritual society, with headquarters in Śrīdhām Māyāpur and branches all over the world, in order to give shelter to all these newborn spiritual babes, so they might grow up strong and healthy in the proper devotional atmosphere. Not only that, but by writing your transcendental books and inspiring your followers to print and distribute them in all languages of the world, you created a powerful mechanism by which the process of reclaiming conditioned souls would go on vigorously far, far into the future.

Thus, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the real “Patita Pāvana”—savior of the fallen. As I am one of those most fallen, I humbly pray on this most auspicious day that you kindly keep a small place for me under the shade of your lotus feet, and that, despite my innumerable disqualifications and offenses, you somehow or other deliver me from the vast ocean of illusion and ignorance. Please engage me in your devotional service and help me do something useful for the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

I beg to remain,  
Your insignificant servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Pṛthu dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In 1936 … Today is ninth December, 1938(68). That means thirty-two years ago. In Bombay, I was then doing some business. All of a sudden, perhaps on this date, sometimes between 9 or 10 December. At that time, Guru Mahārāja was indisposed little and he was staying at Jagannātha Purī, on the seashore. So I wrote him letter, “My dear master, your other disciples, *brahmacārī, sannyāsī,* they are rendering you direct service. And I am a householder. I cannot live with you, I cannot serve you nicely. So I do not know. How can I serve you?” Simply an idea, I was thinking of serving him, “How can I serve him seriously?” So the reply was dated 13th December, 1936. In that letter he wrote, “My dear such and such, I am very glad to receive your letter. I think you should try to push our movement in English.” That was his writing. “And that will do good to you and to the people who will help you.” That was his instruction. And then in 1936, on the 31st December—that means just after writing this letter a fortnight before his departure—he passed away … But I took that order of my spiritual master very seriously.” \[Śrīla Prabhupāda Lecture, Los Angeles, September 12, 1969\]

In these few words we are actually witnessing the birth of ISKCON. This is the nucleus, the actual seed of our movement. And the very reason why we are all here today.

At this time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed your true glory—*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau.* And from there—*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana—*everything became a success!

You were just like Rāmānanda Rāya: “Śrī Caitanya embraced him and said, “Because you have understood the purpose of My incarnation, you are privileged to have seen this particular feature of My personality.” \[*Teachings of Lord Caitanya,* Chapter 32—“Conclusion”\]

You were just like Haridāsa Ṭhākura: “Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was astonished. ‘These are actually My confidential pastimes.’ How could Haridāsa have understood them?” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā* 3.90\]

You were just like Rūpa Gosvāmī: “Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, ‘No one knows the purport of My verse. How could you understand My intention?’” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 1.70\]

In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you understood the intentions of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, perfectly. These intentions were only reflecting the mind of Lord Caitanya and all the *ācāryas:*

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“Not to sit down in a secluded place, and to get cheap popularity as a petty meditator. Even if he’s meditator, but that is for himself. But Caitanya Mahāprabhu says that, ‘Go, everyone, in every village, in every town, and preach this gospel,** ***pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma / sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma.*** **\[*****CB Antya-khaṇḍa*** **4.126\] This is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s** ***mano-’bhīṣṭam,*** **that is His desire.” \[Lecture, Bombay, January 8, 1973\]**

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

Simply preaching the mission of Lord Caitanya as taught by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja, your life and mission!

We pray today, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on the auspicious day of your appearance, that we, your disciples, will understand your mind as well. Then, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are indeed very fortunate. And we will become successful as well.

*Namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe.* This is the activity of your spiritual master: that he is preaching the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. And he’s a disciple of Sarasvatī Ṭhākura … *Sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*. These are the activities … He’s simply preaching the message of Lord Caitanya. …that you go and speak \[to\] everyone about Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s message. This is called *paramparā* system. \[Lecture, London, August 10, 1971\]

“Why this Gauḍīya Maṭha failed? Because they tried to become more than Guru Mahārāja.” \[Bombay, August 16, 1976\]

Pṛthu dāsa adhikārī

## Rāmai Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda has written,

If there is no chance of directly serving the spiritual master, then the devotee does serve him by remembering his instructions only. There is no difference between the spiritual master's instructions and the spiritual master himself. In his absence, therefore, his words of direction shall be the guide of the disciple.…\[A person without a spiritual master\] is compared with a ship without a rudder as such a careless ship cannot reach her destination. \[*Back to Godhead,* March 20, 1960\]

Every year I get more realization of how Śrīla Prabhupāda is so much the mercy manifestation of the supremely merciful incarnation of Kali-yuga, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If Lord Caitanya had not been compassionate upon the poor conditioned souls rotting in this material world, what chance would the living entities have of being freed from the clutches of misery and suffering, and how could they go back home, back to Godhead? Similarly, if it were not for Śrīla Prabhupāda, all the people, especially those living outside Bhāratavarṣa, would also remain rotting in illusion, never tasting the bliss of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the life of a devotee. We are all so deeply indebted to Śrīla Prabhupāda and the movement he created for saving the world, his ISKCON.

One time we were showing Prabhupāda some slides of Lord Nṛsiṁha temples and other holy places we had visited in South India. Prabhupāda was interested to see them and even said he had also visited a few of the ones in the pictures. However, when our slides showed various ISKCON centers around the world, Prabhupāda, who had previously been lying back casually on his couch remarking only occasionally, sat up erect with noticeably increased enthusiasm. He became animated and started to dominate the conversation. “Which temple is that? The Deities are very beautiful there. Very nice kitchen they have made, how much *prasādam* they are distributing? The devotees look very happy and effulgent, I can understand they are following strictly all our principles.” When I saw Prabhupāda speak and act in that way, I immediately appreciated what was closest to his heart, what was very dear to him. He had worked tirelessly for the spiritual and material welfare of others. Especially, he had left India on the order of his spiritual master. He started his ISKCON as the vehicle by which the whole world could get salvation from the miseries of material existence and attain love of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa-*prema*. He was concerned that the temples, devotees, and programs go on very nicely for those who were participating, and for others who came in contact with them.

As we showed Prabhupāda more slides, I could see his interest in every aspect of ISKCON affairs. On one occasion when Prabhupāda visited our temple, he ran his finger along the top of a window ledge. When he finished, his finger was dirty from the accumulated dust. He then said, “Where is the temple president”? The temple president was fetched and Prabhupāda admonished and lectured him for fifteen minutes about keeping the temple clean and tidy. Another time a university professor was invited to speak with Prabhupāda. At first he was cordial, but as the discussion continued he started objecting and then finally criticized Prabhupāda. Prabhupāda didn’t say anything, but the professor became more challenging and more critical up to the point of insulting. Just then, one of Prabhupāda’s disciples interjected and asked the professor why he had come such a long way to be so offensive to Śrīla Prabhupāda. The professor retorted not to the disciple, but directly to Prabhupāda, saying that it was “fanatics like him who give your movement a bad name.” Prabhupāda, who had previously tolerated the professor’s insults, upon hearing the undue criticism of his disciple, replied back sharply, “He is fanatic, or you are fanatic”? The tense mood continued for some time, but amazingly by the end of the conversation Prabhupāda and the professor became friends and he went away with a favorable impression, not only of Prabhupāda but of ISKCON as well.

Prabhupāda was always loving and affectionate to the devotees. Once when he saw a skinny *brahmacārī* he asked him from the *vyāsāsana*, “Aren’t they feeding you properly here?” At a *pāndal* program in Hyderabad a women asked Prabhupāda if he did any miracles, ad Prabhupāda turned and pointed to his disciples, “They are miracle. Before they were living sinful life, now they are saintly.”

In the same way, though, he sometimes lovingly chastised them, such as the time in Perth when his disciples were sleeping for hours after a heavy lunch, he said, “The *karmīs* are all working so hard for their sense gratification, but you are all lying idle for Kṛṣṇa.”

Prabhupāda worked very hard, rising early in the morning, at one or two a.m., dictating wonderful translations and purports to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* etc. He gave his disciples so much time and instruction and showed by example. What a wonderful spiritual master! Always giving and giving! He could have very easily stayed in India preaching, as he already had done from the 1940’s, publishing and distributing *Back to Godhead,* writing books and essays, and contacting important people in high places. He had a good measure of success and appreciation from people in all walks of life, from scholars to laymen. However, having the transcendentally soft heart of a *mahā-bhāgavata* pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, and always desiring to follow the instruction of his guru, he came and saved us.

Prabhupāda himself has written,

A pure devotee is just like the Desire-Tree from whom we can have on request everything desirable. This is no myth or bluff but it is actual fact. The devotee can deliver to us the mercy of God and what is not obtainable by one who has got the mercy of God? Therefore the devotees are the most magnanimous in their charitable disposition of mind. They are also the deliverers of the fallen souls and we need to offer our obeisances unto their lotus feet so that we can invoke their blessings in our all attempts. \[Essay: “Devotee and the Divinity”, pre-1965\]

All I can say, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that because I am so fallen, I always feel the need to glorify and offer obeisances unto you. Just as without the most attentive service of the parent no child can grow up to adulthood, similarly without the care of the spiritual master no one can rise up to the plane of transcendental loving service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa has empowered you to deliver lowly people like me, and by nature you are not only kind but also liberal and munificent. Please hear my prayer.

Your insignificant fool disciple,  
Rāmai Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet. All glories to you, for you are the most compassionate servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, and you answered all my prayers.

It was around 1967 that I found myself praying to a God I did not believe in to give me the faith to believe in Him.

Sometime in 1968, as part of a graduate course in Hinduism, I read (for the third time) the *Bhagavad-gītā* (in yet another translation). This time, I arrived, after a great effort, at a stunning conclusion: that the *Bhagavad-gītā* was ultimately teaching surrender to Kṛṣṇa. Since the *Bhagavad-gītā* had always come to me packaged by impersonalists, I thought this was a great breakthrough, my original find.

I sat at the dining room table, the *Bhagavad-gītā* open before me. “Surrender!” I commanded myself. “Surrender! Surrender!” I squeezed my eyes shut with effort. My forehead nearly hit the table. “Surrender! Surrender!”

I opened my eyes. Nothing. I tried again. And again.

Still, I carefully set my stunning insight at the conclusion of my assigned paper on “The Ethics of the *Bhagavad-gītā*.” It descended in the final pages like a deus ex machina. My professor, not at all impressed, dropped my grade a point.

A little later the same year I became convinced that I needed a spiritual guide, a master, a teacher. I figured I would have to find a way to India.

All around me everywhere there bubbled a witch’s brew of unholy loves. I knew if I stayed I was doomed.

Then, on a sullen and damp winter day in 1969, I found myself negotiating the rotting front steps of a dreary row house in West Philadelphia to a door that opened up into eternal beauty and splendor. The fragrance of sandalwood and camphor blended with the sting of a pungent chaunce, and a climaxing chorus of “Hare Kṛṣṇa” ushered me into Vaikuṇṭha localized. In one step I was translated from the material world into the spiritual.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, brought that door here, to me. You lead my footsteps to it, you opened it with your hand, and, then, whenever I thought to wander outside, you held me in, sometimes roughly.

What would I have done had you not come? Had you not cared? Long before I understood what I was doing, I had called for Kṛṣṇa’s representatives, begged for Kṛṣṇa’s envoys, prayed for the agents of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. And I was only one out of many anguished souls, lost in the land of the golden arches, crying and crying for you to come.

Certainly there were many who could have come for us. But you alone came. Although you had every right to take your ease in Kṛṣṇa’s own *dhāma,* you answered to a higher calling yet. In fulfillment of that, you were made to struggle endlessly and with no reasonable expectations. You were made to arrive on our shores completely divested of all assets: you commanded no funds, manpower, organization, or property. Youth and health at least? No, not that either.

What you possessed was only this: you understood, you caught up, like no one else, the deepest, most confidential intentions of your Guru Mahārāja, and empowered with that alone, you came. You heard our cry in the wastelands, you came for us, and we were saved.

You were still hearing such cries in your final days. Shockingly emaciated and dropsy-swollen at the same time, you once again left Vṛndāvana to the West, for there were millions still to deliver, and you had plans how to do it. You had just begun. We beheld with breathtaking clarity how unlimited your desire was to deliver “suffering humanity.” This desire is your greatest legacy.

It is only superficial to say that you had to turn back from this final journey. In reality, you kept on going, and you are still going on and on, ever moving, always preaching. You live on. As we continue your mission, so you yourself live and preach, satisfying your greed to save the fallen, and therefore I pledge myself, in unending gratitude, to your undying life of preaching. I beg only for your mercy so that I may myself keep my intelligence fixed upon your most confidential instructions and that I may strive with all my energies to fulfill your most confidential desires.

Please grant this benediction to your most unworthy servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Rohinī Suta dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

On this auspicious day of your appearance  
I try to remember some of your instructions,  
but they are like an extensive ocean, deep and vast.  
I feel like an insignificant  
insect at its shore.

There was once a time  
when you were present with us.  
It was a wonderful time,  
because you were the center  
and by your words and your example  
the mission was clear.  
Everyone worked together for one goal—to please you.

“If you really want to please me, then preach and distribute books!”  
“These books are the law books for the next ten thousand years.”  
“As soon as I see that there is such increase in book distribution figures,  
I take it that all other programs are successful as well.”  
“Therefore please distribute books, distribute books, distribute books!”  
“Do just as I have shown you.”  
“If you love me then please cooperate and keep this institution together.”

You are the ISKCON founder-*ācārya,*  
no-one can replace you,  
your instructions and books are wonderful,  
so simple and sublime—  
without them the whole world has no meaning.

In this dark Age of Kali everyone has lost orientation,  
but the sunshine of your books spreads the light far and wide.  
Mahāprabhu is calling, “In every town and village!”

“My ambition is to spread these books far and wide all over the world.”  
“If 1% of the population becomes devotees, that will change the direction of the world.”  
“Whatever progress we have made, it is simply due to distributing these books.”  
“So go on, and do not divert your mind for a moment.”

All glories to the incomparable mercy of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!  
If we remember this wonderful mercy and your wonderful deeds and gift,  
we feel great inspiration within the core of our hearts.  
Everyone jubilates in great happiness like the birds when the sun rises.

Even if sometimes the darkness comes and Kali’s agents try to attack,  
what do we care ?  
We have you, your instructions, your books, and your devotees.  
The mission is clear, though it’s not always easy.  
“First they laugh about us, then they will try to destroy it,  
but then they will accept.”  
“Just keep going—*avyāvṛtta-bhajanāt*.”  
“It takes some time, but if we stick to our principles  
and we do not compromise and push on this way, it will go on.”  
“It will never stop. At least 10,000 years it will go on.”

Single we are weak, but together we are strong.  
The king teaches his five sons:  
“Here, everyone of you, take one arrow and try to break it.”  
It was easy. Crack! Crack! Crack! Crack! Crack!  
“But now try to break all five together”—but nobody succeeded.

This is our strength—Paraṁ Vijayate Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtanam*!  
In this way the good old time will become eternally a good time,  
because we are sure that you are present.  
“I will never die. I will continue to live in my books.”  
“Distribution of all these books is making a firm basis for our movement.”

This movement is based on your books.  
Preaching is the essence,  
utility the principle  
and purity the force.  
You gave us these four statements as the guideline.

“Distributing books will make devotees.  
To make a devotee means to get someone purified.  
Reading the books will purify the intelligence.  
A purified heart and mind means Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

Please give me your blessing  
that I can absorb my mind in you  
and your instructions and always have  
the association of devotees  
and the strength to go on in your mission.

Your insignificant servant,  
Rohinī Suta dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most respectful obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, my most beloved savior and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Meditation upon Your Divine Grace in *any* manner is totally sublime—the deeper, the better! So much apart from all that is imperfect and misery-producing in this world, you shine forth with an emblazoning brilliance of exceptional purity, compassion, wisdom, joy, dedication to the topmost Truth, boundless faith, unparalleled strength of character and conviction, renunciation, courage, humility…in fact, those who know you and serve you and love you know there is *no limit* to your supremely exalted qualities. Yet radiating in brilliance above all other qualities is your boundless and faultless love for guru and Kṛṣṇa. This is the quality in you I would like to especially honor today.

“Even though a man may not like it,” Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī declares, “reputation, as ordained by providence, comes to him. Indeed, one’s transcendental reputation is known throughout the entire world.”

Like a covered *avatāra*, your teachings and your enthusiasm to selflessly dedicate your whole energy to the mission of your spiritual master were equal prior to ISKCON’s formation and afterwards. However, the shaping of that manifested mission via ISKCON awaited a higher plan, which Kṛṣṇa was to startlingly reveal to the entire world, in His own time frame.

Your early writings are replete with reveilles proclaiming to an attentive audience the vibrational form of the revolutionary preaching mission that you would soon unfold. In 1961 you wrote a powerful set of prayers to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura on the occasion of his disappearance day, which I have been relishing practically daily and deriving great inspiration from.

In honor of your Vyāsa-pūjā observance of 1998, I would like to churn the topics of these prayers, written by you in 1961, to help us renew our inspiration and sight *how* we are to effectively carry forward your ISKCON society into the new millennium.

Although these prayers are nearly forty years old, they are perfectly appropriate forever. Timeless are your utterances.

Renunciation in devotion and cooperation within Mahāprabhu’s grand preaching mission are bold messages for your audience to heed. But no ordinary fellow can accomplish these things, you declare, without the nourishing flow of “real spiritual emotion” entering into their heart. And where does *that* commodity come from?? From “service to the message”: propagate the instructions of the guru while keeping oneself free from all pride—this is the formula you proclaim. “Come back to this” is your call. “From the seas, across the earth, penetrate the universal shell; come together and preach this Kṛṣṇa consciousness!!”

This is your message of 1961, and it is the same as that of your Guru Mahārāja. You call all Vaiṣṇavas to come forward and, under the banner of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and with a united, cooperative effort, to make a practical plan for preaching boldly to awaken pure devotional service within everyone; in this way “Kali will flee, screaming, of his own accord.”

Your words are compelling. We must rescue the conditioned souls, “the battered souls” of this world. Your order is there, and the blueprint for such a pure Vaiṣṇava society within this dark material world is also there in your plentiful instructions. We, your followers, need to make that blueprint known and manifest.

Soon we will we move into the new millennium, carrying with us the mighty task of perpetuating the mission you began—preserving it, expanding it, and bringing your instructions to life. I am convinced that the flow of “real spiritual emotion” that you referred to in the first set of your 1961 prayers will make *all* of this possible—the bold preaching, the renunciation in devotees, the spirit of cooperation. All this will be abundantly manifest by fixing ourselves in full faith upon fulfilling your preaching vision. Everything must be done with a practical plan, with great care and skill, devoid of all pride and independent spirit. Your order is our life’s mission and greatest joy.

Mistakes, neglect of your orders, inattention, personal motivation, pride—none of these have a place in a life of pure devotion. By your mercy, may we each dedicate ourselves, one by one, to faithfully executing your preaching mission with pure hearts, in a united preaching spirit.

Your humble servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Śeṣa dāsa Adhikārī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my humble obeisances.

In his “Bhajana-Lālasa” Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sings:

O Gurudeva! By administering a drop of your mercy make this servant of yours infinitely more humble than a blade of grass. Giving me the strength to bear all trials and troubles, free me from desires for personal honor.

O lord and master! Inspire me with the power to befittingly honor all living beings. Only then will I sing the holy name of the Lord in great ecstasy, and my offensive activities will cease.

When will this person be indeed blessed by receiving your mercy, O lord and master? Devoid of all strength and intelligence, I am very low and fallen. Please make me your own.

When I examine myself for worthiness, I find nothing of value. Therefore your mercy is the essence of life. If you are not merciful to me, then I will constantly weep and weep, no longer being able to maintain my life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this mood I pray:

Please grant me the intelligence to understand my duty, and the courage to perform my service without hesitation.

Your servant,  
Śeṣa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

**Ārati to His Divine Grace**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O my unlimitedly affectionate spiritual master, whose heart always weeps for the plight of the conditioned souls and who are very compassionate to your disciples, please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Today is the victorious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā. Transcendental poets like Vyāsa and Jayadeva compose beautiful *stavas* in your glorification that are like the glistening earrings of Lord Viṣṇu. What can a crowlike fool—that is, this worthless self—say in the presence of your true followers and admirers? All glories unto you! With great difficulty I have attempted to scour the recesses of my dirty heart to compose a few words in your exaltation. May they bring you just a little happiness.

Some time after your disappearance, unhappy with the limited memories of your personal association and suffering from the pangs of separation, I constructed a temple within my heart and established you there as the presiding deity.

In that temple you reside always (when you are not traveling and preaching), writing your books, walking in the courtyard, enjoying my cooking, allowing me to visit you at will, taking *darśana* of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and Gaura-Nitāi. When I enter in the morning and place my head at your lotus feet, you do not object but kindly rub my back. In times of distress you give me solace, when I resist your will you chastise me. Always you remain my protector, guide, father, and eternal friend.

Today I perform the *ārati* ceremony of Your Divine Grace, absorbed in the meditation of your *arcana,* my heart seeking to glorify you through the successive offering of objects of worship. Having left your quarters, you now sit majestically in pure silk robes, decorated with many garlands of small white jasmine flowers, a gong in your hand, a benign smile on your lips. The temple courtyard is thronging with Vaiṣṇavas, who have been invited from the very corners of the world. The air is laden with billowing smoke and blossoming flowers as the many birds, who are like visiting demigods, begin to sing your glories.

Initiating the *ācamana* of your remembrance, standing on the mat of self-surrender to your will, I take my place by your *vyāsāsana,* carved of the finest lapis lazuli and inlaid with precious gems of every description. The *ārati* paraphernalia is made of gold, ornately carved with many divine flowers, set atop a golden table finely gilded with silver and pearls. The ocean of pure devotees who stand before you swells with the tide of loving attachment as they begin to sing your praises: *śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma…* begins the heavenly chorus.

Was this snow-white conchshell I now blow recovered from the milk ocean by the demigods when they prayed to Lord Viṣṇu for your appearance? Its formidable sound reverberates throughout the world, dissipating ignorance in the hearts of all and declaring your unparalleled glory. It is you who had come to save the conditioned souls in their darkest hour, it is you who remain their savior through your infallible teachings, and it is you who will continue to save all manner of created beings through your true followers. Attention everyone, the worship of Śrī Guru is about to take place!

Offering the items of worship one by one, I perceive the elements of creation being purified as they are engaged in your service, the hearts of the gross materialists, represented by this worthless *pūjārī,* becoming inclined toward the worship of the Divine. Generally he who is worshiped is freed of all obstacles and enjoys all auspiciousness. Now I see it is you who purify all this paraphernalia, their source of origin, its presiding deities, the *pūjārī,* and all related things and beings.

A divinely aromatic incense represents the fragrance of the earth, and earth itself. It is this very planet that you blessed by walking everywhere with your lotus feet, the dust of which Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva covet as their dearest ornament. The very scent of distress goes to a distant place when you are present, for the fire of devotion burning in your heart easily purifies all ignorance and its concomitant reactions. Thus all foul odors become purified and sacred as I place this incense in the sapphire container at your lotus feet.

The five-wicked lamp is from the reception hall of King Indra, where the universal leaders are regularly honored. As I slowly offer these fires to your transcendental form, the inferno raging in the hearts of the conditioned souls becomes purified and transformed into attachment to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. The five life airs in the body, contaminated by the intrigues of Kali, gradually become refined by the fire of your preaching ordeal, and the hearts of all men become peaceful. The fire of sacrifice burns once again, the cooking fires in the homes prepare offerings to the Lord, and the wrath of Lord Ananta ready to incinerate the atheists is satiated as He sways peacefully to your *kīrtana* of the holy names.

Decorated with *gopīcandana,* a pure-hearted devotee brings a crystal tray. Taking the *prasādam* of that lamp, all those present become overjoyed, their hearts warmed by your inconceivable magnanimity.

Water from the oceans and one thousand sacred rivers bathed Lord Rāma at His coronation ceremony. Those same sacred waters combine in the conch as the next offering to your divine being. Having bathed in the many rivers of the world, you have satisfied their presiding deities, who had been abused by the recklessness of the industrial revolution. You taught that water was for bathing, drinking, purification, agriculture, and making paper pulp. Even today, the origin of all rivers, Gaṅgā-devī, goes out of her way to take your *samādhi darśana,* remembering how you brought her to the homes of the nonbelievers by spreading the glories of her waters in the lands of this world.

Like a shower from the trunk of Airāvata, that water falls on the heads of the fortunate congregation, who dance and smile in great happiness.

A delicate embroidered silk brocade handkerchief that is the grace of the goddess of learning is the solace to wipe away the tears of all suffering humanity. Troubled by the never-ending struggle for happiness, the minds of conditioned souls become peaceful once again as they find solace in the unlimited services to the Lord that you freely offer. You revolutionized men’s and ladies fashions, introducing dhotis and saris as a spiritual vogue.

These flowers appear to be from the gardens of Vaikuṇṭha, brought by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself in appreciation for your pure selfless service. The roses are without thorns, as your pure devotion to Him has no contact with *karma* or *jñāna,* being complete and perfect in itself. Of what value were flowers as an ornament and decoration before you had taught to offer garlands to the Deity? Who was there worthy of recognition and respect until you taught the sanctity of representing the disciplic succession? Now flowers bloom happily, their existence having true value, smiling through their petals, ever so pleased at being more than ingredients to incite lust or symbols to signal death.

The vegetation of this world is also indebted for having achieved the fortune of become offerings to the Lord. The fields of wheat sway in happiness that they will become chapatis for the r*āja-bhoga*, cows graze peacefully in the pastures knowing that the Lord will accept their milk products, and trees cry in ecstasy to know that their fruits are relished as *mahā-prasādam* by the servants of the Lord. Thus the clouds find reason to pour their rain on agricultural fields, for your *seva* revives the very process of sacrifice and is the source of all natural prosperity.

I lift the pure white *cāmara* time and again, remembering how its touch is never displeasing to you. However, it is your transcendental touch that is the purifier of the worlds and its innumerable victims. Shaking hands with state officials and intelligentsia, you imbued spiritual energy into their hearts; touching small children, you became the source of life-long inspiration; your caressing of cows initiated their protection; and your holding of newly published books began a worldwide literary revolution. With an aristocratic gesture you redirected culinary science, and a kindly pat on the head gave life to your followers. Whatever has touched you—clothing, cars, canes, shoes, newspapers, and the like—have achieved perfection and know unlimited benefit unavailable in the planets of the *devas*.

The peacock fan comes last, offering a pleasant breeze in the warm mid-day air. Whoever has escaped your mercy will now be purified by the all-pervasive air that has touched your form, felt your breath, and been moved by your words. Is there any volume of space that does not possess an atom of air once purified by you as you spoke words of pure knowledge and selfless glorification of your beloved Śrī Kṛṣṇa? How will the reluctant atheists escape your association, since Vāyu himself personally delivers your *mahā-prasādam* to every corner of the globe. No use trying to fathom the depth of your service! If I remain in awe, familiarity will stay at a distance, and if I admire your greatness, devotion will increase. What great happiness is there today!

The conchshell resounds once again, renting the very extremities of space, and then there is silence. The offering of flowers, garlands, and words of love by the assembly of devotees (followed by a great feast in your honor) will merge into the external reality of Vyāsa-pūjā 1998, and the subjective and objective realms become one, until once again I turn within, to enter the temple of my heart, where you have kindly accepted residence.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this concludes my offering of words in the *ārati* of your *pūjā*. May these words not simply be a combination of letters but the deities of substantial attachment to your will, manifest by the elemental controllers of this cosmic manifestation. As with everything, that will be possible only by your grace.

One day I came to you asking, “How do I know what course of action to take at times of great difficulty?” You replied “Just ask me!” Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me the humility to turn to you always and the inspiration to hear your divine answer. In that way my offering on this day will continue to reverberate through the elements, proclaiming your glory, never to fade away.

Your very insignificant servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

The GBC meetings held in Māyāpur in 1998 will most probably be remembered as the assessment year. The GBC men were individually assessed for their performance of their duties.

As you are the supreme Governing Body Commissioner, I thought this would be a good year to assess your performance.

It is my humble assessment that you are the greatest spiritual master and *ācārya* and therefore the greatest man (*jīva* soul) of this century and beyond.

What are the criteria of my assessment?

Lord Kṛṣṇa expected a lot from his pure devotees. On studying His teachings we find that there are four main functions that He wanted His pure devotees to carry out.

He wanted them to be *śikṣā-gurus* for all the members of the universe. He wanted them to provide leadership to push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya to every town and village, and he wanted them to manage the movement in a responsible way. He also wanted them to act as watchdogs, seeing that the proper standards of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are maintained by all their disciples, and in all the temples that they open, that assets are protected, and that philosophical deviation is not allowed to creep in.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the primary *śikṣā-guru* for millions and millions of devotees, both now and in the future, for at least the next 10,000 years. How did you establish these relationships?

First of all, right from your birth in a pious and well-to-do Bengali family, you were a perfect example of *ācāra* (practice) and *pracāra* (preaching). Throughout your life, whatever you taught you also practiced perfectly. Therefore everyone was and is ready to accept your authority.

*Yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas*—*Bhagavad-gītā*.

Secondly, you devised and executed many strategies to disseminate Lord Kṛṣṇa’s message. You were constantly giving enlivening morning and evening scripture classes in your temples; you regularly lectured at public programs and in the houses of life members and sincere well-wishers. You also expertly gave training to your disciples by personally training some and sending them out to train others.

Your most significant contribution in giving *śikṣā* was by your translation and publication of Vedic scriptures into the English language. This was a brilliant stratagem that will destroy the forces of *māyā*. People receive your books, become enlightened by reading them and keep them and share them with others for years together.

Your books are the most authoritative body of literatures presenting state-of-the-art spiritual technology in a very user-friendly, readable format. Those who read them regularly are inspired to directly or indirectly distribute them. This kind of writing, which convinces people to the point of their taking some action, is very special.

The second category of my assessment is leadership.

Naveen Krishna dāsa, Madhu Paṇḍita dāsa, and I once took a professional leadership course that has been taken by tens of thousands of executives and managers of the top corporations in the world. We gave our teacher a copy of your biography, *Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta,* and asked for an opinion. This professional teacher of leadership was so impressed that she underlined all the wonderful examples of your numerous leadership qualities and remarked that she had never read of such a wonderful person before.

A leader is a person who always keeps everyone focused on the ultimate destination. A leader shows the way by going in advance.

You taught us everything. How to work, how to eat, how to sleep, how to worship, how to manage, and how to lead. We were completely inexperienced, so you became our father, our friend, our master, our teacher, and our guru. You kept us steady on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You criticized us. You lovingly screamed at us. You corrected us. And you took great pain to keep us on the straight and narrow path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*. You are the greatest spiritual leader.

Thirdly, you managed ISKCON better than any global entrepreneur. In any business the three most crucial assets or resources are physical, financial, and human.

In the beginning you did everything, just to teach the standards. You were the most frugal with physical assets, even up to the point of writing replies to letters on their torn-apart envelopes just to save the cost of a sheet of paper. You established the first official ISKCON society with Brahmānanda as the president, Ṛṣi Kumar as the secretary, and yourself as the treasurer. The next day, when Ṛṣi Kumar came to you (the treasurer) to get some money, you replied, “You should not ask your spiritual master for money.” And later on you spent millions of dollars to build temples and print books.

You encouraged spiritual businesses, such as Spiritual Sky Incense, to increase funds. You had no qualms about a *sannyāsī* doing the needful to generate financial assets to push on the preaching. Then you introduced the best business—selling of your books.

But it was your masterful management of your disciples’ lives, the human assets of your mission, that inspires me the most. As I look back over those years of rapid growth and the innumerable personal problems of your young students, I see how you simultaneously pushed us forward in the service of your mission and yet extended so much love and compassion that we hardly noticed our austerities.

Lastly, I don’t see you as an ordinary watchdog who watches over something separate from himself. I see that you are your mission, ISKCON. ISKCON is your body. ISKCON is the most successful manifestation of the practical achievement of *nagarādi grāma,* “in every town and village of the world.” You are supremely conscious of and sensitive to any deviation that might harm the development of Lord Caitanya’s movement in ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your arguments are the most convincing because you embody both Bhakti and Vedanta. Over and over again you clarified, in plain and simple language, issues that others might shy away from, not wanting to risk any chagrin from critics. You were a bold preacher who engaged in controversy when it was appropriate. Jīva Gosvāmī and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī are also famous controversialists in our line.

I must admit that I was fearful when I first dared to assess you. I am known as impudent and imprudent.

I am most happy to report that it has been extremely pleasurable because this exercise has greatly increased my realization that there is no one else in your universe. There is no scale to compare you with anyone else. As far as I can see, you are the perfect master to lead all of us (and I sincerely mean all of us) out of darkness. I can only pray at your divine lotus feet for your special mercy on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

Please continue as our Supreme Governing Body Commissioner.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me many opportunities, such as regular appraisal or assessment by my peers, to see my faults, so that someday, by your mercy, you will appraise me and allow me to follow in your footsteps.

Your humble servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On this 1998 Vyāsa-pūjā, I submit myself as a servant who has no other shelter than your lotus feet. I pray for your continued protection in my small attempts to somehow assist you in your mission. You are my only hope.

Thank you.

Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

**A Beggar at Prabhupāda’s Door**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Today, I stand before you, a wayward son, pitiably reduced, bowed and bereft—more a beggar than when we first met thirty years ago. At that time, Haight-Ashbury was a blazing crematorium filled with ghoulish, ghostly characters, I among them. The entire city of San Francisco was spooked by our outrageous behavior. No one had any idea what to do with us—not the mayor, nor the governor, nor the police. Had you not arrived, though uninvited by them, the entire city would have been desecrated. Yet none of those municipal authorities could fathom your glories. Instead, seeing you dance and sing and associate with the half-crazed, they mistook you for one of us, not recognizing who you were, where you had come from, and what your mission was.

Lord Śiva, too, is grossly misunderstood due to his unusual behavior and ghostly entourage. You, like Lord Śiva, are the best of the devotees, the deliverer of the most fallen. As Lord Śiva rides upon his white bull Nandi, you are carried on the back of Dharma. Śiva means “auspicious”: he holds the Ganges and the moon on his head, a *tṛśūla* and *ḍamaru* drum in his hands. Similarly, you are the brilliant moonlike disciple of the star-studded *sarasvat* firmament. You are as auspicious as Lord Śiva, for the same Ganges only agreed to come to earth if she could be purified by personalities like you. The whole world dances to your *bṛhad mṛdaṅga* rhythms, urged on by the three prongs of Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam,* His holy names, and your books.

You are the destroyer of all that is inauspicious. Like Lord Śiva, without waiting to be asked you proceeded to drink the poisonous ocean of that city’s sins. Āśutoṣa is another of Śiva’s names, but are you not equally magnanimous? We pleased you simply by our wild dancing, encouraged by your nods and smiles. With no effort on our part, you freed us from ghostly existence by giving us birth as your own sons and daughters. Then you nourished and raised us to one day inherit your divine mission—Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

So now I stand here, remembering my former life, knowing what I was and what you wanted me to become. I feel so inadequate, completely unqualified. I feel naked, revealed by my failings, ever reminded of my past, which is much closer than I wish. Thirty years of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has changed many things, but at least one thing remains the same: I am still the shameless beggar I was the day we first met. Therefore, on the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, when I should offer rather than take, I stand before you empty-handed, with only this pauper’s appeal to make: Independence has been my undoing; free will has been only misused. I consign these, now, to you. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not return them. Despite my hesitancy and possible unwillingness, force me to accept your causeless mercy. May I never know ghostly existence again; please make me your authentic son.

Begging always to remain your servant,  
Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, who are the most intimate servant of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda!

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“In the revealed scriptures it is declared that the spiritual master should be worshiped like the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and this injunction is obeyed by pure devotees of the Lord. The spiritual master is the most confidential servant of the Lord. Thus let us offer our respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of our spiritual master.”**

By the grace of guru and Kṛṣṇa everything is possible. This conviction never leaves me. You have showed us that impossible dreams are a myth. Everything is possible for one who trusts in the instruction of his spiritual master. You have taught us with your example that perfection is reached by faithfully following the teachings of the spiritual master. Your faith in the words of your spiritual master has been the flag that has driven you to victory. To fortify our faith, we should constantly meditate on your deeds and assiduously read your books. The Vedic knowledge flows through your writings with eloquent clarity and transparency. Your simple and direct language lets us grasp the deepest truths of the Supreme Reality. If until now we’ve been saved from taking a wrong path, it has been because of the help received from your magic books. Actually, most of the devotees join the movement inspired by the reading of your books.

In times of bewilderment, there’s nothing better than reading your books. The reading of your transcendental books is the nourishment that our soul needs. Our spiritual anemia can be cured only with vitamin B (*Bhagavad-gītā*), complex B12 (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*) and vitamin C (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*). The attentive reading of your commentated works wakes up our consciousness, fortifies our spirit and arises new hopes in our heart. Reading your Bhaktivedānta purports with open mind purifies our understanding and expands the perceptive capacity, which makes transcendence more accessible. By regularly reading your work one can become conscious of the real nature of things in all circumstances.

To all kinds of people, you present in your books the knowledge of the Supreme Absolute Truth in a transparent and accessible way. Actually, your books are the basis supporting your movement. Reading your books connects us with the spiritual world. Through your books you’re restoring the consciousness of *bhāgavata-dharma* in the hearts of your millions of readers. Beyond a shadow of doubt, your books are lifesavers, because everyone who reads them goes from the shipwrecked condition the one of survivor. The flow of spiritual knowledge emanating from your books makes us float and breathe with satisfaction in the dangerous ocean of material existence. The purifying effect of reading your books turns everything positive and brilliant, and we amazed to see ourselves awakening a little more from the illusion that stopped us from seeing things in the right perspective. For example, after reading the first canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the great movie director Federico Fellini commented, “When reading these books I seem to remember things I never before knew.”

Reading your books definitely awakens and recodifies our spiritual consciousness. By reading, we discover our deficiencies and capacities and realize that we can understand many things we couldn’t understand before, or we notice things that we had simply ignored. Reading your books enables us to have incredible perceptions. It’s as if the sublime magic of the divine reaches down to us and delivers us from illusion and removes our anxiety.

Nowadays, despite your clear instructions, we still theorize thoughtlessly about the practical application of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Obviously we still lack a lot of experience concerning the proper way to process the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Most of us are still practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to drive away the dangers of material existence, not to achieve genuine devotion. But, little by little, the higher taste of devotional service impels us to spontaneously serve Kṛṣṇa.

We have to get accustomed to value things through the light of your wise teachings and mature reflection, not through improvised thoughts. If we control the tendency to speculate, the Lord will reward our every austerity of thought and word with pure intelligence and right comprehension of things. It is necessary to learn from you to be driven by the fascination of real Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Actually, any difficulty we find in understanding our real duty in life vanishes when we place our faith in you. But faith without acts is something deathly. You instruct us to have living faith, which is manifested through acts. You showed us your faith in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by means of your own work.

From any point of view, you are unique, and so is your movement. You were the first worldwide Vaiṣṇava preacher. After you, some others are running through the world you have created and recognizing your greatness. You have always been an example of spiritual perfection. As an expert gardener of *bhakti,* you have planted the seed of devotion in our hearts after transforming its dryness into fertility. We all have been mercifully saved by you. Our capacities and talent were rusting in the shadow, but you have taken them to the light in order to purify them and make them useful in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Actually, we’re very fortunate: the devotional attitude is natural in the spiritual world, but who would think that our devotion for Kṛṣṇa could flourish in the material world? This is definitely a miracle. That’s why we fervently offer you—now and forever—our humble, respectful obeisances, recognizing you as the most genuine and distinguished representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

You have spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness plentifully and generously through the five continents and taught us how to serve Kṛṣṇa by your chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and offering your whole life in sacrifice. Your compassion toward us has been unlimited. You have had the audacity to convert us into preachers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, a mercy we find explained in your own words:

Because a bona fide spiritual master is a representative of Kṛṣṇa, if he bestows any blessings upon his disciple, that will make the disciple immediately advanced without the disciple’s following the regulative principles. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 13.8–12, purport\]

Your unlimited qualities are the continuous inspiration that fortifies us. We are very proud to be your followers. The invisible force that activates our understanding comes from you. You are the official distributor of the mystic potency that transforms us and makes us preach. You were the first to astound the world in general and the Vaiṣṇava community in particular with the realization of utopia: to convert all people around the world—from the most varied filiations and tendencies—into genuine Vaiṣṇavas. In yourself you perfectly fulfilled the Kṛṣṇa’s statement in the *Bhagavad-gītā* about great personalities :

Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues. (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.21)

You haven’t been just one more guru. You have been a great reformer, an *ācārya* with the power to determine the best way to spread the movement of Lord Caitanya throughout the world. You have shaped a worldwide institution with its own physiognomy, whose centers, in spite of being interconnected through one single will in pursuit of a single goal, still keep their own operative autonomy. You renewed your own *sampradāya,* establishing a new order of genuine characteristics and convincing successes. Like some former remarkable *ācāryas,* you abolished some traditions and introduced some innovations to ignite the universal spiritual revolution we all know. You clearly delineated the formula of success for those who wish to help your mission: “Books are the basis, purity is the force, preaching is the essence, and utility is the principle.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, now more than ever we are proud to testify that, despite our imperfections, your International Society for Krishna Consciousness keeps on working vigorously just as you conceived it. This is a great event, an unsurpassable success unique in the history of Vaiṣṇavism. Never before has there been such a united worldwide institution of devotees, with an undeviating mission to broadly distribute the Vedic literature in every country of the world and to faithfully fulfill each precept and instruction of the founder-*ācārya*.

As followers of you and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we have to endeavor to please you both, although that may imply some risks. As you once said,

One who does not like to take the risk, they do not take the risk of preaching. But one who takes the risk, he is recognized by Kṛṣṇa immediately, “Oh, he is taking risk.” But he must know where to take risk and where to act foolishly. \[Conversation, Chicago, July 8th, 1975\]

Your explanations of the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness place us on the transcendental platform and make us deliberate with the proper clarity. Whatever the issue, one who meditates seriously on your words should have no doubts:

This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement directly receives instructions from the Supreme personality of Godhead via persons who are strictly following His instructions. Although a follower may not be a liberated person, if he follows the supreme, liberated Personality of Godhead, his actions are naturally liberated from the contamination of the material nature. Lord Caitanya therefore says: “By My order you may become a spiritual master.” One can immediately become a spiritual master by having full faith in the transcendental words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and by following His instructions. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.18.5, purport)

Dear lord and master, please be patient with us. We still can’t understand you, because we’re still involved in mediocrity. We often misunderstand your simplicity with our vulgarity. Forgive us. By your mercy we will overcome all the obstacles and reach the goal you have inculcated in our hearts. You manifest the unlimited greatness of mystic perfection and the most rare devotional virtues one may imagine. Each of your words is a precise clue to discover the real dimension of spiritual consciousness and reach Kṛṣṇa. If we still disagree about what you have taught us, it is because we have not yet assimilated the real meaning of the word “surrender.”

Your unconditional servant,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet, which are the shelter of unlimited *jīvas* in this world. All glories to you and the glorious service you performed and are still performing for the pleasure of your Guru Mahārāja, our Lord, and the conditioned *jīvas*.

*prabhupāda pada-kamala, koṭī-candra suśītala,*  
*ye chāyāya jagat juḍāya*

Without doubt this verse applies to you in every way. So many of us were burning up in the all-consuming fire of material lust. But now by your great compassion the heat that made us completely delirious is now abating, and real, normal, true consciousness is beginning to be established. The cooling rays of the shelter of your lotus feet is bringing sanity to this fallen fool and many, many others also. By taking shelter of your moonlike lotus feet, all souls can experience the great cooling effects that are bringing relief from the fierce raging fever of this horrific bewildering age.

Your instructions are the great medicine for putting an end to the great disease that caused the fever to rise and the delirium of lust to overcome this soul. I am so very fortunate to be in the situation that I am, and only due to your compassion, your mercy. What can I offer you? Only what you have given me! But I have only accepted a fraction of what you have given, and yet the cooling rays are felt so much. How much more are you offering? I will try to stay under the shade of your lotus feet, and then I will find out just what more you have to offer.

Please never kick this fool away. Please just keep me engaged in your service in whatever way you wish. I thank you for the association of all kinds of devotees. You have given me a nice soul in the form of Śivarāma Swami. He tolerates me so much that he must get such a great quality from you. He works so hard that it makes me feel I am just a time-wasting fool. He makes me want to do something for you. Thank you!

Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami. He loves to chant and dance for hours, and loves getting everyone else involved. He is enthusing me. He likes to cook for the devotees and serve them *prasādam*. It is so nice to see that service attitude in him. It all came by your mercy.

Bhakti Vidyā Pūrṇa Swami. He’s a good one. What a devotee! He can talk on virtually any subject, and is so simple and austere in his own ways. Another soul who likes cooking for and serving the devotees. He enthuses me very much, another great gem in the Bhaktivedanta army.

Indradyumna Swami. Loves putting on big festivals so that the fallen souls can hear and chant the holy names, get the philosophy, eat *prasādam,* and become attracted to your great movement. He works so hard putting himself out so much to please you. Thank you for his association.

Bhuvaneśvara Prabhu. Simple soul. Just continues with his service in Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma’s kitchen. But every time we talk, he is always quoting something you have said in your books in such a simple way. Nearly everything is “Śrīla Prabhupāda said …” It is so nice and warming to the heart. There are so many nice souls in your movement.

Mahādyuti, Kṛṣṇa Kṣetra, Kīrtirāja, Śrīdhara Swami, Muralivādaka, Bhakti Caitanya Swami, Medhavī, Bīmal Prasād, Harikeśa Swami, Bhakti Cāru Swami, and many, many more. They all have so many great devotional qualities. And all these qualities are there by your mercy, and I am here only by your mercy.
