I am hoping one day very soon to be the devotee that you want me to be. Till then, please tolerate and continue to guide me in my life. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please forgive this fool for all his offenses. You must be tired of hearing me say this, as I say it to you so many times a day.

All glories to your moonlike lotus feet, which are as cooling as millions of moons!

Your insignificant servant, the worthless  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on your Vyāsa-pūjā and this offering, which attempts to glorify the *sannyāsī* way of life.

You bless the order  
Of the saffron man,  
Who owns no home,  
No burrow or tree,

Who speaks of life,  
Its unending circle,  
And the option  
To move beyond.

He is poor by choice.  
All that’s his is  
A staff, a pot,  
And a piece of cloth

There is no mistress,  
No fancy food,  
No soft comfort,  
And no perfumes

The thirst for name  
And hunger for fame  
Must all be behind.  
They have no place.

He welcomes the wind,  
Heat, cold, and rain.  
Indifferent he is  
To the seasons’ spin.

You declare him meek  
When he’s on his feet,  
Touching the hearts  
From soul to soul.

He is not alone.  
There are always two.  
You bless this order  
For the sake of man.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, and all glories to your unlimited compassion on the fallen souls of this material world!

You were not one to minimize the words of your Guru Mahārāja, and when he suggested that you try to preach in the English language you took it as an order. While your Godbrothers were locked in legal battles to acquire custody over the properties of your Guru Mahārāja, you seized the initiative. Your Guru Mahārāja’s real asset was not temples and other property but the potency to preach. This you inherited in full, and thus you opened up the Western world to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But to your transcendental eyes Kṛṣṇa consciousness was not meant to stop in the West: it had to be distributed to every race all over the planet.

The scriptures tell us that unless we are empowered with *kṛṣṇa-śakti* we will not be able to spread the chanting of the Lord’s Holy Names. As a perfectly pure representative of the Lord, you appeared in this world to distribute love of Kṛṣṇa without concern for your own well-being. You taught us by both your words and your actions that pure devotional service is not a business—not a question of giving something to get something in return—but that it is a transcendental process of giving everything for Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s service.

In the material world, memories of departed souls are quickly replaced with new plans for sense pleasure, but it is very wonderful that every year devotees are becoming more and more attached to your lotus feet. More and more devotees are publishing their memories of your association, and even those devotees who were unable to avail themselves of your physical association aspire for the shelter of your lotus feet. All this is not very surprising, because you are the pure devotee of the Lord, the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva who appears in order to deliver us from the ignorance of this material world and engage us in the supreme occupation, devotional service to the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our actual offering to you comes not just in these words but with the dedication of every act and thought to your service.

Praying for your mercy,  
Your most insignificant servant,  
Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Praying to Be of Use**

Wandering  
on the desolate streets of Māyā,  
we kick empty cans of promise—  
searching,  
for substance.

Looking up,  
we meet your caring eyes,  
with hand  
outstretched  
you beckon us.

Standing before you,  
we feel shameful,  
as our endless material endeavor  
has made us  
useless.

Overlooking our weakness,  
you encourage us,  
inviting us to help you,  
giving us a place  
in this mission of the Lord.

This lonely wayward soul,  
humbly taking your order on his head,  
prays to be of service,  
prays  
to be of use.

dāsa Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the great *ācārya* whose mission is to save all the souls in the universe. Your drum is now beating in every town and village of the world, and in every stratum of society. Those who are active in that mission glorify you at every moment, for their glorification of Śrī Kṛṣṇa necessarily includes glorification of you, who gave Him to us.

Nevertheless, Vyāsa-pūjā is a special day for remembrance and glorification of your divine personality, pastimes, and qualities. Today we shall offer homages, flowers, and feasts, and hopefully many a tear. But maybe the most important offering your direct disciples can make to you is that of the many souls whom we accept as our disciples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you told us to “Do as I am doing.” Although all of your disciples are highly elevated souls (by your mercy), all of us together cannot add up to even a fraction of what you are, just as all the *gopīs* together cannot equal Rādhā. Still, we are trying to do as you did, and by your blessings the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is becoming more and more successful.

Doing as you did, I also accept disciples. As you were worshiped, I, the most despicable and mean of your disciples, also accept worship. I am certainly not fit to be worshiped; I deserve to be flogged in the streets. I am not fit to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, or to wear the *tilaka* mark of devotees, or even to have any connection with Kṛṣṇa or His devotees. But you assure me, “Yes, this is what you must do.”

Accepting worship seems as antithetical to Vaiṣṇavism as anything could be, but you have taught us this culture, and the Vaiṣṇava secret that goes with it: “Offer everything to guru and Kṛṣṇa. Don’t accept anything on your own behalf.”

They are singing *saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-…*But it is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who receive benediction from the ocean of mercy. It is you who dance in ecstasy in Lord Caitanya’s *kīrtana*. It is you who teach Deity worship and enjoy distributing *prasādam*. It is you who relish the pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhikā-Mādhava and are expert in assisting the *gopīs* in the groves of Vṛndāvana. It is you who directly represent Kṛṣṇa, and by whose mercy the whole world receives His benediction. It is you, therefore, who are fit to be worshiped as the representative of the Supreme Absolute Truth Personality of Godhead.

Like everything else I have in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, my disciples are gifts from you, entrusted into my care to help keep me engaged in your service. I must offer them back to you, as Gaṅgā is offered to Gaṅgā.

I know nothing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but what you have taught me—which is everything. I have barely realized a dram of your teachings, but I know they constitute the Absolute Truth. And I know that, by conveying your message to others, they will receive the highest benediction.

My duty is to train my disciples to be worthy followers of the Prabhupāda line. Only if they properly represent you can I happily present them to you. My success will be in training them to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you taught it, and to teach them to recognize your unique and unsurpassable position as the founder- *ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, the vehicle through which Lord Caitanya’s inner desires (*mano ’bhīṣṭam*) are to be fulfilled.

This eulogy will take shape not only as writing on paper, but in the words, actions, and thoughts of my disciples. Therefore I am presenting some guidelines so that my disciples may become true Prabhupādānugas. I desire that my disciples:

(1) Be staunch followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda in example and precept;

(2) Be convinced that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead;

(3) Preach this message with full vigor and confidence;

(4) Become learned in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books;

(5) Follow the process of *sādhana* given by Śrīla Prabhupāda;

(6) Be ideal examples of Vaiṣṇava behavior and dealings;

(7) Not be rascals and renege on their initiation vows;

(9) Become attached to the holy names of Kṛṣṇa, both in *japa* and *kīrtana;*

(10) Not be afraid to present themselves as devotees, both in dress (always wearing Vaiṣṇava dress and *tilaka* and not hiding behind *karmī* appearances, unless actually necessary) and in principle (not mixing the philosophy with compromises and speculations);

(11) Always remain within Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON, and not go seeking so-called spiritual solutions elsewhere.

Above all, if my disciples become pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa, they can deliver themselves and the whole world by your grace. They can also deliver me from the ocean of offenses and ineptitude that blemishes my life, and help make me worthy to be placed along with them among the flowers at your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am your very fallen and lowly servant, battered by *māyā* but ever willing to keep up the fight, by your blessings. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept me as an eternal assistant in your mission of bringing millions of *jīvas* to the shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Let me not be a miser in distributing your mercy. I pray that you always protect me from the madness of becoming a usurper and a hypocrite.

Desiring ever the dust of your feet on my head,  
The most wretched, dīna hīna kaṅgala pāmara,  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

There are many bad qualities in my character. I have prayed to have my imperfections revealed to me, and recently some certainly have been. Of course, everyone else is well aware of them, I am sure. But what to do? If I remain contaminated, then how can I assist in the spreading of Lord Caitanya’s mission? Purification is required. Of all the resolutions I could make to improve, the most effective would be to commit myself to increasing the quality of my chanting.

To perfect the repetition of the Holy Name, we have to remove the ten offenses. This is where the work comes in. The mother of all offenses is inattention. Kṛṣṇa is right there in His Name. Though I say it, where is my attention? In a mind that is fixed even on just the sound vibration, what to speak of being fixed on the deep realization of the Lord, where is the possibility for any offense? It is only because the mind is doing something else that there is room for offense. Yet my mind babbles on, mantra after mantra. Chanting is the process whereby I can immediately improve my service so much, yet I fail to take it. Luckily, Kṛṣṇa has given me eternity to perfect chanting, and for me it may take that long.

There have been times in my devotional life when I have felt the mercy of Kṛṣṇa come upon me. And in these times, the Name was there. Sometimes I saw character flaws that have been with me for a long time suddenly disappear; sometimes I saw myself step past temptations I usually go for; sometimes I saw myself take up a task I usually avoid or minimize because of its austerity. I searched for the reason behind these events and concluded it was the Holy Name. This experience could be mine again if I will just focus on the Holy Name. Dear Prabhupāda, please help me do this. All other ways of improving my nature are sure to fail—they have failed.

May Kṛṣṇa grant me the intense desire to raise my level of concentration. Every day Māyā offers the same diversions to chanting, and I fall for them. Somehow, let me gain the intense desire to really hear the Holy Name. Chanting is the king of all types of purification.

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam*

**“O King, constant chanting of the holy name of the Lord after the ways of the great authorities is the doubtless and fearless way of success for all, including those who are free from all material desires, those who are desirous of all material enjoyment, and also those who are self-satisfied by dint of transcendental knowledge” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.1.11).**

*harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā*

**“For spiritual progress in this Age of Kali, there is no alternative, there is no alternative, there is no alternative to the holy name, the holy name, the holy name of the Lord” (*****Bṛhan-nāradīya Purāṇa*** **38.126).**

*kaler doṣa-nidhe rājann asti hy eko mahān guṇaḥ*  
*kīrtanād eva kṛṣṇasya mukta-saṅgaḥ paraṁ vrajet*

**“My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: Simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***mahā-mantra,*** **one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom.”**

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Chandramauli Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

All glories to your most precious and divine lotus feet! Those feet have left their impressions throughout the entire globe, implanting the message of pure *bhakti* to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Please accept my most fallen obeisances in a spirit of humble submission.

On this most glorious and supremely auspicious occasion of your appearance, I find the need to rededicate my life in assisting your mission of trying to reclaim the fallen conditioned souls.

Although I can see just how unable I am to truly accept the mercy you are giving me, still I have hope that someday I’ll be able to come up to the standard that is required to preach effectively in this Kali-yuga.

Presently I am visiting the holy land of Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma* and sincerely praying to receive the blessings and mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu so I can return to my service in the US with renewed enthusiasm and greater determination.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me understand how I can truly serve you more and more in a most pleasing way, and bless me with exclusive attraction for the lotus feet of the divine couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

Your lowly servant,  
Chandramauli Swami

## Dānavīr dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. We find present in Your Divine Grace the first fifty qualities of Kṛṣṇa described in the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*. With your permission, we shall briefly discuss them.

(1) Beautiful features of the entire body: Why else would so many photographs have been taken of you?

(2) Marked with all auspicious characteristics: Your horoscope read, “This child, after seventy years, will be a great religious propagator and will go across the sea to foreign countries and establish so many temples.”

(3) Extremely pleasing: Once I witnessed how you hosted the parents of a Western disciple. You personally served them so attentively that it seemed they were as important as the King of Heaven, Indra, and his wife, Śacī.

(4) Effulgent: Like the brilliant full moon, you outshone the firmament of twinkling Indian swamis and gurus.

(5) Strong: You single-handedly pushed back the powerful ocean of material existence.

(6) Ever youthful: *kīrtir yasya sa jīvati:* “One who has done something substantial, he lives forever.”

(7) Wonderful linguist: You wrote, “Personally, I have no hope for any direct service for the coming crores of births of the sojourn of my life, but I am confident that some day or other I shall be delivered from this mire of delusion in which I am at present so deeply sunk.”

(8) Truthful: In Portland, you were asked a question by a young radio interviewer: “Are you the messiah?” You replied “Yes.”

(9) Talks pleasingly: In Mumbai, on a morning walk accompanied by several gentlemen, you said, “Now, one thing I must explain. I think Dr. Patel is the eldest of you.…He’s just like my younger brother. And you are all, mostly, you are just like my sons.…So I have got the right to say sometimes ‘Rascal, fool.’ Don’t be angry. That is out of love.”

(10) Fluent: Perfect in Sanskrit, Hindi, Bengali, and English. Furthermore, you communicated skillfully with earth’s many varieties of animallike species.

(11) Highly learned: The Minister of Education of Maharashtra, India, wrote, “It is good fortune of the world that these two spiritual works \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*\]…have been translated and commented upon in the style of a true scholar by His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, out of his obviously great desire to inject the world with his vast storehouse of learning.”

(12) Highly intelligent: In Washington, D.C., Dr. Sukla said to you, “So I decided and we were using the entire *Gītā* for the second year. Your contributions can’t be duplicated. And same thing with the *Bhāgavatam*.…The translations are very accurate. So it’s real scholarship there. And people who were not aware of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, they know that if the intellect is so powerful, the spirit must be powerful too.”

(13) A genius: Who else could turn forty rupees into forty crores within ten years?

(14) Artistic: In 1972, you taught the artists: “You can show in sequence in a painting how Nārada Muni is leaving the material body and accepting a spiritual body and entering into Lord Viṣṇu and again coming out through Brahmā’s heart.”

(15) Extremely clever: You outfoxed the shrewd Nairs and won the Juhu property in Mumbai. Your strategy: “Chaganlal must insist on the contract price, 7 lakhs and if we give him 3-4 lakhs more profit, that will be a very excellent arrangement between Chaganlal and ourselves…”

(16) Expert: To the devotees at New Vrindavana you explained, “As soon as the milk is drawn it is put into the pan. The pan is in the fire. So as much as you like, drink milk, children, elderly persons. Then at night, when there is no demand for milk, it is converted into yogurt, not wasted.…Then in daytime also you take yogurt, as much as you like. If it is not all consumed, then it is stored in a pot. Then when that pot is enough stored, then you churn it…and you get butter and…buttermilk. So again you take buttermilk with chapati and everything, not a single drop is lost. Then the butter, you melt it, convert into ghee and store it, it will stay for years.…Ghee is very important thing in the city. So they purchase.…In exchange of that money, whatever they \[you\] want, they \[you\] purchase in the city and come back.…So not a drop of milk can be wasted.”

(17) Grateful: In London you said, “By the help of my disciples this movement is gaining ground and is spreading all over the world. So therefore I have to thank you. It is all due to you. It is not my credit, but it is your credit that you are helping me in executing the order of my Guru Mahārāja.”

(18) Firmly determined: To Captain Singhal in 1972 you wrote, “I shall be the last man to give up the Hare Kṛṣṇa Land to the rogue Mr. Nair.”

(19) An expert judge of time and circumstances: You told a disciple in 1972: “But in order to expand properly all over British Isles we shall require a big, big headquarters as you are contemplating.…Better to seize the iron while the fire is hot, that my Guru Mahārāja used to tell me. So George is now convinced of our movement, he will pay, so why the delay and difficulty? I think you should very seriously and with a cool head apply yourself to getting that place or some other place as soon as possible, at least by springtime. Otherwise Kṛṣṇa may take away this golden opportunity, being disappointed by our lack of enthusiasm and labor.” Bhaktivedanta Manor ki jaya!

(20) Sees and speaks on the authority of *Vedas,* or scriptures: In a Los Angeles lecture you said, *yo me bhaktyā prayacchati / tad aham…aśnāmi*. “I eat,” Kṛṣṇa says. Kṛṣṇa is a liar? He’s God, He says that “I eat.” You cannot say that Kṛṣṇa does not eat. Kṛṣṇa says “I eat.” Who are you to say that Kṛṣṇa does not eat?”

(21) Pure: In a 1974 Calcutta lecture you said, “We speak from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we speak from *Bhagavad-gītā,* and present them as it is, without any adulteration. This is our position. If you like, then you make progress. If you don’t like, that is your option. But we cannot make any compromise. We must present the *śāstra* as it is.”

(22) Self-controlled: It is universally known that you never fell down from the regulative principles.

(23) Steadfast: You once told a friend, “In India, the Delhi is the coldest part. In my business life, in the hotel, I was taking cold water \[bath\]. Everyone was surprised. “How you can?” In Saharanpur, in bitter cold, I was taking cold water early in the morning.”

(24) Forbearing: You recalled, “The seventieth year was severe, three heart attacks and paralysis. Without any help. At that time none of you were with me. I was alone.”

(25) Forgiving: Upon hearing of a *sannyāsī*-disciple’s difficulties, you wrote the following in a telegram, “My dear son.…Please immediately come and live with me¾letter follows. So, when you come here we shall talk in detail and according to the conditions we shall arrange everything. There is no worry. I am still prepared to accept you as my original disciple and if there is anything wrong, I am prepared to try and correct it so that your life may not be spoiled. Please therefore come here immediately and live with me.”

(26) Grave: You preferred not to discuss with others your own personal *rasa* with Lord Kṛṣṇa.

(27) Self-satisfied: You said, “We have kept two chairs for the visitors. (laughter) We don’t require. We can lie down on the floor. We can use only one or two cloths, that’s all, throughout the whole year. We have no demands, only bare necessities. We don’t smoke, don’t drink. There is no expenditure. Don’t go to cinema, don’t read newspaper or ordinary magazines, nothing.”

(28) Possessing equilibrium: You declared, “Our court case \[New York Supreme Court\]…would have been continued for years, but \[in\] one day…Even though there was some difficulty, it is finished. So our enemies, they tried to block our march, but we shall kick on their head and progress our end—if we stick to our principles.”

(29) Magnanimous: You wrote, “The spiritual master accepts the sinful activities of his disciples from the first initiation. I may give initiation very easily, but what can I do? I am prepared to go to hell for service of Lord Caitanya.”

(30) Religious: Your every act was in accord with Kṛṣṇa’s desire; therefore you are religion personified.

(31) Heroic: The terrorist Naxalites sent a note threatening murder if you continued preaching at the Calcutta pandal programs: “Fly or die!” But instead of fleeing, you called the Naxalites together and you *preached* to them!

(32) Compassionate: You once told a reporter, “We welcome women, men, poor, rich, everyone, because in that platform equality.…We do not refuse anyone.”

(33) Respectful: In Los Angeles you spoke from the *vyāsāsana,* “Kṛṣṇa, kindly take it. I have no qualification. I am most rotten, fallen, but \[begins to cry\]I have brought this thing for you. Please take it.”

(34) Gentle: When a devotee artist apologized for stepping on your sitting place to hang a painting, you replied that the devotee could step on your head for the Lord’s service.

(35) Liberal: You said, “A *brahmacārī* is strictly prohibited not to see even one young woman. But what can be done? In the Western countries, the boys and girls, they mix very freely. And if I say, “My dear boys, you cannot see even a young girl,” then finished. My business there is finished. Therefore I have to arrange according to the country, according to the circumstances, as far as possible. So gradually, they are coming to the perfectional stage.”

(36) Shy: You sang, “Somehow of other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like.”

(37) The protector of surrendered souls: You wrote, “These children are given to us by Kṛṣṇa, they are Vaiṣṇavas and we must be very careful to protect them. These are not ordinary children, they are Vaikuṇṭha children, and we are very fortunate we can give them chance to advance further in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

(38) Happy: Unless you were unlimitedly happy, how could so many people gain so much happiness simply by associating with Your Divine Grace?

(39) The well-wisher of devotees: You wrote, “I am always praying to Kṛṣṇa that my disciples will not fall away from the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness”

(40) Controlled by love: You wrote to a devotee, “I am simply at your mercy and I have only the protection of Kṛṣṇa in all circumstances; therefore, whatever you make me to do, that I am prepared.”

(41) All-auspicious: You said, “We are guru not that because we are very learned, so-called Vedāntist. No. We are lowest of the lowest. But we are faithful to the words of Kṛṣṇa. That’s all.”

(42) Most powerful: You said, “And I say to them \[the scientists\], ‘If life originated from chemicals, and if your science is so advanced, then why can’t you create life biochemically in your laboratories?’”

(43) All-famous: The *Encyclopedia Britannica* recognized your astounding literary achievement, and the Indian government issued a special postage stamp of you.

(44) Popular: Otherwise, how can it be explained that nearly two hundred million of your books have been purchased in dozens of languages around the world?

(45) Partial to devotees: You said to your disciples, “I have called you for that purpose. So if death is to take place, let me die in your association and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.”

(46) Very attractive to all women: You enchanted thousands of beautiful young ladies and then delivered them all to the supreme enjoyer, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

(47) All-worshipable: Śrī Kavi said to Mahārāja Nimi, “An intelligent person should engage unflinchingly in the unalloyed devotional service of the Lord, under the guidance of a bona fide spiritual master, whom he should accept as his worshipable deity and as his very life and soul.”

(48) All-opulent: Hundreds of centers, thousands of disciples, millions of followers, and billions of dollars in assets. Or as you put it: “What is our asset? Asset is only Kṛṣṇa.”

(49) All-honorable: Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura sings, “The spiritual master is honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord.”

(50) The supreme controller: Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī told Mahārāja Parīkṣit, “The Supreme Lord has one transcendental attribute: He comes under the control of His devotees.”

His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda 108 Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your poor servant,  
Dānavīr dāsa Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your books, your dedicated disciples, and your competent granddisciples are now established as bright beacons in the darkness before the storm.

Sociological studies say that in porlonged situations of complete upheaval, normal social behavior cracks, like a split atom. Then something strange and volatile is let loose. The usually noncommittal mainstream of society breaks apart. The people become polarized. Some go far over to the saintly side; others veer far into the demonic. The apathetic middle disappears. Certainly you are happy to see your ISKCON poised to come to the fore amidst such a gale.

In any case, whether global war, economic ruin, or natural disaster strikes, you have finally convinced me that a major world-wide catastrophe is already here. Western civilization is a total wreck. Perhaps during its bygone days of Christian influence, elements of crude religiosity sometimes prevailed. But now even the faint whiff of *yavana* piety has long faded. Both the traditional and modern Euro-American world views are collapsing. Increasingly the people do not know what to believe, nor do they care.

If the dusk of Western society indeed turns into a disastrous night, you have devotional armies of compassionate Vaiṣṇavas that will spiritually survive—even thrive. The Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra you brought to the West and the books you insisted be produced and distributed are more than enough to outshine both so-called good times and bad. You once said that just as an expert businessman makes a profit whether the price of rice goes up or down, similarly a real devotee can take advantage of any situation to generate attractive service for Kṛṣṇa.

I pray at your lotus feet that I may render better service if acute times do arise.

Attempting to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Dyutidhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance, I fall prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet. I personally have no qualifications whatsoever to consider myself your disciple or offer any meaningful praise to your divine self.

You have many, many wonderful disciples and followers boldly preaching the superexcellent precepts of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. These great souls are actually a testament to your eternal greatness. Through them you will certainly live forever as the pastimes of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu inundate the whole material creation and drown all the inhabitants in love of Godhead.

You started the Hare Kṛṣṇa explosion in the lowly streets of New York City. In your first Hare Kṛṣṇa temple, at 26th Second Avenue, you were said to have a bucket of *gulabjāmuns* just by the side of the doorway. The reason you had this bucket of *gulabjāmuns* was that if some unnerved or discouraged follower felt the urge to leave, he or she might hesitate and pass by the doorway to take just one more ISKCON bullet and then just one more, and then another. That juice is what you have given me as meaning to my life. Please let me pause by that bucket eternally.

Begging to remain a servant,  
Dyutidhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

So try to spread this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. This is your *sādhana,* execution of austerity, penance. Because you have to meet so many opposing elements. \[Lecture, November 15, 1968\]

We are spending our blood, gallons of blood, to make them *bhāgyavān*. This is the sacrifice of the devotees. \[Conversation January 1, 1974\]

To face such reverse conditions of life \[to approach the Jagāis and Mādhāis\] and to suffer thereby the results of actions is considered as the greatest penance and austerity in the matter of spiritual advancement of life. \[Letter, January 27, 1970\]

Your Divine Grace has taught us that preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is itself the greatest austerity we can perform in this age. But if we study the lives of our great predecessor *ācāryas,* we find that they first qualified themselves for their missions by the practice of personal austerities. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were certainly no exception.

We learn how Gandhi became powerful due to his sacrifices and austerities. You wrote that celibacy by itself could insure a position in Satyaloka. You also informed us that every great man (like Napoleon) didn’t sleep much, considering there were more important things to do. And who placed more value on time than the great *mahājanas* did in their lives (*avyartha-kālatvam*)?

So every nice thing presented in the world, that requires austerity. Very devout, painstaking. Then it becomes successful. That is called *yajña*. *Tapasya*. \[Conversation, September 11, 1969\]

In material existence, we are impure, and thus we cannot do anything wonderful. But if we purify our existence by *tapasya,* we can do wonderful things by the grace of the Lord. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.4.51, purport\]

Sometimes we speak that “I’ll preach.” What you will preach? First of all get the power of attorney, then preach. Preaching is not so easy that anyone can preach.…So to get that power of attorney one has to qualify himself, not that power of attorney is hanging in the tree and you can take it. \[Lecture, July 11, 1976\]

Once, you termed devotional service “a learned labor of love.” You said the Lord wants to see how painstaking a devotee is in his service. Surely, to carry on the tradition established by such exalted stalwarts as Lord Brahmā, Nārada Muni, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Madhvācārya, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, and your divine self is a very tall order, one that will demand our utmost surrender.

No one in our disciplic succession felt themselves qualified for the task at hand. Our *sampradāya*-head, Lord Brahmā, set the standard by his practice of meditation for one hundred years atop the sacred lotus. Similarly, Nārada Muni achieved his elevated status by meditation upon the Supersoul in a deep forest while he was but an orphan of five years. And Śrīla Vyāsadeva, under the instruction of Nārada, sat in meditative trance in order to achieve full realization of the Absolute before compiling his literary masterpiece, the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Arjuna’s preaching took the form of extinguishing the negative elements on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra. For this purpose, he acquired the necessary celestial weapons by dint of austerities so severe that he rivaled even the feats of Hiraṇyakaśipu.

Before writing his commentary on the *Vedānta-sūtra,* Madhvācārya sought the blessings of Vedavyāsa. Not only did he accomplish the entire grueling journey to Badrinath on foot, but he prepared for the final ascent by observing forty-eight days of fasting and silent meditation!

Śrī Mādhavendra Purī traveled thousands of miles just to collect sandalwood by begging, although he would never beg for food to eat. Without considering his personal comforts, he carried over one hundred pounds of sandalwood and camphor through dangerous forests and toll gates to smear over the body of Lord Gopāla.

Advaita Ācārya and other Navadvīpa Vaiṣṇavas were so stricken with grief due to the suffering of the materialists that some of them even considered leaving their bodies. They lost their appetites and began fasting, at last renouncing all material comforts.

In order to deliver the poor conditioned souls, the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana kicked off all association of aristocracy as insignificant. They accepted loincloths, treating themselves as simple mendicants, sleeping under a different tree every night. Raghunātha dāsa would collect and eat decomposed rice that even the cows had rejected!

Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura demonstrated that the *gṛhastha-āśrama* poses no impediment to the divinely-inspired preacher. Although he held a responsible government position and maintained a large family, he wrote almost one hundred books on *Bhāgavata* philosophy by reducing his bodily necessities to almost nil.

Preparing to become the *dīkṣā-guru* of the eminent *jagat-guru* Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, Śrīla Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī exhibited an unparalleled mood of renunciation. He washed discarded cloth to cover his body and drank from a rejected clay pot. Parched rice mixed with Ganges water, or simply mud from Gaṅgā’s bank, sustained his life.

Although he is remembered and glorified mainly for his incredible preaching exploits, your own Guru Mahārāja performed many years of *tapasya* before he launched his Gauḍīya Mission. At the age of thirty-one, he began his great vow, following in the footsteps of Nāmācārya Haridāsa Ṭhākura, to chant three lakhs of Holy Names (192 rounds) per day. He strictly followed his vow and observed Cāturmāsya, cooking sun-dried rice and eating it from the floor in the manner of a cow. When it rained on his grass hut, he would simply use an umbrella and go on chanting.

Even though seeming not as spectacular as some of the above examples, Your Divine Grace’s sacrifices should not be taken as any less demanding than the rest. We should first of all remember that, being an eternal associate of the Lord, you did not descend into this material world by the force of *karma,* but to execute the personal plan of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Although it would have been easier for you to drop everything to follow Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī at your initial meeting with him, you executed his order to preach in English while maintaining your family responsibilities, just to set an example for those who followed you. In spite of suffering from heat-stroke and being gored by a cow, you never diminished your enthusiasm to produce and distribute *Back to Godhead* magazine.

Finally relinquishing your *gṛhastha* duties, you took shelter of Vṛndāvana *dhāma,* and following in the footsteps of Śrī Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, you lived only to create a revolution of consciousness through your Bhaktivedanta purports to Vaiṣṇava scripture. You not only translated and explained, but also collected funds, published, and personally distributed the finished copies as a one-man *saṅkīrtana* brigade.

Perhaps the most extraordinary symbol of self-sacrifice in our experience came when you again left the spiritual world (Rādhā-Dāmodara Maṇḍira) for the hellish regions of the Bowery. You did so at an advanced age, against formidable odds, and without any financial, institutional, or relational support. Śrīla Prabhupāda *ki jaya*!

Although anyone else would have surely retreated after the first expiry of his visa, you tolerated heart attacks, paraphernalia thefts, a Māyāvādī benefactor, a drug-crazed roommate, and the sub-freezing New York winters to make your journey fruitful.

But that’s not all. You sacrificed your rest by taking only 2 or 3 hours of sleep a night, you sacrificed your peace by constant global circulation, and you sacrificed your *samādhi* by maintaining a consistent managerial vigilance over the affairs of your Society. You wouldn’t even accept the luxury of leaving your body in a mood of retirement. Your example is incontestable.

And what then is my predicament? Am I to assume that I will automatically inherit your legacy just because I have become your “disciple” officially? How can I claim to be your follower with a mind as prone to lethargy and sense gratification as mine? When I hear your statement, uttered under physical debilitation, “Don’t think this won’t happen to you,” should I not also heed your intrinsic warning, “Don’t think your life will be easy going” as well? Please save me from this foolishness!

May I beg your mercy so that one day you can count me as one of your authentic followers.

Your unworthy aspirant,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept this humble offering on this most divine day of your appearance in this world.

**A Bow at Your Lotus Feet**

Riddled with doubt,  
Crawling about,  
No one to trust,  
Shackled by lust,  
Lost in the night,  
Nothing seemed right,

I was confounded—  
Māyā abounded,  
All-pervading,  
Never fading.

And then your word,  
Like naught I’ve heard,  
So pure and bold,  
Broke Māyā’s hold.  
With *bhaktas* devout  
Helping throughout,  
Off came the shroud.  
Finally I bowed.

I’ve bowed much since then.  
Three decades it’s been.  
But looking back,  
I see my lack,  
And cry inside.  
For bows can’t hide  
The lust and pride  
That still reside.  
An ugly me  
I pain to see,  
Quite far way  
And lost, I’d say.

But you’re so kind,  
For though I’m blind  
And fall behind,  
I’m still aligned,  
But can’t compete  
With those I meet,  
Who always stay  
Absorbed each day,  
Cent per cent,  
With pure intent,  
While at your feet,  
Their lives complete.

But I’ve got hope.  
You gave great scope  
To even me.  
You let me see  
How as I teach,  
And learn to reach  
To those in need,  
That simple deed  
All wrong will thwart,  
And clean my heart.  
You said it would.  
“No deed’s as good.  
The more we give,  
The better we live.”  
You lived that way,  
Till your final day.

And by your grace,  
I’ll quick’n my pace.  
And in due time,  
I pray this grime  
Within my heart  
Will finally part.

And so sublime  
Will be that time,  
For when I bow,  
I’ll then know how:  
Full devotion,  
Real emotion,  
One with you,  
Real life renewed,  
No lust, no pride,  
Nothing to hide.  
A bow complete,  
At your lotus feet!

This beggar is praying for your mercy and trying to be…  
Your eternal servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust at you lotus feet. I sometimes think of your divine lotus feet standing and walking in the dust of Vraja and hope that there are no thorns to cause you problems. Your lotus feet seem perfectly situated in the soft powder of the dust of Vraja, far, far from this modern cosmopolitan society. So far away that all thought of this cosmopolitan world is forever forgotten and you can relax from all the strains you undertake to give your mercy to the fallen souls in this material world and be completely united with Kṛṣṇa and all the cowherds and mothers and *gopīs* and Nanda Mahārāja and all the Vrajavāsīs in their service and activity.

I am certainly a very humble member of your movement and hope to continue in some happy function in this wonderful culture and gradually become a little more responsible and sincere in your service. Thank you for ISKCON and so many nice devotees with whom to associate. Thank you for the chance to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. I hope to get free from the world of death so that I may be able to participate in your ecstatic service to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. Please give me whatever mercy you can during the forthcoming year so that I can advance in devotional service and so that we can all advance and make some real progress in helping others.

All glories to His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda!!

Your very fallen servant,  
Hanumatpreṣaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

Twenty-one years ago you left this world to rejoin your beloved Lord and His associates in His spiritual abode. Their daily festival must have been especially sweet the day Lord Kṛṣṇa called you back to the spiritual sky.

But what can be said for those of us you left behind? Being only infants in spiritual life, we wept in separation and struggled to find you once again in your immortal words of wisdom, describing the spiritual master:

“He lives forever by his divine instructions—and the follower lives with him.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’ve tried to follow your instructions very carefully since your departure, and perhaps I’ve made a little progress. To not make progress would be to deny the very principle that by surrendering to a great soul, a conditioned soul conquers over the material energy and develops his love for the Lord. Of course, I realize that I have a long way to go, but genuine love has already begun to awaken in my heart…and that love is for the most important person in my life, Śrīla Prabhupāda—you.

Just as a child’s affection for his parents develops as he grows older, so my love for you, my spiritual father, deepens as I appreciate more each day this wonderful life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you’ve given me. You’ve bestowed upon me the shelter of your lotus feet, the divine holy name, the association of devotees, and the most sacred privilege of service to Lord Caitanya. But my love for you is not without pain, for it is love in separation.

I once asked you the question, “Why does the pure devotee feel separation from Kṛṣṇa, when the *śāstra*s say that he sees the Lord everywhere?”

You looked at me for along time—it seemed like an eternity—and then replied, “You will know the answer to that question when you become a little advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

Through the years I feel you’ve given me the answer, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for though you are not here, I feel your presence more each day, in the service of your mission. Transcendental relationships increase in separation and become more intense in the expectation of actually meeting once again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to meet you once again. Quite frankly, I consider this the cherished goal of my life. Of course, I know our “*siddhānta*” is love of God. But for me the Lord remains ever distant. You, however, are a transcendental personality whom I saw walk, talk, laugh, and even cry for Kṛṣṇa before my very eyes. Only now have I come to fully appreciate how fortunate I was to sit before you in class, as you perfectly expounded the science of the Absolute Truth. Only now can I see the great mercy I had to taste the remnants of your *prasādam* and sip the water that washed your lotus feet. Only now have I come to understand the grace I had in rendering you personal service, the highest benediction. Thus for me, “going back home, back to Godhead” means going back to you—to assist you in your personal service to the Lord.

But I know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I cannot ask such a blessing unless I have successfully served your order to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness within my lifetime. One in the renounced order of life is duty-bound to preach. It is for this reason that I have accepted so many responsibilities—responsibilities that are often very difficult and troublesome to perform. But as Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura expresses in his *Śaraṇāgati,* in a prayer to the Lord:

*tomāra saṁsāre koribo sevana*  
*nāhibo phalera bhāgī*  
*tava sukhe jāhe koribo jatana*  
*hoye pade anurāgī*

**“All the troubles encountered in Your service shall be the cause of great happiness, for in Your devotional service, joy and sorrow are equally great riches. Both destroy the misery of ignorance.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once personally told me that preaching is never easy within this material world. At that time I thought that you were simply referring to the austerity of distributing your books for five or six hours a day in the street. I never imagined it would one day mean fighting the great opposition we now face where I am preaching, in Eastern Europe and Russia. Here there are many formidable groups and institutions who consider us their sworn enemies and who have strategic plans to stop our preaching and close our temples, farms, and schools.

What great courage we require to fight such repression! What skill and tact we need to defend your cherished mission! What intelligence we must have to defeat our opponents! And how much mercy we require to always remember your lotus feet in such conditions!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the risks are real—and what’s more, victory or defeat will certainly define the shape and future of your movement in these countries for many years to come. We have inherited your mission, and we cannot fail in our attempt to defend you and the efforts of all the great *ācāryas* who came before you. Sometimes the odds against us are overwhelming, and one could easily give up the fight for an easier life of renunciation. However, history has shown how you faced even greater opposition in your struggle to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. By your strong faith in your Guru Mahārāja and the holy names of the Lord, you were successful beyond a doubt. May we follow your illustrious example and be victorious also.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I first joined the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, I was promised *halavah* and sweet rice, *purīs* and cakes. I never imagined that devotional service would one day mean fighting for what is right. But I’m happy to accept such service if it guarantees that once again I’ll have the benediction to associate with you—whom I saw walk, talk, and even cry for Kṛṣṇa, before my very eyes. For such a privilege I’m prepared to take as many births as you see fit in the service of your sacred mission.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Only Kṛṣṇa can glorify you adequately. And He has glorified you, deeply and splendidly. As you have glorified Him, He has glorified you. Not just “tit for tat,” a business deal, “a reciprocation.” Your glorification of Kṛṣṇa is glorious, and so is His glorification of you.

In a small way, we too can hope to glorify you—by following you, following strictly, living according to your words and your example, by preserving (or retrieving) what you gave us and expanding it, by transmitting Kṛṣṇa’s message intact, unadulterated, as we received it from you, by pulling others out of the muck of nescience and helping them happily proceed on the way to Kṛṣṇa.

And we can keep in our hearts the days when you were physically with us to praise our efforts or to smash us and call us fools.

This one small fool desires to desire the purity and strength to put aside all else and wholeheartedly serve your lotus feet, now and forever.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Keśava Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. On this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, as we are celebrating your transcendental appearance within this world, please accept my humble attempt in making this offering.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

With great ecstasy, sitting on the *vyāsāsana*, you are singing “Jaya Rādhā-Mādhava.” In that moment, we are remembering your personality, so kind and compassionate, so enthusiastic and happy. We are thinking of your glorious activities. Now we are thinking of your divine instructions. I feel that I must take these instructions within my heart and make them my life and soul. Because these instructions are nondifferent from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. But unfortunately I do not have the qualities to serve you properly. Still, if I only try I will be able to hear you. And only by your mercy, by hearing the words emanating from your lotus mouth, by carefully studying your books and serving you with faith, will I see my consciousness become gradually purified. This is my hope—that one day will come when I may serve you nicely.

We want to assist you in your mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your birth and activities in this world were all-auspicious and have brought great fortune into our lives. And by meditating on your exalted qualities, we discover the perfect guru, friend, father, grandfather, and pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi*** **1.45).**

Falling at your lotus feet, I beg to remain

Your eternal, insignificant servant,  
Keśava Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony.

When I was a young man, even before I joined ISKCON, I had a strong desire to devote myself to mundane social welfare work by serving my society and country. By your mercy, I can dovetail my propensity to serve society by serving the society of devotees. Now, I can serve the “world of Hare Kṛṣṇa.”

The Ministry for Social Development Information has been formed and promises to serve ISKCON devotees by helping them to get better situated so that their social, emotional, and spiritual needs can be met. The citizens of your society are Vaiṣṇavas—servants and lovers of Kṛṣṇa. I am pleased and honored that you have given me the opportunity to serve them.

The 1998 GBC resolution reads as follows:

THAT the Ministry for Social Development Information is established. The purpose of this Ministry is to: (a) help with the development of other ministries in the Social Circle, (b) actively pursue improved communications between ISKCON and its members, (c) facilitate communications among ministries on matters of social development, (d) propagate understanding of social principles among devotees by publications, seminars and visiting devotee communities, (e) identify competent devotees to head various projects for social development, engage in fund-raising for it and the ministries it represents.…Lokanātha Swami is appointed the Minister.

Thank you for this wonderful opportunity. Social development within ISKCON, along with Padayātrā Worldwide, is sufficient to keep me busy for the rest of my life.

While referring to *varṇāśrama* development within ISKCON, one of the last things you said, during your last days, was “Fifty percent of my work is still unfinished.”

I would like to join a dedicated team of devotees working toward the goal of finishing that 50% of your work. To bring your cherished dream of a socially and spiritually healthy ISKCON (*daivī-varṇāśrama*) society to reality, we are preparing to get fully into action. We want to implement your *daivī-varṇāśrama* concept as much as it is applicable in different places and circumstances. My life’s ambition is to fulfill your dream in this regard.

You condemned mundane family, friendship, and love, speaking very heavily on this subject. I wonder whether devotees have not gone too far by condemning not only their previous mundane relationships but also by minimizing their present-day spiritual relationships and situations with the members of your spiritual world-wide family. As a result, some ISKCON devotees seem to think it is unnecessary or *māyā* to take care of their nuclear families and cultivate spiritual friendship and love for Vaiṣṇavas. To renounce or neglect things connected with Kṛṣṇa and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is *phalgu-vairāgya,* false renunciation.

“Your love for me will be shown by how you co-operate to keep this institution together.” This is one of your most famous and significant statements. Social development or *varṇāśrama* development is all about cooperation. We cannot make any progress without the highest degree of co-operation.

Unfortunately, the proper vision and understanding has not yet permeated our Society. As a result, the essential co-operation to bring about a complete *varṇāśrama* society, as you envisioned, has been undermined. Please give us the intelligence, patience, and compassion to overcome these obstacles to bring a healthy state to your Society.

If we understand that ISKCON is your body (as you said it is), we will realize that by serving ISKCON we render you *vapu-seva*. Those who had no opportunity to serve you in your physical presence can serve the ISKCON body that you conceived and gave birth to in New York in 1966. By trying to develop *daivī-varṇāśrama* in ISKCON, we will serve different parts of the body of ISKCON and different aspects of that body. Practically everyone will have to adjust, reorganizing his or her situation so that ultimately the entire ISKCON body becomes healthy and rejuvenated. *Varṇāśrama* is an opportunity for everyone—young and old, disciples and grand-disciples—to benefit and advance by rendering *vapu-seva* to ISKCON—your body.

I would also like to take the opportunity to beg forgiveness for all the offenses committed by my lowly self. Please don’t kick me away. I humbly beg you to make me the servant of your servants. Whether I am honored or whether I am crushed under your lotus feet, you are my master, unconditionally. I know no one else but you as my master. If you want to punish me, please do that by making me do menial service to your lotus feet as well as those of your servants.

Thank you for engaging me.

The servant of your servants,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances! All Glories to your divine lotus feet!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Yesterday was Rāma-navamī and the historic grand opening of the “Glory of India Vedic Cultural Centre” by the Indian Prime Minister, Atul Behari Vajpayee. Your beloved Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Pārthasārathi at last were moved to Their new palatial home on the rocky hilltop overlooking Delhi. Śrī Śrī Sītā-Rāma, Hanumān, and Lakṣmaṇa were also installed, and now, with Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi also present, there are three attractive altars. Many of our *sannyāsīs* and other devotees took part in the *abhiṣeka* ceremony. The octagonal temple room is spacious and airy and houses about 1,000 persons.

The Ambassadors of Russia, USA, and Croatia were struggling to get in when the Prime Minister’s security forces clamped down before he had *darśana* of the Deities. Later the Prime Minister watched moving robotic forms of Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna speaking the *Bhagavad-gītā* accompanied by a film of the Universal Form. The opening, featuring the Prime Minister’s speech, was widely publicized through print and electronic media. The Prime Minister said many positive things, praising ISKCON and your work of translating and distributing the *Bhagavad-gītā* globally. He also read directly from your Introduction to your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is:*

In this world men are not meant for quarreling like cats and dogs. Men must be intelligent to realize the importance of human life and refuse to act like ordinary animals. A human being should realize the aim of his life, and this direction is given in all Vedic literatures, and the essence is given in *Bhagavad-gītā.* Vedic literature is meant for human beings, not for animals. Animals can kill other living animals, and there is no question of sin on their part, but if a man kills an animal for the satisfaction of his uncontrolled taste, he must be responsible for breaking the laws of nature.…All of this is clearly described, and if we properly utilize the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā,* then our whole life will become purified, and ultimately we will be able to reach the destination which is beyond this material sky.

In the temple room and elsewhere are beautiful paintings of *kṛṣṇa-līlā,* some of which seem to surpass paintings found in any of your publications so far. These are done by your Russian grandchildren artists in Vṛndāvana. I will describe for you one long five-sectioned painting from left to right, as follows:

Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma feed the monkeys yogurt in the pantry but are being spied on by Mother Yaśodā, who carries a stick. Balarāma is just noticing Mother Yaśodā’s foot by the pillar and has realized They are caught red-handed, but Kṛṣṇa is oblivious to the danger. Some monkeys beg humbly for yogurt while others show aggression. The monkeys are so realistic that they seem to have come straight from Vṛndāvana.

In a new section, Mother Yaśodā is offering Kṛṣṇa many delicious-looking preparations in fine silver bowls and trays painted in great detail. Kṛṣṇa is hanging lovingly and playfully round her neck from behind her right shoulder, pretending He doesn’t want to eat. Mother Yaśodā, holding His arm, has her eyes half closed, feeling overwhelmed with love for her beloved child. In the far distance below and out of the window, cows and bulls look up longingly to try to see Kṛṣṇa taking His meal with Yaśodāmayī. Maybe they expect remnants. In the distance the blue Yamunā wends her way through very green Vraja-bhūmi.

In the next picture Nanda Mahārāja and Yaśodāmayī are on the Nandagrāma terraces and turrets, looking out to the forest and feeling intense separation from Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. They are calling out and wondering when They will return home with the cows and calves.

But the cowherd boys are laid back in the forest glade, which is complete with Govardhana mountain caves and a stream with jumping ecstatic fish. Casual country boy Kṛṣṇa is in a new, nonchalant pose, with His lotus feet on Balarāma’s lap, who massages Them. Another boy is fanning Them. Juicy fruits sit on nice green leaf plates beside Them. Peacocks and other birds fly, strut, and pose. Fatty Madhumaṅgala takes yet another sweetball while Kṛṣṇa pokes him with his flute, and other boys lounge, eating lunch or looking at Kṛṣṇa with love. Deer and other animals stand bewitched. The center is Kṛṣṇa.

In the last picture Kṛṣṇa pets a standing cow while a huge tiger sleeps nearby, stretched out peacefully. Lord Brahmā descends on his swan carrier, looking regretful, embarrassed, and a bit out of place.

It is very obvious to me that you have empowered your sincere disciples and grand-disciples, who have dedicated their lives to serving your mission to save the world through the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya. Since you left us in Vṛndāvana twenty-one years ago, the devotees in Delhi, inspired by His Holiness Gopāla Krishna Goswami, have gone on undeterred to accomplish this, their offering of love. Now they will go on even further to make the Glory of India project more and more effective and perfect. In the coming millennium the Indian Prime Minister may well be our daily visitor, taking shelter at you lotus feet.

But for us the real Glory of India, of course, is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have done such an unheard-of thing as to give Kṛṣṇa to the whole wide world. This year I also saw the grand opening of the majestic Bangalore temple. I also visited forty-two ISKCON centers and temples in Russia and saw your temples in Lithuania, Poland, the Czech Republic, Slovakia, Hungary, Slovenia, Croatia, and Bosnia. Everywhere I saw your enthusiastic followers—Prabhupādānugas. The only explanation for all this is that you are still forcefully preaching through your books and sincere followers. Therefore you are more than the Glory of India, or the glory of the world or universe. Even Kṛṣṇa Himself wants to glorify such a topmost devotee as you:

*sādhavo hṛdayaṁ mahyaṁ sādhūnāṁ hṛdayaṁ tv aham*  
*mad-anyat te na jānanti nāhaṁ tebhyo manāg api*

**“The pure devotee is always within the core of My heart, and I am always in the heart of the pure devotee. My devotees do not know anything else but Me, and I do not know anyone else but them” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*** **9.4.68).**

As for myself, on this your holy Vyāsa-pūjā day I can only once again try to sincerely beg forgiveness for all my uncontrolled lust, greed, and anger, which still emanate poisonously from my heart, which is still as hard as the stones under the Glory of India project. My only hope is to be engaged as a servant of a servant of your true devotees, so that one day I can be purified enough to be worthy of you.

I hope that by your and Kṛṣṇa’s grace I can now take renewed strength from last year’s preaching experiences and in this way try to dedicate myself and instill my disciples with renewed enthusiasm to help fulfill your glorious desires. I am also responsible for helping to develop the “Glory of Bihar” project in Patna and a wonderful project coming up on the Yamunā bank at Baluaghat, in your old home town of Prayag Rāj Dhām (Allahabad). There should also be a “Glory of Nepal” and many more.

But even more than empowering me to help manifest these glorious transcendental constructions meant to attract the conditioned souls, please somehow or other arrange that my stony, dirty heart become gloriously melted and purified once and for all so that it will manifest its true transcendental nature of love of Kṛṣṇa. In this way please let me, along with all your dear family, be sacrificed by you to help create a flood of love of God that will sweep the whole world so that all the miserable living entities become happy. Let me in this way play a little part in helping form the glorious spiritual cultural house for the whole world to live in. Then you will be happy too. “Then the Vaiṣṇava’s desire will be fulfilled.”

You have glorified Kṛṣṇa most in this world and are therefore the most dear to both Lord Nityānanda and Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.

I pray to be always engaged in service at you lotus feet.  
Mahāviṣṇu Swami

## Nityodita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, I am reflecting upon all your amazing devotional accomplishments, and my heart swells with transcendental pride. And when I hear of the incredible achievements of your devotees all over the world, I realize even more just how great you really are! To inspire such commitment and surrender in the hearts of the devotees, even after your disappearance, is another sign of your divine empowerment. Giving pure devotional service to the world is the only “business” of great devotees, and you have proved to be the greatest transcendental “businessman”!

On this most holy day, I am ashamed to admit that even after a quarter century of opportunities, I have not purchased much of your product. However, the depth of your mercy makes it possible for even the lowest person to find complete satisfaction in his heart by rendering even the most menial service in your ISKCON movement.

In July 1969 you wrote to Hayagrīva Prabhu about the challenges of managing New Vrindavana: “Regarding the usefulness or uselessness of the inmates, it all depends on proper management. You can’t expect that all men and women who will be going there will be very useful. You have to make them useful by your arrangement.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am one of those useless inmates, and I am begging you to please make me useful by your arrangement.

Hoping to become a humble servant of your servants’ servants,  
Nityodita Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda made many significant contributions to the world. But two which are especially prominent are his multifaceted instructions on spiritual topics, presented in the form of his books, lectures, conversations, and letters, and his spiritual movement and organization, ISKCON. Knowledge without practical application is dry speculation, while activities not based on solid philosophy are fanaticism. Śrīla Prabhupāda combined both philosophy and its practical application when he created ISKCON, a movement based on the *Vedas* and having as its goal Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Together Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions and the movement in which they can be practiced, ISKCON, make a perfect combination for spiritual progress. It is especially the duty of the leaders of ISKCON to clearly understand Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions and how to practically apply them so that ISKCON is appreciated as an incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda describes this incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.2.20):

Therefore Kṛṣṇa has taken birth or taken shelter within the womb of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Men of Kaṁsa’s class, therefore, are very much afraid and are busy trying to stop this movement, especially in the Western countries. One politician has remarked that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading like an epidemic and that if not checked immediately, within ten years it may capture governmental power. There is, of course, such potency in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. As stated by authorities (Cc. *Ādi* 17.22), *kali-kāle nāma-rūpe kṛṣṇa-avatāra:* in this age, Kṛṣṇa has appeared in the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading like wildfire all over the world, and it will go on doing so. Men who are like Kaṁsa are very much afraid of the movement’s progress and acceptance by the younger generation, but as Kṛṣṇa could not be killed by Kaṁsa, this movement cannot be checked by men of Kaṁsa’s class. The movement will go on increasing more and more, provided the leaders of the movement remain firmly Kṛṣṇa conscious by following the regulative principles and the primary activities of chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* regularly.

Since *bhakti* automatically manifests knowledge and detachment, people in general should appreciate the ISKCON movement as a model of spiritual enlightenment and purity. This may not easily manifest all at once, and therefore those praying for ISKCON’s success may have to be patient, but not complacent. True spiritual life can be uncovered and discovered if the members of ISKCON try to understand Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings by applying them practically in their lives. Then clear knowledge can be there without speculation, and devotional love without selfish motivations.

ISKCON should not be an ordinary religious institution; it is meant to be a mission to respiritualize the world by creating a cultural revolution dedicated to the art of using everything—theater, dance, art, music, and especially Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books—in the service of spreading Lord Kṛṣṇa’s glories. ISKCON must create a revolution for the actual benefit of the entire world. *Sarve sukhino bhavantu:* “Let everyone become happy.”

Prahlādānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

May the whole world join together in saying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, “Thank you for coming into this world and giving us Kṛṣṇa in His most merciful form: the Holy Name.”

Today, on your divine appearance day, I am meditating upon your greatest gift—the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*.

*nāma cintāmaṇiḥ kṛṣṇaś caitanya-rasa-vigrahaḥ*  
*pūrṇaḥ śuddho nitya-mukto ‘bhinnatvān nāma-nāminoḥ*

**“The Holy Name of Kṛṣṇa is transcendentally blissful. It bestows all spiritual benedictions, for it is Kṛṣṇa Himself, the reservoir of all pleasure. Kṛṣṇa’s name is complete, and it is the form of all transcendental mellows. It is not a material name under any condition, and it is no less powerful than Kṛṣṇa Himself. Since Kṛṣṇa’s name is not contaminated by the material qualities, and there is no question of its being involved with maya, Kṛṣṇa’s name is always liberated and spiritual; it is never conditioned by the laws of material nature. This is because the name of Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa Himself are identical” (*****Padma Purāṇa,*** **quoted in the** ***Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Madhya*** **17.133).**

One of my most vivid memories of you is when we visited you in your room in Paris in July 1972 to take your leave before returning to Germany. At first we could not see you—but then upon our second entrance we discovered you, sitting on your rocking chair in the shade of the opening door. Chanting with eyes closed, you were fully absorbed and appeared to be relishing the association of the Lord in His Holy Name.

Without disturbing, we quickly receded, silently closing the door behind us. It seemed to me that I had intruded. But I also felt blissful: We had discovered a great secret—your own secret, and the secret of your movement: the means and the goal! I’ll never forget.

Early in San Francisco you once told a difficult-to-handle man, “I have no other medicine. Please chant this Hare Kṛṣṇa. I have no other explanation. I have no other answer.” Little did your visitors know that you were offering them the Lord Himself:

“In this Age of Kali, the holy name of the Lord, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* is the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Simply by chanting the holy name, one associates with the Lord directly. Anyone who does this is certainly delivered” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 17.22).

Of all your instructions the instruction to chant our rounds without offenses is most important. That is where our individual and collective strength comes from. That is the secret.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, the waves of time roll over this spiritual son of yours—taking some of his vitality, health, illusions of youth, etc. But by Your Divine Grace a realization is slowly and sometimes painfully growing—almost like a valuable pearl inside an oyster: in the Holy Name is everything. There is only one need: to chant it offenselessly!

But unqualified as I am, I need to be infused with strength. Offenses are weakening me. My humble desire is this: Please call me back into your room—in Paris or wherever—to chant with you in your presence. I promise to not talk or disturb. With all the humility at my command, I appeal to you in the voice of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura: “Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. I am simply running behind you shouting ‘Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa.’”

Your most unqualified beggar son,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*aiṁ gurudevāya vidmahe kṛṣṇānandāya dhīmahe tan no guroḥ pracodayāt*

**“Let us try to understand the spiritual master, who is always in blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Let me meditate on him, being enthused as he enthuses us.”**

The Gāyatrī *mantra* provides us with a meditation on the spiritual master that may be applied to Śrīla Prabhupāda. There are actually two *mantras* to the spiritual master in the Gāyatrī. The first is *aiṁ gurave namaḥ:* “I make offer respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master.” This means we should approach Śrīla Prabhupāda not as an equal but as we would approach the Supreme Personality of Godhead, bowing down and offering obeisances. Śrīla Prabhupāda is actually a most intimate friend, but he should not be seen as an ordinary person.

The Gāyatrī *mantra* advises us to “meditate on him being enthused.” Once when he was commenting on the word enthusiasm (*utsāha*) as it is used in Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Upadeśāmṛta,* Śrīla Prabhupāda gave himself as an example. He said, “How could I have come to America at such an old age unless I was enthusiastic?” He was enthusiastic to come to America because his guru ordered him to come, and when for a whole year he got almost no response in America, Śrīla Prabhupāda remained patient, but still enthusiastic. That enthusiasm can be heard on tape recordings of Prabhupāda’s lectures on the Lower East Side, his voice often cracking with earnestness as he delivered the Kṛṣṇa conscious message.

Prabhupāda was so enthusiastic to write *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that he would rise at one in the morning to do so. His composing of his purports was the perfect example of combined patience and enthusiasm. As he said, “Little drops of water wear away the stone. In this way, I’ve written all these books.”

“Let us meditate,” the Gāyatrī *mantra* advises, and so we can continue on our own, or in the company of devotees, to think about and discuss the enthusiastic moods and activities of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda. Prabhupāda’s translation contains a particularly interesting phrase, “Let me meditate on him being enthused as he enthuses us.” A genuine disciple has become enthusiastic in devotional service, and he knows that this is due to the enthusiasm of his spiritual master.

We may meditate on how our enthusiastic spiritual master has transferred this *śakti* of *utsāha* to us. Prabhupāda said that the mother of his disciple Acyutānanda complained that her son used to lounge around the house all the time and was unwilling to go on errands. But since he had come to live with Swamiji, he was always enthusiastic to cook and run any errands his spiritual master requested. Śrīla Prabhupāda was also fond of a pamphlet produced by a Christian minister in Boston that made reference to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s enthusiastic disciples. The minister said that he met a Hare Kṛṣṇa devotee giving out literature in the neighborhood of the church. The minister said, “These are actually our boys, but previously they were never interested in going to church. Now they have become mad after God.” Śrīla Prabhupāda also said that it was only by the enthusiasm of his disciples that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was spreading around the world.

When we do not feel enthusiastic, let us meditate on the enthusiasm of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let it ignite within us our natural enthusiasm to serve Kṛṣṇa. This is *kṛṣṇa-ānanda,* which motivates all the thoughts and activities of a pure devotee. Whether he is grave and silent or dancing Lord Caitanya’s *kīrtana,* whether he is being honored by many followers or treated as an outcast, the pure Vaiṣṇava is always merged in enthusiasm and bliss. Let us meditate upon him being enthused just as he enthuses us.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! The dawn heralds the coming of the sun. It is dawning upon me that you are bringing me close to where I belong. You have done many great things—you have established Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the planet earth. But for me the most important thing is that you are awakening my dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In your address on the Govinda album you say that “Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is not an artificial imposition on the mind”—that it is the natural state of the living entity to be Kṛṣṇa conscious.
