As conditioned souls we are forgetful of our eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa. But with the torchlight of knowledge you have opened my eyes, given me some spiritual vision.

You have given me some understanding of the factual situation. You have done this by bestowing on me your mercy in the form of transcendental instructions. Your teachings are clear and convincing, but instructions alone are not enough, and therefore you have also given us the process by with we can realize that reality: *sādhana-bhakti,* practical devotional service. You have engaged us in the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* of preaching the glories of the Lord and His holy name.

That preaching can be done in a variety of ways. In one lecture you said that *saṅkīrtana* is of two kinds, indoor and outdoor. Outdoor *saṅkīrtana,* you explained, includes *harināma* and distribution of Kṛṣṇa conscious literature, and indoor *saṅkīrtana* includes preaching programs and Deity worship. At the time I heard this lecture I was engaged as a *pūjārī;* thus I felt very encouraged that Deity worship was also *saṅkīrtana*. You explained that it was *saṅkīrtana* because when the *pūjārī* decorates the Deity nicely, the devotees and guests who come before the Lord appreciate His beauty, and so the *pūjārī* is spreading the glories of the Lord. To me all this indicates that your vision is so broad that you’ve made a place for everyone in your movement. And your vision is also bold. You want to save the world and change the world by establishing a God-centered society.

You are concerned for the welfare of the whole human society. Actually, your concern is without limitation, for you care about the welfare of all living entities. And your concern is for the well-being of your disciples—that we develop our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your heart is filled with compassion; this is not just a theoretical or sentimental concept. Your compassion is demanding, for you want us to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and to preach.

Sometimes, when remembering you, I feel some pain due to your absence, but in that pain causes me to feel as if you are present. Thus in your presence I feel your absence. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for those moments.

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvanmanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27).**

You are not an ordinary man. You are as good and worshipable as Kṛṣṇa, thus in all your temples around the world your worship is performed daily. That keeps us spiritually alive. You are also worshipable because you are the sum total of all the demigods.

*yasyāsti bhaktir bhagavaty akiñcanā*  
*sarvair guṇais tatra samāsate surāḥ*

**“All the demigods and their exalted qualities, such as religion, knowledge, and renunciation, become manifest in the body of one who has developed unalloyed devotion for the Supreme personality of Godhead, Vāsudeva” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **5.18.12).**

In you all the demigods and their good qualities are manifest. One who worships you performs the best worship; indeed, all the three worlds are satisfied when you are worshiped, and more than that, the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is satisfied. So let me serve you and worship you to your full satisfaction, according to my best capacity.

Thus I offer my respectful obeisances unto you because you have opened my heart to Kṛṣṇa and annihilated so many doubts. Thus you have extinguished the fire of lust, anger, and greed and destroyed the poison of illusion, madness, and envy.

I beg you to let me serve you and your servants in truth, humility, and devotion. Let me do so while maintaining cleanliness, mercy, and renunciation, and may I do this for all eternity.

I beg to remain your humble, aspiring servant,  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

## Subhāga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

When I go out, I see people are becoming more and more unhappy; they are in distress; some are in great anxiety; some are in tension.

You came into our life, you brought hope, you loved us, you removed our distress, freed us from anxiety and tension.

You brought us the nectar of Vaikuṇṭha and showered it throughout the world.

Now everyone has begun chanting the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and Lord Nityānanda.

Still so much to do. This is your mercy. There are unlimited services.

If we truly love you without reservation, then we surrender to you absolutely. Then we can receive guidance, capture you within our heart, you will inspire us from within.

We will become Kṛṣṇa conscious. In this way we can offer you our best homage.

I am an ignorant, insignificant, fallen fool. When I will surrender completely to you and offer my best at your lotus feet?

Your fallen and foolish son,  
Subhāga Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your causeless mercy, you have given us the eyes to be able to see Kṛṣṇa’s nature and Māyā’s nature. You have made this difference clear for us, so we can now decide who we want to serve. This benediction empowers us with the most valuable, substantial thing, i.e., freedom. We are no longer slaves to our lower nature. Of course, this freedom implies the risk of misusing it, because the nature of love is always voluntary. However, by attracting us to your transcendental person you have made our choice easy. Serving Lord Kṛṣṇa by serving His pure representative, you, is natural for us.

Along with freedom you have also given us hope. I remember, as a young university student, sitting in class, listening to the beat of my heart and thinking that with each beat my life was becoming shorter, and that when I died everything would be finished. The materialist has such a hopeless future. But now, by your mercy, I have full hope that the future will be glorious and complete faith in the eternality of my person.

This faith, which is your next gift, is not some blind faith but is based on the most consistent, sublime philosophy, that presented in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. As we following the teachings enunciated therein, and your instructions, our faith grows stronger and we gradually become free from the demon of doubt. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this wonderful gift.

Another most valuable blessing that you have bestowed on us is saintly association. You have introduced us to so many *sādhus* on the pages of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. Who can count the benefits from such association? Also, the association of your disciples and grand-disciples makes us strive to improve ourselves. This is all your mercy.

Another blessing of yours is the opportunity to bestow charity. By your mercy, we are now in a position to be the greatest benefactors. Who can be more charitable then your servants? They can freely give all the gifts they have received from you, and by so doing they get more and more of these priceless gifts.

So on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we thank you from the core of our heart for giving us these wonderful gifts, and we request you to bless us with more and more enthusiasm for the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your less important servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I often think about how easy it is to find a topic when we want to praise Śrīla Prabhupāda. Everything about Śrīla Prabhupāda is glorious. His wit, his intelligence, his compassion—we all know there is no limit.

But this year I am caught up in the thought of how it is easy to miss the mark, easy to think that we can praise Śrīla Prabhupāda yet neglect his life’s work: his International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Can we say that we love Śrīla Prabhupāda but hate what he did? Have any of us ever known Śrīla Prabhupāda outside of ISKCON? In fact, whenever we take the time to praise Śrīla Prabhupāda for anything at all, we praise him for what he did in the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. We do not know anything else about him.

“An offering of fruits and flowers does not constitute worship,” Śrīla Prabhupāda once wrote. And we might add that a few words in a book do not constitute praise. To really praise Śrīla Prabhupāda, we must devote our lives to continuing his work. And that means ISKCON.

It is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON that is spreading the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa to every town and village in the world, and it is through Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON that we have received all the wonderful gifts we are thankful for.

Not everyone can write an article of praise for this Vyāsa-pūjā book, but everyone can offer the praise that Śrīla Prabhupāda likes best: a lifetime of service to ISKCON.

Sometimes I think about seeing Śrīla Prabhupāda again some day. I want to knock on his door and hear him say, “Yes? Come in.”

I want to prostrate myself and hear him say, “You want to see me?”

I may not be able to look up right away. As usual, I will feel embarrassed because I have done so many stupid things. But a disciple should offer some praise to the spiritual master, so if I can just manage to say, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I love ISKCON, I have served ISKCON, and I want to serve ISKCON forever,” then I am sure he will smile.

And that’s all I want. I have understood at least this much.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
All glories to ISKCON!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

It is a bitter cold night  
and the snow is drifting  
as I struggle to reach  
Rādhā-Dāmodara’s Maṇḍir.

Attaining my destination,  
I play a favorite recording—  
you are leading the *kīrtan*  
to glorify and please  
Their Lordships.

Nostalgia sets in  
and I cannot control it—  
a bitter-sweet remembrance  
of bygone times.

It is cold in the temple,  
so I start a fire,  
leaving the door open  
on the wood stove.

As the flames begin to dance  
they create heat and light,  
and a shadow behind Their Lordships  
begins to dance as well.

This scene fosters a  
fond remembrance  
of my beloved spiritual master  
who brought life and light  
into the temple of our hearts,

Making us dance  
like the flames in the fire.

From the mercy you gave,  
we have hope to see  
that the Lord is dancing  
to express His joy

In the *kīrtan*  
sung by you.

But now that you have gone away  
all the hope we have left  
is outlined by shadows.

We find that our love  
is a shadow attachment  
and our only light  
is a gift from you.

So I pray on this day  
when you appeared in this world  
to kindle the fire of devotion

and make our hearts blaze  
with a love ever growing  
until the shadows are dispelled  
and we see the Lord  
dancing with joy  
to the *kīrtan* you sing  
in His *saṅkīrtan* party.

As I gaze over the ruins  
of this morbid Western world  
and hear your voice calling from afar,

I feel your presence  
more than ever before,  
and this is your ultimate blessing.

In a place that is quiet, secret, and old,  
we reflect upon your pastimes,

and the pain of separation,  
growing even stronger,  
brings us near to you  
and closer to home.

I know you live eternally  
in Rādhā-Dāmodara Maṇḍir,  
singing for Their pleasure,  
awaiting our return.

In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
you have given us hope  
and taught us to love and trust.

You have shown us an inner warmth and light  
to reveal our role in life.

The mission you gave us makes life worth living,  
helps us face darkness, loneliness, and suffering  
with patience, gratitude, love, and joy.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servant,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsa-priya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you for allowing Kṛṣṇa’s divine grace to shine through you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how incredible it is that in the pious land of Bhāratavarṣa it has become so difficult to preach even about *yukta-vairāgya* according to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, what to speak of complete renunciation of material life. It seems that the Māyāvādīs have changed even the definition of renunciation. When a devotee really renounces by utilizing all resources for Lord Kṛṣṇa’s service, the Māyāvādīs deride the devotee and demand that all the resources be handed over to them! The real renunciant should simply renounce everything, and that’s it! This is their sole definition. Utility is the principle in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but according to the Māyāvādīs nonutility is true virtue, real renunciation. Because they are impersonalists, they do not recognize the Lord to be a person like them, and that is why they do not utilize resources in His service. Rather, they justify themselves being the recipient of the resources instead, for they are real persons! Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are *nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe,* I consider this attitude of Bhāratavarṣa-vāsī Māyāvādīs to be an outright rebellion against your desire, and therefore your devotees are at war with them.

Recently in Bharatpur I have been confronting some Māyāvādī sponsors on this issue. I put a lot of endeavor, time, and resources into spreading your glories there. Now I am leaving there after helping to save a few devotees. It was worth it. I believe that in Vraja Dhāma there is so much service to render for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace. My experiences in Bharatpur have invested me with the fighting spirit while remaining at your lotus feet. Hence I am enthused by your mercy.

I am at your service, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Vedavyāsa-priya Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**Thank You, Thank You, Thank You**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, who are the topmost swanlike devotee, delivering all the fallen conditioned souls in this dark Age of Kali.

Thank you, thank you, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As you know, I’m very fallen. Yet, despite my many shortcomings, by your mercy I’m trying to remain chaste and loyal to you. Thank you for giving me that opportunity and for not kicking me away.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to start the CHAKRA Web page to defend your movement from ISKCON’s critics. Devotees and friends with access to the World Wide Web now have a positive alternative source of ISKCON-friendly news to counter the confusing array of anti-ISKCON propaganda on the Internet.

Thank you for sending Umāpati Mahārāja and Mother Madhusūdanī Rādhā to help shoulder the burden of CHAKRA, and thank you for making our humble efforts be noticed and appreciated by devotees around the world, including the GBC Executive Committee and GBC members.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for showing me how to write every member on COM, sending each member the Independent Internet Newsletter. Through this e-mail newsletter we created further setbacks for ISKCON’s critics by gathering support from devotees who normally don’t have access to the World Wide Web.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to start Kṛṣṇa’s Internet Radio web page, where people all over the world with Internet access can now hear your lectures and *kīrtanas,* as well as lectures and *kīrtanas* of your disciples, coming right from the Internet through the speakers and headsets attached to their home computers. In this way we were able to help bring Kṛṣṇa conscious sound into thousands of homes around the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me the chance to serve and take care of some of your disciples and grand-disciples in Canada, New York, England, Wales, and Ireland last year.

Thank you for allowing me to continue managing the GBC web page. Through this service I am able to help the GBC body make themselves more accessible.

Thank you for allowing me to attend the GBC meetings every year and participate in some small way, though I am not a member of the GBC.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to travel to Māyāpur, Vṛndāvana, Jagannātha Purī, and Bombay last year. Thank you for allowing me to travel to dozens of other cities as well, meeting hundreds of devotees on several continents. Thank you for giving me *prasādam* to eat and a place to rest wherever I went, and thank you for giving me intelligence to speak from your books.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for suddenly giving me a big service this year. It wasn’t what I expected, but I am honored that I am now serving as the temple president at your Bhaktivedanta Manor. I think I would not have received this service had it not met with your approval, since Bhaktivedanta Manor is obviously very dear to you. This task requires expertise, intelligence, and wisdom, yet you know very well that I am inexperienced, foolish, and dull-headed. Where will I get the intelligence and wisdom I need to help manage the Manor, Śrīla Prabhupāda, if not from you? Please, therefore, help me serve you. You arranged for me to take this service. Please also help me do it nicely for you.

Last, but not least, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being all that you are to me and the other devotees. You are the greatest, unrivaled spiritual master in the modern world. There is no doubt about it. Thank you for giving me the opportunity to meet you and become your disciple. Thank you for engaging me and encouraging me in the past, and thank you for increasing my attachment to you. Thank you for extending your mercy for future generations as well. Thank you for starting ISKCON.

No one could ever properly glorify you or thank you enough for the treasures you have brought us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All we would ever be able to say is simply this: Thank you, thank you, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and thank you yet again and again.

Your servant,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Abentheuer

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

Although most of us here did not get a chance to see you in your *vapu* form, we feel that you are in the center of our community and our hearts.

We not only want to thank you eternally for what you gave us, but also for what you took away from us—namely illusion, wrong conceptions of life, and material goals. How could we have gotten free of pride and attachment on our own?

This year we are trying to meditate on your unlimited good qualities and follow your perfect example in all aspects of life, as far as we are able.

People in the material society are always hankering for good leadership, but especially in your ISKCON society we are all looking for strong leadership, divine leadership.

Your example of how to preach, how to lead, how to organize, how to serve, how to worship, how to help, how to behave, how to cook, and how to be merciful is complete is completely transcendental.

Your example and teachings guide us in life and give us hope in our otherwise hopeless condition. Unfortunately, due to our fallen position we often let the clouds of material desire cover your shining example. But you have sent your empowered disciples to help us push the clouds aside. You have trained them by your words and your example, showing how a single pure and dedicated person can be empowered by the Lord to save the whole world.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the blessing to never forget your transcendental qualities so that we can sincerely try to follow your example in all conditions of life.

Your servants in Abentheuer, Germany.

## Adelaide

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories unto Your Divine Grace, the savior of the fallen.

Another year has past, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and it is again time for us to deeply reflect upon what we have achieved in your service since your last Vyāsa-pūjā. A lot of internal struggle and purification have taken place amongst us, and we feel ashamed that we have wasted so much time engaged in sense gratification, when we could have been preaching instead. But still you have said failure is the pillar of success, so we pray we do not fall back into the same trap this year, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but rather offer you a year of successful preaching activities.

Your Divine Grace has been immensely patient with us. Although we have behaved in a nonsensical manner, you did not withdraw your mercy from us. Rather, as a caring grandfather, you arranged for us to realize that your service is our real shelter, and you have given us another opportunity to serve you. Thank you, grandfather.

You are answering our prayers, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please fulfill our spiritual desires and burn our material desires to ashes. We desire to one day become your pure servants, and we know that you will make this come true, because you love us.

Your aspiring servants in Adelaide, Australia.

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to the glorious disciplic succession that you so profoundly represent cent percent. You always explained to us that the idea of disciplic succession means the chain of perfectly liberated souls, *mahā-bhāgavatas,* delivering pure uncontaminated knowledge of the Absolute Truth from generation to generation for eternity. That is the *Bhāgavata* chain as envisaged and presented by your illustrious Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. He was indeed a revolutionary philosopher, who could not tolerate even the slightest deviation in the *siddhānta* of *bhakti* as given by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. Therefore he is addressed as *mādhuryojjvala-premāḍhya-śrī-rūpānuga-bhaktida,* meaning “one who is absorbed in and who delivers devotional service that is enriched with the most brilliant mellow of conjugal love of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and that follows exactly in the line of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī.” Also, he was known as *rūpānuga-viruddhāpasiddhānta-dhvānta-hāriṇe,* or “one who destroys the darkness of *apasiddhānta* created by principles opposed to the *rūpānuga* principles.” Indeed Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī disturbed so many vested interest groups in the Vaiṣṇava line to uphold the pure teachings of the *ācāryas*. He especially disturbed people by presenting a disciplic succession that excluded direct initiators of the great *ācāryas* such as Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla Gaura-Kiśora dāsa Bābājī. The Vṛndāvana caste Gosvāmīs closed the doors of their temples when he visited them, and they were ready to fight with him. Even his own brother Lalitaprasad was critical. But as you once said, “Our Guru Mahārāja’s brother, he criticizes Guru Mahārāja. But what has \[the brother\] done for Kṛṣṇa? And what my Guru Mahārāja has done? Unless my Guru Mahārāja had produced me, how this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is going all over the world?”

Spiritual life is indeed like a razor’s edge. You were so protective and careful that you didn’t want your followers to go even to other Vaiṣṇavas. You said, “If they say one thing different from what I am saying, it will create havoc.” We have recently seen a Sanskrit-scholar follower of yours who associated with a so-called learned Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava scholar. This follower left your mission, claiming that because Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta is not properly connected through *dīkṣā* his line is not bona fide. What an ungrateful fool! Even though you picked him up from rolling in the stool of material life in the Western countries, he thought some bookworm who never understood the first lesson of the missionary preaching activities of Lord Caitanya can pass judgment on the two most powerful *ācāryas* in the history of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism, namely Your Divine Grace and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta. Such a person is certainly lost forever. *Yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi:* “Without the grace of such a spiritual master, one cannot make any progress.”

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 25.264–66, 76) the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is described perfectly as follows:

*jīva nistārite prabhu bhramilā deśe-deśe*  
*āpane āsvādi’ bhakti karilā prakāśe*

**“To deliver all fallen souls, the Lord traveled from country to country. He personally tasted the transcendental pleasure of devotional service, and He simultaneously spread the cult of devotion everywhere.**

*kṛṣṇa-tattva, bhakti-tattva, prema-tattva sāra*  
*bhāva-tattva, rasa-tattva, līlā-tattva āra*

**“Kṛṣṇa consciousness means understanding the truth of Kṛṣṇa, the truth of devotional service, the truth of love of Godhead, the truth of emotional ecstasy, the truth of transcendental mellow, and the truth of the pastimes of the Lord.**

*śrī-bhāgavata-tattva-rasa karilā pracāre*  
*kṛṣṇa-tulya bhāgavata, jānāilā saṁsāre*

**“Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has personally preached the transcendental truths and mellows of** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****.** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **and the Supreme Personality of Godhead are identical, for** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **is the sound incarnation of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”**

*ei amṛta anukṣaṇa, sādhu mahānta-megha-gaṇa,*  
*viśvodyāne kare variṣaṇa*  
*tāte phale amṛta-phala, bhakta khāya nirantara,*  
*tāra śeṣe jīye jaga-jana*

**“The devotees who have taken shelter of the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu take the responsibility for distributing nectarean devotional service all over the world. They are like clouds pouring water on the ground that nourishes the fruit of love of Godhead in this world. The devotees eat that fruit to their hearts’ content, and whatever remnants they leave are eaten by the general populace. Thus they live happily.”**

You took the pains to go to the West at the risk of your very life just to glorify Kṛṣṇa. Therefore you are the only one who knows *kṛṣṇa-tattva, bhakti-tattva, prema-tattva, bhāva-tattva, rasa-tattva,* and *līlā-tattva*. You prayed to Kṛṣṇa, “My dear Lord, I do not know why you have brought me to this land of *ugra-karma*. These people are all covered with the modes of passion and ignorance. So how will they become attracted to *vāsudeva-kathā*? But you have brought me here. So there must be some purpose.” You are certainly an instrument in the hands of Kṛṣṇa and the disciplic succession. So if one thinks you to be ordinary, what kind of Vaiṣṇava is he? Just see the bewildering and deluding potency of Māyā! Even after spending fifteen years in your mission, even after seeing how you delivered the world, someone can become so totally bewildered. Once we asked you about the most famous writer of *rāma-līlā* from North India. What was wrong with him?, we asked. Why isn’t he considered bona fide? And you said that your Guru Mahārāja said that this writer proudly claimed to belong to the brahminical line of Bhṛgu Muni, and that a real Vaiṣṇava would never want to belong to the line of Bhṛgu because he kicked Lord Viṣṇu in the chest. This was the most astounding experience in our life, and it made us realize how dangerous it is to deviate even the slightest from the *siddhānta*.

You attribute all the credit for your success to firm faith in the words of your spiritual master. That is why we beg you on this auspicious day to give us a drop of the same faith you have in the pure preceptorial line. As time passes, the world is led more and more by blind leaders without any character or knowledge. The brahminical culture is totally lost, and no one wants to give even lip service to the mission of the Lord. The Lord appears in order to establish *dharma* in its entirety, from the basics to the highest. Only a very fortunate person can understand this and become an assistant in His mission. After seeing so many deviations within and without our own ISKCON family, we now know the true meaning of *kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*. Without the power of Kṛṣṇa, no one can substantially preach His mission. One must take shelter of a cent percent pure devotee, a transparent via medium. All the scriptures say the same thing. Mucukunda says,

*bhavāpavargo bhramato yadā bhavej*  
*janasya tarhy acyuta sat-samāgamaḥ*  
*sat-saṅgamo yarhi tadaiva sad-gatau*  
*parāvareśe tvayi jāyate matiḥ*

**“The material life of a wandering soul ceases, O Acyuta, when he attains the association of Your devotees. For when he associates with them, there awakens in him devotion unto You, who are the goal of the devotees and the Lord of all causes and their effects” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.51.53).**

Jaḍa Bhārata says,

*rahūgaṇaitat tapasā na yāti*  
*na cejyayā nirvapaṇād gṛhād vā*  
*na cchandasā naiva jalāgni-sūryair*  
*vinā mahat-pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekam*

**“My dear King Rahūgaṇa, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth simply by observing celibacy, strictly following the rules and regulations of householder life, leaving home as a** ***vānaprastha,*** **accepting** ***sannyāsa,*** **or undergoing severe penances in winter by keeping oneself submerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer by fire and the scorching heat of the sun. There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee.”**

And Prahlāda Mahārāja says,

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your purports to the *Upadeśāmṛta* (Text 5) you have so kindly explained that one should carefully take shelter of a pure devotee (*uttama-adhikārī*) only, and you explain what the qualifications of the *uttama-adhikārī* are:

When a person realizes himself to be an eternal servitor of Kṛṣṇa, he loses interest in everything but Kṛṣṇa’s service. Always thinking of Kṛṣṇa, devising means by which to spread the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, he understands that his only business is in spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Such a person is to be recognized as an *uttama-adhikārī,* and his association should be immediately accepted according to the six processes (*dadāti pratigṛhṇāti,* etc.). Indeed, the advanced *uttama-adhikārī* Vaiṣṇava devotee should be accepted as a spiritual master. Everything one possesses should be offered to him, for it is enjoined that one should deliver whatever he has to the spiritual master. The *brahmacārī* in particular is supposed to beg alms from others and offer them to the spiritual master. However, one should not imitate the behavior of an advanced devotee or *mahā-bhāgavata* without being self-realized, for by such imitation one will eventually become degraded.

In this verse Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī advises the devotee to be intelligent enough to distinguish between the *kaniṣṭha-adhikārī, madhyama-adhikārī* and *uttama-adhikārī*. The devotee should also know his own position and should not try to imitate a devotee situated on a higher platform. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has given some practical hints to the effect that an *uttama-adhikārī* Vaiṣṇava can be recognized by his ability to convert many fallen souls to Vaiṣṇavism. One should not become a spiritual master unless he has attained the platform of *uttama-adhikārī*. A neophyte Vaiṣṇava or a Vaiṣṇava situated on the intermediate platform can also accept disciples, but such disciples must be on the same platform, and it should be understood that they cannot advance very well toward the ultimate goal of life under his insufficient guidance. Therefore a disciple should be careful to accept an *uttama-adhikārī* as a spiritual master.

Here you have warned us not to imitate the behavior of an *uttama-adhikārī*. While explaining the failure of the Gauḍīya Maṭha, you said,

Why this Gauḍīya Maṭha failed? Because they tried to become more than guru. He, before passing away, he gave all direction and never said that “This man should be the next *ācārya*.” But these people, just after his passing away they began to fight, who shall be *ācārya*. That is the failure. They never thought, “Why Guru Mahārāja gave us instruction so many things, why he did not say that this man should be *ācārya*?” They wanted to create artificially somebody *ācārya* and everything failed. They did not consider even with common sense that if Guru Mahārāja wanted to appoint somebody as *ācārya,* why did he not say? He said so many things, and this point he missed? The real point? And they insist upon it. They declared some unfit person to become *ācārya*. Then another man came, then another, *ācārya,* another *ācārya*. So better remain a foolish person perpetually to be directed by Guru Mahārāja. That is perfection. And as soon as he learns the Guru Mahārāja is dead, “Now I am so advanced that I can kill my guru and I become guru.” Then he’s finished.

One thing is very clear. If a conditioned soul takes up an absolute position, or if one conditioned soul gives an absolute position to another conditioned soul and worships him accordingly, immediately a deviant *apasampradāya* is born. That is why the world is full of different kinds of pseudo religions. Therefore, as you mentioned above, it is best to remain a perpetual fool at the feet of a *śuddha-bhakta* such as Your Divine Grace.

Your movement is the only hope for suffering humanity and the only representative of brahminical culture in this world. But we have a thankless task of living in the middle of Kali-yuga. Just as Kali began his notorious influence by polluting the mind of a *brāhmaṇa* boy, Śṛṅgī, Kali is constantly trying to attack the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya by polluting the minds of the *brāhmaṇas,* practically the only *brāhmaṇas* of the world. Please protect us from this constant attack of Kali and keep us in the shade of your lotus feet.

We pray at your feet on this auspicious day that we may always remember the infinite mercy you have bestowed on us, that we may always remember you as an eternally liberated *siddha-puruṣa,* much beyond our materially afflicted brain, and that we may introduce all the conditioned souls to you through your books, your words, and your wonderful pastimes and thus ensure their firm connection to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at ISKCON Ahmedabad, India.

## Austin

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The modern world has never seen a spiritual master such as you. You have single-handedly launched a powerful spiritual movement that is revolutionizing the entire universe. There are no more fortunate persons than those who have dedicated their lives to assisting you in your mission.

You were especially deputed by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself to come to this material world to manifest your divine presence and write your books. Even though they are printed on ordinary paper with ink composed of material elements, by your sacred potency they are extraordinarily transcendental and are completely transforming this material world. Anyone who comes in contact with them experiences an amazing metamorphosis in their lives. Even though this may not immediately manifest overtly, the effect is nonetheless there, and the transition from material consciousness to spiritual consciousness in the hearts of those contacted has indeed begun.

Even though you are attracting persons into your movement from the material society, by your transcendental *śakti* they are being transformed into spiritually surcharged representatives of the Brahma-sampradāya. And when they in turn take up preaching on your behalf, we see that, amazingly enough, the people that they draw into the network of your loving mercy also become spiritually surcharged to spread the movement even further.

Your amazing potency reminds us how Lord Caitanya would walk down the road in South India. Anyone who saw Him would become transformed with love of God and would immediately join Him in walking down the road. Then upon reaching a junction Lord Caitanya would send these new devotees down a different road. Anyone who saw them would immediately become inundated with pure love of God and then would join them in walking down that road. Upon reaching a junction they in turn would be sent down a different road. This astounding divine energy of Lord Caitanya simply went on expanding more and more until all of South India become overflooded with *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. In this way Lord Caitanya miraculously spread His *saṅkīrtana* movement.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even without your physical presence you are still spreading your movement like wildfire all over world, both in ways that we can recognize and also in ways that are unseen. In spite of all the critics, you are still manifest on this planet as ISKCON, which you said was your body. The ripples of the waves of your preaching between the years 1966 and 1977 are still being felt all over the universe. Instead of weakening over time, as material waves do, the waves from your preaching are becoming stronger and stronger all the time. In this way, gradually by your merciful potency the entire universe will become completely immersed in an ocean of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the great spreader of Lord Caitanya’s movement! And all glories to your followers—disciples, grand-disciples, and the future great-grand-disciples, great-great-grand-disciples, etc., etc.—whom you are engaging and will be engaging as your instruments to go on defeating this nonsensical Age of Kali more and more.

Your servants at the Hare Krishna Center in Austin, Texas.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa adhikārī)

## Austria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Because you appeared on this planet—the planet of death—to spread the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it is just a question of time before the holy names of the Lord are to be heard in every town and village. Without you, we would have remained eternally in the cycle of birth and death. Life after life we would have chewed the chewed, and, frustrated in low spirits, we would have lived a sinful life, stamped by the reactions forced upon us.

Because we thought we were qualified competitors of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we immediately got victimized by the illusory energy and sank into the deepest regions of material existence. Here we are now, looking for something to hold on to, some relief, love. Every action of the living entity is a cry for love. But only someone like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who possess real love, transcendental love, can *give* it to others. You are like a cloud, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and because of your compassion for the conditioned souls, who are scorched by the forest fire of material existence, your causeless, endless rain of mercy flows down from you in the form of your transcendental literatures, directly into the ears and sight, without exception. These books are the juice of life for human beings and represent the essence of your love for us.

Quarrel and hypocrisy are characteristics for the Age of Kali. Where there is quarrel, there is loss of intelligence. Therefore out of your generosity you taught by personal example the greatness of a Vaiṣṇava, a worshiper of Śrī Viṣṇu. You are undoubtedly the embodiment of all good qualities. You unreservedly took shelter under the lotus feet of the supreme authority, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the most obedient confidential servitor of the Lord, being equal to Him in quality. How can I adequately praise you? My praise is like an offering of Gaṅgā water to mother Gaṅgā. Whatever good qualities we may have are all reflections of the rays of your divine qualities and those of your representatives. My lack of realization is a hindrance in trying to understand your inconceivable glories. Many, many devotees know you better, for I’m only an insignificant grandson of Your Divine Grace. We beg to be allowed to always remain at the shelter of your divine lotus feet.

Your aspiring grandsons and granddaughters in Austria.

## Baku

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet.

Our dear grandfather, this is the first time we are writing an offering for your Vyāsa-pūjā book. Somehow, previously we could not do it. But this time we felt the urge to send our humble offering of admiration and gratitude.

Before the movement started by Your Divine Grace was brought to our country by your missionary followers, there was not a single person in this country who could imagine that this movement could have any success here; no one thought anyone here could be serious about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because Āryan life and its goals, worldview, traditions, philosophy, eating style, and everything else associated with it were all very alien to the people here.

But when people got in touch with the things given by Your Divine Grace, they started to change. The practices of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you so expertly introduced are so sublime and powerful. They touched people’s hearts and made their spiritual identities respond. You found the ways to the hearts of people through their stomachs, tongues, ears, eyes, and intelligence by introducing *bhāgavata-prasādam,* which they had never tasted before, philosophy that has no equal, ecstatic *kīrtanas,* etc. These experiences have changed people’s minds and hearts and convinced them of the truth of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Within only ten years not only has almost the whole country gotten to know about your movement, but most people have become favorable toward it. The government has accepted your Society as a serious organization, and the people have accepted it as an exemplary institution.

In our country, festivals introduced by Your Divine Grace have become popular and are gradually acquiring a traditional character. Festivals like Gaura-pūrṇimā, Rāma-navamī, Śrī Nṛsiṁha-caturdaśī, Janmāṣṭamī, the appearance day of Your Divine Grace, Śrī Rādhāṣṭamī, Śrī Govardhana-pūjā, and many others are now eagerly awaited events for hundreds of families, including the elderly and youngsters. At least five hundred people participate in each of them, and on Janmāṣṭamī the number swells to seven hundred.

On festival days the Deities are dressed in newly sewn outfits and decorated with crowns, ornaments, and garlands, and flowers are place all around. The temple room and temple yard are decorated with flags and pictures, the devotee men dress in *dhotīs,* and the ladies dress up in colorful saris and decorate themselves Vedic style. All this creates a festive mood.

Traditional Vedic dance and music performances, dramatic and humorous theater shows, a three-hour *kīrtana,* and a *mahā* feast make people extremely happy. They feel very free and joyful. After festivals people become so much transcendentally intoxicated that they don’t want to return to their homes. At the end, out of gratitude they simply go on chanting “Jaya Prabhupāda! Jaya Prabhupāda!”

In this way you are succeeding in bringing us into the divine realm and making us deathless.

The people here have a great need to establish firm and positive relationships among themselves. Enmity between people has become a big problem and gives them great pain. They are suffering not so much from external inconveniences but from constant conflicts. Even religious principles have failed to control the hatred, lust, greed, and anger within human society and to normalize personal relationships. There is a great need to connect people with each other. But how?

You and your movement have become such a connector. All people in the world can be connected through your Personality and on the basis of your mission. Your qualities, your instructions, and your heritage in the form of the many practical ways you have given us to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa leave no place for hatred in our lives, and they bind us in affection to you and around you. In this way peace and mutual respect can be established in human society.

One devotee’s father said that among all the prophets your special blessing is that you have left behind so many sincere followers. These sincere followers of yours are continuing your mission with great enthusiasm and force, having been enlivened by you. By our good fortune we have gotten your beloved disciples as our spiritual masters and GBC’s. Their visits to our *yatra* are turning unto true festivals. Association with them gives us access to the divine taste and deep realizations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness with which you have inculcated them.

For all eternity we owe you an unpayable debt of gratitude, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for everything you have given us, for everything you have done for us. Thank you again and again.

Your grateful grandsons and granddaughters in the Baku yatra, in Azerbaijan.

## Bali

**The Spiritual Master in the Kali-yuga**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I don’t have the ability to be successful,  
Yet if you so desire, you can change my materialistic consciousness.

You have seen myself, who am powerless.  
Then you came to give me a blessing.

Please, help me control my senses  
and save me from material activity.

You are the leader among spiritual masters.  
No one else can do what you have done.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The devotees of ISKCON Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Vidura dāsa and Bhāgavatī-devī dāsī)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, O great savior of the fallen souls. All glories to you and your divine mission, which we have been entrusted to carry out!

Though there are many holy days to inspire us, today is a day like no other. This anniversary day of your appearance in this world also reminds us of your appearance into our lives, as well as who we have become because of you. Though we follow the *bhakti* path imperfectly, somehow we are in Kṛṣṇa’s loving network. How amazing! Amazing grace! If you had not compassionately, valiantly, and determinedly come to the West, where would we be today? Upon what would the foundation of our lives rest? What standard would we use to evaluate the value or use of anything? Where would our consciousness be and in what direction would we be going? After our body’s demise, what would be our ultimate destination—the sum total of our life’s endeavor and thought? We can only shudder to think!

For many of us, overcome as we were with miseries, you were our last hope. The only other option was death—even nonexistence seemed better then living a pointless life. You literally saved us from being lost due to the desperation and frustration of this Kali age! How important it is to remind ourselves of this fact! O Prabhupāda, please help us remember the unhappiness of our life without Kṛṣṇa so we can always feel grateful and humble, and so we can appreciate how you gave us new life, real love, spiritual light, eternal wisdom, The true goal, and our eternal service! We are in your eternal debt for saving us! Let all the devotees join together today to tell their stories, and in loving friendship let us encourage one another to go forward to serve you and Lord Kṛṣṇa as we are able. Let us come together and pray for each other, for our families, our temples, all the leaders, for whoever we feel has offended us or whomever we have offended, for ISKCON, and for the whole planet. Let us look at our successes and areas that we need to work on.

On this anniversary of your appearance, we pray that you will be pleased by our offering to you as we endeavor to call for unity and love between all the devotees despite differences. We do this by attempting to look at ways we push each other away and are diverted from Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We pray that by looking at ourselves in problem areas, your Society can be truly united and function in the broadminded and loving capacity you intended. We pray for the intelligence to do this, and pray that our endeavor will be welcomed by you, as well as by all the devotees, and that it will be taken in the spirit of service to you and for creating unity among the devotees.

O Prabhupāda, you teach us what unity is in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

Disunity between individual souls is so strong within this material world that even in a society of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, members sometimes appear disunited due to their having different opinions and leaning toward material things. Actually, in Kṛṣṇa consciousness there cannot be two opinions. There is only one goal: to serve Kṛṣṇa to one’s best ability. If there is some disagreement over service, such disagreement is to be taken as spiritual. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.30.8, purport\]

Let all the devotees help each other advance and realize unity through service to Śrī Guru and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! O Gurudeva, we don’t want to offend but rather to “upend” whatever keeps us from our mission of advancing in and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Charity does begin at home. If we are to influence the world, we need to have our own house in order and live what we preach. Dear devotee physicians, let us heal ourselves. That is our real service and offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let each of us purge ourselves of whatever keeps us away from Kṛṣṇa and whatever mentalities cause us to offend and criticize the Vaiṣṇavas. The cure for the ills of any society always begins with each individual.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, when our brothers or sisters stumble, fall, or become emotionally wounded, let us have the compassion and love to try to understand their position and reach out our hand to help them. The worst thing is to ignore or belittle. That almost insures they will stay casualties.

Our experience is that as soon as we criticize or condemn, we put “them” outside our heart and our compassion, pushing them away. “They” become an object, divorced from us, and we lose our sense of being connected. We are all brothers and sisters of the same family of Kṛṣṇa. One of your stated purposes of ISKCON, dear spiritual father, is to bring the members closer to Kṛṣṇa and each other. Please bless us with the desire and will to accomplish this.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us realize that karmically what we do to others we truly do to ourselves. It is Kṛṣṇa’s perfect law that we will be on the receiving end of every thought and deed we give out. Therefore please bless us to be very careful how we deal with others, looking first at the condition of our own heart and consciousness before we speak or act. After all, if I “have a problem” with someone, the difficulty is really with me and how I respond. The problems we have in our relationships with others—the way we react to them—teach us volumes about what we need to work on to advance or what needs to be purified or “healed” within us.

Our relationship problems can be compared to the coming together of rough stones in a river. The friction smoothes and polishes the stones. Actually, our conflicts with others may help us the most to advance. By your mercy, dear Prabhupāda, let us welcome them as a wake-up call exposing our shortcomings and humbling us. Please let us appreciate the importance of devotee association and other devotees’ difficulties. Where would we be without devotees? How could we live, and who would we go to for shelter?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us understand that seeing anyone only externally is dangerous, since the true position of the person’s heart may not be visible. Certainly you don’t want us to be sentimentalists (though we all need a good dose of love in all our dealings), and through scripture we must see how the modes are working, yet the fact is that in the short term it is not possible to tell who will be in what position in the long term.

Sometimes we require a “dark night of the soul” or a “bottoming out” before we are able begin to really surrender to the Lord. We must allow for that. Today’s fallen souls may be tomorrow’s saints, while those who appear advanced today may stumble tomorrow. O devotees, haven’t we seen this so many times? Where would we be if Śrīla Prabhupāda had seen us only externally? He saw our potential, that covered spark of spirituality. He saw us and sees us as we truly are. O Gurudeva, let us have that spiritual vision!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let kindness, love, compassion, empathy, and understanding reign supreme in our hearts. Their application may bring a person “back” (did they really leave?) much faster then our stark presentation of “the truth.” The medicine of Kṛṣṇa consciousness must be administered expertly and on an individualized basis. As you, dear master, so often demonstrated, truly, one size doesn’t fit all. Though we devotees all share the same philosophy, we are all very different, and we may practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness differently. O Prabhupāda, you gave us the real standard for unity and compassion: “Real unity is advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.…In the Kali-yuga you cannot strictly follow, neither I can strictly follow. If I criticize you, if you criticize me, then we go far away from Kṛṣṇa consciousness” (Morning Walk, 10 March 1976). Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us understand that we need to embrace “unity in diversity” if we are to be broadminded and not fanatics or impersonalists.

The spiritual world is full of wonderful variety and differences in the “oneness” of service to Kṛṣṇa. “Each and every one of the sons of King Prācīnabarhiṣat was an individual soul, but they were united in offering transcendental service to the Lord. The unity of individual souls attempting to satisfy the Supreme Lord or rendering service to the Lord is real unity” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.30.8, purport). Please, dear Gurudeva, bless us to have this unity with all the devotees.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you loved us and were beyond material duality, even your chastisement was like nectar. “The highest development of Krsna consciousness understanding will be when you are able to give anyone the truth but in such a manner that they will respond in a positive way.” (Letter to Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa, 13 December 1971). Dear Prabhupāda, please bless us to radiate unconditional love, happiness, and encouragement to attract everyone to Kṛṣṇa.

O Prabhupāda, nowadays, in this time of factionalism of devotees, some of us see more in common with, or are more favorable to, other religions or spiritual paths than with devotees of Kṛṣṇa with different views. People who look at our Websites on the Internet wonder why we have so much disagreement. If we are encouraged and enthused by our understanding, that is wonderful, yet if another is encouraged and enthused by a different flavor of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, should we not be glad? Does everyone have to be just like us?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, though we strive to follow your standard *as we understand it*, please let us to have transcendental equanimity. We can remain faithful to our understanding yet be inspired by others who are engaging in service according to their faith and convictions. Let us have that much respect for each other. “So if I find simply fault with you, and if you find fault with me, then it will be factional, and our real business will be hampered” (Morning Walk, 10 March 1976).

As you know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, some devotees may not come back to the standard of spiritual practices they formerly embraced, or we may have some heated differences, or they may be in a different camp then we are. Regardless, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us to see them as deserving of our love, prayers, and encouragement on their spiritual journey. They are not aliens—they are devotees—and in this world how rare they are!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us be convinced that we truly need each other in all our differences. Our differences let us look at what we really stand for and deeply believe. “They should always chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* then everything will be adjusted. Otherwise, it is not possible to advance. We shall be complicated with details only. This is called *niyamāgraha*” (Morning Walk, 10 March 1976). Thank you, dear Prabhupāda, for challenges to work through, for through them we can make more advancement. Thank you also for your books and instructions. Please let us become free from false pride.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us all acknowledge that when we come to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the material world, we can’t help bringing our “stuff”—our misconceptions and conditioning—into our practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We sometimes follow your instructions or style without understanding your true spirit or the rationale behind your actions. Certain instructions we may not really, deeply understand (or even have faith in), nor may we know how to apply all your instructions in different circumstances. Dear Prabhupāda, let us not only preserve the structure of ISKCON but the content and spiritual purpose that form its true foundation.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please inspire us to be careful what we do in the name of “preserving what Prabhupāda gave us” or in “saving ISKCON” (very emotionally charged ideas), so that we don’t become self-righteous in believing we know definitely what you would do in every situation. We have to endeavor to see the true Kṛṣṇa conscious perspective, praying to understand, while acting with caution, humility, and love.

You certainly showed your ocean of mercy in all your dealings with your “problem” disciples, or with those whom others labeled problems.Good intentions without insight and real compassion can lead us on the wrong path. O divine master, please lead us on the right path!

O Prabhupāda, with all the benefits of having a formal way to associate with devotees, institutional living can certainly complicate our personal practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and test our real advancement. Though we attempt to make ISKCON flourish as our service to you, we don’t want to become known as a society of law enforcers or quarrelers but a society of lovers of God, who demonstrate this love by our behavior (even with those with whom we disagree). This is who we truly are. Please dear Prabhupāda, help us exemplify ideal character and true compassion, and help us be as liberal-minded as you.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, as soon as we have a project or a home to take charge of and become a leader or head of the household, we have special challenges. There is a tendency to adopt the material vision of defense and see in terms of friends and enemies. Our false ego can rear its ugly head and bring us into a “black and white” consciousness, where there is only one way (our way) to serve. Whether we are in charge of a temple, a zone, or a house, this vision makes it very difficult to understand other points of view and creates divisions and cliques.

The Krishna Consciousness Movement is for training men to be independently thoughtful and competent in all types of departments of knowledge and action, not for making bureaucracy. Once there is bureaucracy the whole thing will be spoiled. There must be always individual striving and work and responsibility, competitive spirit, not that one shall dominate and distribute benefits to the others and they do nothing but beg from you and you provide. \[Letter to Karandhara, 22 December 1972\]

O Prabhupāda, please bless us to become pure devotees with spiritual vision, as you desire!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, for real unity every devotee’s input and assistance is vitally important. To illicit that support, the proper climate or mentality must be established in each temple and in the heart of every leader and devotee. We must be able to listen to each other with respect. Dear divine master, please give us the desire to really listen to each other and help us stop habitually criticizing or condemning (which is just like cursing) each other or holding grudges. Let us start praying for each other’s welfare—especially those we differ with.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us not underestimate the power of combined prayer to rectify a mentality or situation. We remember how you engaged all your disciples in praying for you when you were ill. The prayers of the devotees are powerful. After all, ours is a movement of prayer for service and mercy. Our thoughts and intentions toward others are vitally important for our own advancement and others’ welfare. Our inner life is the root of our actions and future.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us come up with novel ways to create unity. Unity is possible by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and with our faith and constant endeavor. Unity is more difficult than disunity (which is like the air in Kali-yuga) but far more satisfying and pleasing to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅgadeva. Whatever effort it takes to create unity, it is well worth it, because we know that our love for you will be shown by how we cooperate.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us truly respect and honor every person, especially every devotee who has ever rendered service, including ourselves. It is really amazing how we can be so kind to nondevotees in preaching yet sometimes so cruel and hard-hearted with devotees we disagree with or feel offended by. Without forgiveness, love, kindness, and mercy, how can we expect Kṛṣṇa to find our heart a fit place in which to dwell? O Gurudeva, let our hearts become purified, and let us hear and act upon the Lord’s guidance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us to look for the best in every devotee, seeking points of agreement, not emphasizing the differences, knowing that we tend to notice what we focus on. Let us be able to step back to gain perspective on our problems and differences or find an impartial person who can. Sometimes we are too close to our problems to have an objective view. This is why we need to create an atmosphere of openness and sharing, so that we can gain new perspectives and solutions from those not embroiled in controversies. The “silent majority” has much to offer. O beloved Prabhupāda, as always, though especially now, we need your help, blessings, and illuminating guidance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the moonlike beacon that illuminates our mirrorlike hearts according to how much dust has been removed by the holy name and devotional service. The fire of the Lord’s light shines through you, setting our hearts ablaze. Our responsibility is to be keepers of the flame of devotion, shining that light to benefit the world and to illuminate the most auspicious spiritual path for everyone. However, if we are too busy arguing with and criticizing one another, our light will be eclipsed and your mission spoiled.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let the power of love and devotion for you and our dear Lords Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa uncover what pushes us away from one another, and let that power thus create unity of purpose, bringing us all together to work at spreading the chanting of the holy names, despite our differences!

Let us, by your grace, become uncommonly good souls. “Among the uncommonly good souls there are still gradations, and the best good soul is one who accepts an insignificant asset of a person and magnifies that good quality” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.4.12 purport).

Let our purified, compassionate hearts, spiritual vision, combined prayers, and endeavors for unity be our constant offering to you.

Your spiritual family in Baltimore, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Karṇāmṛta dāsa)

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*bhavataha caraṇa-kamaleṣu koṭi koṭi vandanāni*

We bow down innumerable times at your lotus feet.

Before offering our homage to you on the auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we must frankly admit that we are wholly unqualified to do so.

That great Vaiṣṇava *pūrvācārya,* Śrī Yāmunācārya, lamented his fallen position in his *Stotra-ratna:*

*na ninditaṁ karma tad asti loke*  
*sahasraśo yan na mayā vyadhāyi*  
*so ’haṁ vipākāvasare mukunda*  
*krandāmi sampraty agatis tavāgre*

**“There is not a single reprehensible act in the world that I have not committed thousands of times. O Mukunda, now when those deeds are fructifying, I cry out helplessly before you” (*****Stotra-ratna*** **20).**

Similarly, we must cry out for your mercy, even though we are wholly unqualified to receive it.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, now it is more than twenty years since you concluded your divine activities here on Bhu-maṇḍala, planet Earth. Your movement is quite obviously the fulfillment of the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

Your ISKCON institution, despite its faults, has fulfilled many of your desires. There is room for much improvement, but there is also the fact that transcendental literatures meant to purify the minds and hearts of the fallen members of the Age of Kali, such as the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* have been widely distributed and are now being read in numerous languages in numerous countries around the world.

These transcendental “time bombs,” as you called them, have surely brought about a “Hare Kṛṣṇa Explosion,” much to your delight.

The *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement of Caitanya Mahāprabhu has been globalized. It has also been revived in India. In view of the fact that Kali-yuga is increasingly becoming filled with atheistic and irreligious persons who are actually no better than polished hogs and dogs, these facts are indeed miraculous.

Śrī Yāmunācārya prayed to Lord Vishnu:

*tvāṁ śīla-rūpa-caritaiḥ parama-prakṛṣṭaiḥ*  
*sattvena sāttvikatayā prabalaiś ca śāstraiḥ*  
*prakhyāta-daiva-paramārtha-vidāṁ mataiś ca*  
*naivāsura-prakṛtayaḥ prabhavanti boddhum*

**“My Lord, those influenced by demoniac principles cannot realize You, although You are clearly the Supreme by dint of Your exalted activities, forms, character, and uncommon power, which are confirmed by all the revealed scriptures in the quality of goodness and the celebrated transcendentalists in the divine nature” (*****Stotra-ratna*** **12).**

This wonderful description of the blissful Lord very strikingly reminds us of your transcendental qualities.

It is impossible to really count your innumerable glories. Your transcendental activities and their impact on the world lead us to conclude that the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa must be extremely pleased with you, just as Lord Śrī Nṛsiṁha-deva was pleased with Bhaktarājā Prahlāda Mahārāja.

You taught all by the mood you exhibited while you were present among us. It was exactly like that of Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja:

*ahaṁ tv akāmas tvad-bhaktas tvaṁ ca svāmy anapāśrayaḥ*  
*nānyathehāvayor artho rāja-sevakayor iva*

**“O my Lord, I am Your unmotivated servant, and You are my eternal master. There is no need of our being anything other than master and servant. You are naturally my master, and I am naturally Your servant. We have no other relationship” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.10.6).**

The humble mood of service you displayed during your manifest appearance—completely freed from any desire for material pleasures and benefits—ought to have been sufficient to teach us to be humble and detached.

Unfortunately, we are constantly and intensely being influenced by the highly contaminated atmosphere of this dark, quarrelsome Age of Kali and find ourselves contaminated by the lower modes of nature.

As Lord Kṛṣṇa described in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (16.21):

*tri-vidhaṁ narakasyedaṁ dvāraṁ nāśanam ātmanaḥ*  
*kāmaḥ krodhas tathā lobhas tasmād etat trayaṁ tyajet*

**“There are three gates leading to this hell—lust, anger, and greed. Every sane man should give these up, for they lead to the degradation of the soul.”**

Our prayer is that you bless us with the good sense to give up these three enemies of the soul. Your instructions—*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya*—are clear. However, we are weak in applying them to each and every moment of our lives. This is indeed the test of spiritual life. In order to pass this test, we beg for your mercy. That alone can carry us across the ocean of material life.

We know that the Lord tests his devotees. Those with weak determination fail to pass His tests. You are glorious because you were so tested and you passed them all. You graduated to become glorified throughout the world as a great devotee such as Śrī Prahlada Mahārāja.

All glories to your example of determination to succeed in devotional service despite the many impediments on the path! You will remain an inspiration for millions of sincere souls who want to follow the path of pure devotion.

Kuntī Mātājī prayed to Lord Kṛṣṇa:

*namo ’kiñcana-vittāya nivṛtta-guṇa-vṛttaye*  
*ātmārāmāya śāntāya kaivalya-pataye namaḥ*

**“My obeisances are unto You, who are the property of the materially impoverished. You have nothing to do with the actions and reactions of the material modes of nature. You are self-satisfied, and therefore You are the most gentle and are master of the monists” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.8.27).**

You showed by your example what it means to give up everything. You explained this very nicely in the purport of this verse:

*Akiñcana* means one who has nothing to give materially. A factual devotee, or *mahātmā,* does not give anything material to anyone because he has already left all material assets. He can, however, deliver the supreme asset, namely the Personality of Godhead, because He is the only property of a factual devotee.…

Advancement of material vision or material civilization is a great stumbling block for spiritual advancement. Such material advancement entangles the living being in the bondage of a material body followed by all sorts of material miseries. Such material advancement is called anartha, or things not wanted. Actually this is so. In the present context of material advancement one uses lipstick at a cost of fifty cents, and there are so many unwanted things which are all products of the material conception of life. By diverting attention to so many unwanted things, human energy is spoiled without achievement of spiritual realization, the prime necessity of human life.

You endeavored to defeat various materialistic concepts of life by constantly writing, lecturing, and speaking the philosophy of the *Bhagavad-gītā* to whoever listened. You did this personally, and as your ISKCON institution grew you lectured to your disciples in the temples you established and also to thousands at public programs, especially here in India. To reach all humanity you authored the Bhaktivedanta Purports.

Unfortunately most people, being miserly, do not give up anything they possess due to attachment for *bhoga* and *aiśvarya*. Even after hearing the truth from the lips of a pure devotee such as yourself, such persons remain materially attached and do not attain the mercy of the Lord.

Will materialistic devotees such as ourselves be favored by the Lord in the same way as he favored Bali Mahārāja?

*na vai bhagavān nūnam amuṣyānujagrāha yad uta punar*  
*ātmānusmṛti-moṣaṇaṁ māyāmaya-bhogaiśvaryam evātanuteti.*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead did not award His mercy to Bali Mahārāja by giving him material happiness and opulence, for these make one forget loving service to the Lord. The result of material opulence is that one can no longer absorb his mind in the Supreme Personality of Godhead” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **5.24.22).**

You instructed both your devotees and the general masses, both verbally and by your life’s example, that we must detach ourselves from *māyā*. But her power is so great that we do not give her up.

*na dharma-niṣṭho ’smi na cātma-vedī*  
*na bhaktimāṁs tvac-caraṇāravinde*  
*akiñcano ’nanya-gatiḥ śaraṇya*  
*tvat-pāda-mūlaṁ śaraṇaṁ prapadye*

**“O You who are worthy of being sought as refuge! I am not fixed in** ***dharma,*** **nor do I know my own self, nor have I any devotion to Your lotus feet. Possessing nothing and having no other resort, I take shelter under Your lotus feet” (*****Stotra-ratna*** **19).**

We feel totally unqualified and helpless in this ocean of Kali-yuga. We accept you as the “captain of the ship.” Kindly guide us and purify our consciousness so that Kṛṣṇa consciousness may supplant our contaminated material consciousness.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22).**

Someday, if we give up *māyā* and our “agendas”—those concoctions, speculations, and attachments to gross sense gratification that you condemned on so many occasions—we might actually purify ourselves by following your divine instructions.

However, due to the influence of duplicity, which is so common at the present moment in Kali-yuga, we fall victim to the tendency of *ātma-kaitava,* simply cheating ourselves.

When will we give up this tendency to commit spiritual suicide in so many ways? When will we give up our attachments for sense gratification, ceasing all other activities and worshiping Lord Viṣṇu alone? When will we give up the quest for the myriad forms of knowledge to be acquired here in this world? When will be give up chasing after yogic and other perfections to attain an exalted position in this life and the next?

You continue to inspire us to both purify ourselves and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the upliftment of the other fallen souls in human bodies.

Therefore we humbly beg you to allow us to continue humbly serving you and your transcendental mission so that we might make our human lives and the human lives of others successful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your fallen servants in Baroda, India.

## Belo Horizonte

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept the humble obeisances of the fallen souls of Belo Horizonte, Brazil. Though you never came here, by your mercy and magnanimity we have the opportunity to exit the cesspool of material existence and terminate our estrangement from our original home, Kṛṣṇaloka, and our beloved Flute-player, whom we have forgotten or ignored for countless lifetimes.

Though His love for us is ever present, like the sun, we have sought out the darkness of the lowest, darkest shadows of sinful life. The personal sacrifice you made to try to drag us back to the light has saddled us with an unrepayable debt. We are eternally grateful to you. Even the meager service we do does not diminish what we owe but rather increases it, for through such service we taste the nectar of relating to Kṛṣṇa in our constitutional position.

Through your books you continue to inspire us to forsake our hard-hearted desire to be the Supreme. You beckon us to forget about our own pitiful fame, beauty, wealth, wisdom, power, and even renunciation and to glorify Him who possesses those qualities to an unlimited degree. You offer us freedom from the frustrating futility of material enjoyment.

The opportunity you have given us is more rare than diamonds and gold. Please give us the intelligence to seize it wholeheartedly and to share it with other suffering souls, for their salvation and your glorification.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Belo Horizonte, Brazil.

## Benin

Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

Your Vyāsa-pūjā reminds us to rededicate ourselves to your extremely compassionate mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. It requires great mercy and compassion to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, especially in this part of the world, predominated as it is by the material mode of ignorance. Though we are most degraded, rebellious, and sinful, you have sent us a most capable commander to assure our deliverance.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect us from the degrading qualities of the mode of ignorance, such as foolishness, madness, and illusion, so that we can remember the instructions contained in your books. Give us steadfastness so we can render quality services and emulate the wonderful examples our spiritual masters show us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything, but we are unable to properly utilize this gift, just as a poor man does not know how to handle money if he suddenly gets it. Please continue to protect us so that at least we remain in devotional service throughout this lifetime.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your devotees in Benin, Nigeria.

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

The most obvious purpose for writing an offering for this book is to glorify you—the Lord’s pure devotee. However, it seems that several other purposes are also served by this activity: those glorifying you (as well as any readers or listeners) must undergo some reflection about you, their relationship with you, the ways in which you’ve transformed their lives, etc. Such meditation and sound vibration purify their hearts. It is a learning experience for all involved.

Reading through some of the offerings you wrote for your spiritual master on his Vyāsa-pūjā, we find you displaying different moods. There is the memorable poetic offering of 1935, featuring the stanza that pleased your spiritual master so much:

Absolute is sentient thou hast proved,  
Impersonal calamity thou hast removed.

There is the wonderful offering of 1936, the “Universal Teacher,” which reads almost like a philosophical treatise about the role of the spiritual master and ends with the following extraordinary sentiment revealing your true humility:

Personally, I have no hope for any direct service for the coming crores of births of the sojourn of my life, but I am confident that some day or other I shall be delivered from this mire of delusion in which I am at present so deeply sunk.
