We are also trying to make more facilities on the farm so that many more guests can stay here to take *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Prahlāda-Nṛsiṁha-deva, attend programs and seminars, and get a good, ever-lasting impression of everything Kṛṣṇa consciousness can offer.

We hope that you will be satisfied with our endeavor and bless us, so that we can always be engaged in your mission and never, ever deviate from the path you showed us. Your mercy is our life, your mission is our soul, your movement is our home. Please take us back to Kṛṣṇa’s abode.

Your insignificant servants at Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, Germany.  
(written by Avidmān dāsa)

## New Badarikāśrama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our humble obeisances hundreds and hundreds of times on this auspicious day.

Since you left, so much has changed. We guess it’s only natural, in a way. Still, we must question ourselves as to what we are doing wrong. Surely things aren’t as we expected they would be. Sometimes we feel bewildered and pessimistic. As we take shelter of your books, we find so much that we failed to notice before. We pray for the intelligence to apply your teachings. We’ve been requested not to submit grievances, only glorification. But our grievances give rise to your glorification, because we are made acutely aware of the differences between you and us, of your extraordinary greatness and our mistakes. We become awestruck at how you could manage a rapidly growing society, when we are overwhelmed at managing a small temple with a handful of devotees and a few hundred congregational members, not to mention worrying about the state of affairs of the whole of ISKCON. Besides, you must detect the most grievances—like how much we have failed to live up to your expectations of us.

The essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is hearing, chanting, dancing, taking *prasādam,* and preaching. How blissful! How easily we feel reinstated in our true identity when we do these things. How much we desire to immerse ourselves in this divine process. But then we feel obliged to take up extra responsibility to push on your movement, like managing. We never desired it, but somehow or other it has fallen upon us. You have always had to tolerate our shortcomings and limitations. When others complain about our incompetence and accuse us of material motives, we are solaced by thinking of how you are kind and understanding. When discouraged in our service, we like to remember that you would sometimes quote the adage “A blind uncle is better than no uncle.” Why would we take up this service except to try to please you? It is a burden of love. Only for you.

How you lived amongst us, in spite of our waywardness, abominable habits, and attachments to nonsensical things, amazes us. Because of not advancing beyond the bodily concept of life, we are not exactly a harmonious, unified, loving community of like-minded people. We are committed to you and the Deities. And we feel spiritually stronger in the association of devotees. So, putting aside our dissimilarities, we stay together in gratitude. Only for you.

Before joining ISKCON we were hardly adept at making money. What a relief it was that you maintained us, housed us, fed us, and provided all shelter. We didn’t even have to think of money. But now we find that we must worry so much about money. You showed by your example that Lakṣmī stays with Nārāyaṇa and that where His glories are preached there is enough money. That must be why the Temple remains open in spite of our debts. We wonder how we will raise the funds to cover the operational expenses and Deity worship. Certainly we must increase the glorification of Nārāyaṇa. And then there’s all the accounting that must be done…We accept this tremendous headache of handling money as an offering of love. Only for you.

We thought spiritual life meant love and peace. We never dreamed we would have to attend temple board meetings and property zonal commission meetings. We have to hire lawyers and sometimes even attend court to ensure our constitutional rights after being harassed by envious and ignorant people. We have to obtain permits for book distribution, festivals, *prasādam* distribution, construction, insurance for property and automobiles. The list goes on. We marvel at how you remained ever so transcendental, on the *brahma-bhūta* platform. Loving and peaceful. We can only aspire to gain some competence. It is essential that young men and women be trained to take up the management, to become leaders.

Falling at your feet, we humbly pray for empowerment, that you may preserve what we have and carry what we lack. We know that we are useless without your mercy. As inexperienced as we are, we continue to serve you by managing, until you reveal another plan for us. We do it only for you. Because we love you.

Your devotees at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, Colorado, USA.  
(written by Kamalinī-devī dāsī)

## New Dvārakā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which for the first time brought to the Western hemisphere the unadulterated teachings of the Vaiṣṇava *paramparā*! We are unqualified to glorify you adequately on this most auspicious day, but out of duty and love we will make our best attempt.

As you instructed us from the very beginning of your preaching in America, worship and glorification of the spiritual master are essential. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (2.7) Arjuna appeals to Kṛṣṇa:

*kārpaṇya-doṣopahata-svabhāvaḥ*  
*pṛcchāmi tvāṁ dharma-sammūḍha-cetāḥ*  
*yac chreyaḥ syān niścitaṁ brūhi tan me*  
*śiṣyas te ’haṁ śādhi māṁ tvāṁ prapannam*

**“Now I am confused about my duty and have lost all composure because of miserly weakness. In this condition I am asking You to tell me for certain what is best for me. Now I am Your disciple, and a soul surrendered unto You. Please instruct me.”**

In your purport you explain:

By nature’s own way the complete system of material activities is a source of perplexity for everyone. In every step there is perplexity, and therefore it behooves one to approach a bona fide spiritual master who can give one proper guidance for executing the purpose of life. All Vedic literatures advise us to approach a bona fide spiritual master to get free from the perplexities of life, which happen without our desire. They are like a forest fire that somehow blazes without being set by anyone. Similarly, the world situation is such that perplexities of life automatically appear, without our wanting such confusion. No one wants fire, and yet it takes place, and we become perplexed. The Vedic wisdom therefore advises that in order to solve the perplexities of life and to understand the science of the solution, one must approach a spiritual master who is in the disciplic succession. A person with a bona fide spiritual master is supposed to know everything. One should not, therefore, remain in material perplexities but should approach a spiritual master.…

Although \[Arjuna\] could understand that his duty to fight was awaiting him, still, on account of miserly weakness, he could not discharge the duties. He is therefore asking Lord Kṛṣṇa, the supreme spiritual master, to make a definite solution. He offers himself to Kṛṣṇa as a disciple. He wants to stop friendly talks. Talks between the master and the disciple are serious, and now Arjuna wants to talk very seriously before the recognized spiritual master.…

To serve you in a serious mood similar to Arjuna’s, the leaders at Los Angeles ISKCON have accepted a very special mission. Actually, each department head must have a very special vision, because you especially wanted ISKCON Los Angeles be the national and even international leader in setting devotional standards. Therefore, we see that Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa’s *pūjā* standard is world famous. And beyond any doubt, Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa are the most famous and glorified Deities in ISKCON. We have personal experience that no matter where one travels on this earthly globe, he will find pictures of Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa. In every temple and in all the life members’ homes, and even in some businesses of friends of this movement, in every city on each and every continent, Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa are there, standing in exaltation, blessing the conditioned souls and reminding them of their transcendental nature. And it is all because you gave the inspiration to worship the Deities to such a high standard. We rarely find this high standard even in India itself. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that your inspiration came only from Kṛṣṇa Himself.

ISKCON New Dvārakā has an especially enthusiastic *saṅkīrtana* spirit, unrivaled in America. Of course, this fired-up book distribution spirit is the life of the temple. So at this time we are very happy to report to you the good news that in 1997 the New Dvārakā *saṅkīrtana* team again won the first-place title among all the temples in North America in your book distribution, both for the year and for the December Marathon. Your devotees here distributed almost 12,000 *mahā*-big books, 64,000 big books, 54,000 medium books, 43,000 small books, 25,000 pamphlets, and 23 full sets of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatams* for the year! We know this gives you tremendous transcendental pleasure. And because the temple is fortunate to house your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, we can hear the nectar of the numerous letters coming daily, describing people’s appreciation of your transcendental literatures. All the devotees are quite moved to hear how much the conditioned souls are dramatically affected by your divine association.

In 1997 ISKCON New Dvārakā was also able to distribute many thousands of plates of *prasādam:* well over 150,000 plates of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s special mercy made it into the hands of fortunate souls last year. This was made possible by an enthusiastic Govinda’s Buffet crew, caterings, dynamic Sunday Love Feasts, and of course the world-famous Los Angeles Ratha-yātrā. All this is your divine transcendental plan. Dozens of new *bhaktas* are an essential part of this temple, and they played a vital role in the success of all the above-mentioned programs. They are becoming inspired to maintain and increase these programs in the years to come. In this way we can begin to repay our debt to our spiritual master. From the earliest days, you wanted your Los Angeles ISKCON center to train up leaders to help the rest of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness society. Today many temples send their new devotees to New Dvārakā for association and instruction in the very principles you worked so hard to establish. Then we are able to send these qualified devotees around the world to start new centers, to help established centers, and in general to give the conditioned souls your association so they will take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**“The value of a moment’s association with a devotee of the Lord cannot even be compared to the attainment of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which is for those who are meant for death”** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **(1.18.13).**

The spiritual master’s duty is to increase the disciple’s affinity for him. You knew the importance of this very personal relationship. Therefore in your books we find thousands and thousands of references to the relationship between the spiritual master and his disciples. And we find a direct connection with the dynamic service performed in New Dvārakā Dhāma as the culmination of this relationship.

It takes many years to gain someone’s full love and trust, but material love and trust can be lost in a moment by the betrayals of this world of duplicity. Moreover, deviation from the regulative principles and offenses at the lotus feet of the spiritual master are falldowns that embarrass your movement and shake the faith of the devotee community. And it takes much time to restore one’s love and trust, if it is even possible to fully do so. Therefore, in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.5.25 purport) you write,

Without being self-controlled, without being disciplined, and without being fully obedient, no one can become successful in following the instructions of the spiritual master, and without doing so, no one is able to go back to Godhead.

What is the use of half-following your instructions and not being able to contribute significantly to your movement? Better that we try to keep the highest possible standard, as set by your example, and simply depend on Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and your mercy. And we can factually see the result here in New Dvārakā: Because here there are many devotees who are striving to execute Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the highest standard, we see that there are new devotees coming, massive distribution of your transcendental books, *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and *prasādam* distribution on a large scale. And this proves how amazing your plan was and is. Please let our faith in you continue so we can continue to cling to your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we don’t feel qualified to fulfill the immense responsibility you have given us. Our only hope is if Kṛṣṇa helps us. But since we cannot directly approach Kṛṣṇa to plead for help, we appeal to you to plead with Him on our behalf—then surely we will be blessed beyond our imagination. In the *Mahābhārata* Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira says, “The solid truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of an unadulterated self-realized person. Consequently, as the *śāstras* confirm, one should accept whatever progressive path the *mahājanas* advocate.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the *mahājana* whose path we accept. Therefore we accept the following instruction in relation to you:

*etāvaj janma-sāphalyaṁ dehinām iha dehiṣu*  
*prāṇair arthair dhiyā vācā śreya-ācaraṇaṁ sadā*

**“It is the duty of every living being to perform welfare activities for the benefit of others with his life, wealth, intelligence, and words” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.22.35).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this body belongs to you, and this life belongs to you.

This temple is your temple, these buildings are your buildings. And this philosophy is your philosophy. You have Kṛṣṇa’s inspiration because you have surrendered everything perfectly to Kṛṣṇa. You are Paramahaṁsa. We therefore owe you everything we have because you gave us the greatest gift possible—Kṛṣṇa consciousness and the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. We have no right to try to add anything, subtract anything, or change any of the philosophy you have given us. We have great respect for your disciples and grand-disciples who are carrying out your instructions and mission, and we would like to take this opportunity to thank them. Their association is very valuable to us, and we pray that they may continue to be inspired to carry on enthusiastically glorifying you and your movement here in Los Angeles and around the world for many years to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your servants in New Dvārakā Dhāma, Los Angeles, California, USA.

## New Goloka Dhāma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to your sincere and dedicated followers, who are pushing forward this transcendental movement.

As this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement continues to expand and take root throughout the world, we are faced with bigger and greater challenges every day. These challenges will surely be surmountable, for your potency is shining and emanating through all of your dear devotees who are carrying out your instructions. We have personally been able to observe the depth of the love of your dedicated disciples, whose foremost desire is to serve you and to please you. Your desires are manifesting through their unalloyed devotional service. Throughout the world, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is becoming firmly established as many great temples are being constructed and as your books continue flooding every inch and every corner. This is one of your greatest desires.

Here at New Goloka Dhāma, we are strongly feeling your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the purity of the spiritual guidelines you have given to us have been upheld and maintained throughout the years. You have blessed us with the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Golokānanda, of whom so many souls have taken shelter. You have built a house in which the whole world can live, and within our house here at New Goloka there is true protection, guidance, and pure devotional service for those who take shelter and follow your instructions.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our community continues to grow year by year. Your transcendental potency continues to manifest as we watch your mission become more and more powerful. You continue to inspire us through your teachings in your books, audio- and videotapes, and your representatives.

Please continue to give us the strength and determination to adhere tightly to your words, and give us the potency to change the face of the world. We pray to be always under your divine protection, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to remain forever chaste to you—to your instructions.

Begging to remain your eternal servants,  
Your servants at New Goloka Dhāma, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.

## New Govardhana (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious appearance day of yours, please accept our humble obeisances.

In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.87.1), King Parīkṣit asks Śukadeva Gosvāmī, “O *brāhmaṇa,* how can the *Vedas* directly describe the Supreme Absolute Truth, who cannot be described in words? The *Vedas* are limited to describing the qualities of material nature, but the Supreme is devoid of these qualities, being transcendental to all material manifestations and their causes.” Similarly, how can we use our limited words to properly glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are situated on the absolute platform? Still, we pray that by the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu (the most munificent incarnation), and Nityānanda Prabhu (the original guru), words of glorification will come from our mouths, and pages of poems of glorification will be written through our hands, so love and respect will develop naturally in our hearts. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our words are stammering and our prayers are no good, yet we still beg you to accept this humble prayer of ours.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it seems that you are far away from us. Though you traveled around the world many times, you never visited this fallen piece of land. When your were still on this planet, we never had the good fortune of seeing your brilliant smile and hearing your teachings.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also seem to be very close to us. We feel that you are by our side, giving us instructions. We even feel that you are situated in our hearts and are instructing us from within. Is it not true? Since the Lord is all-pervading, His pure devotees’ energies cannot be checked, can they? The Lord is willing to see that His devotees become greater and more successful than He is. There is no doubt about it.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you arrived at America, you were penniless. People thought you were just a poor monk. Yet in fact you were the richest person in the world, for you were carrying Kṛṣṇa with you. In all the three worlds, what is more precious than Kṛṣṇa? Nothing! Definitely nothing! So who can say that you were poor?

With the development of ISKCON, you established many temples. Yet you did not want to be worshiped. You just wanted people to worship Kṛṣṇa in the temples you established. You wrote so much literature, which turned many fallen souls into pure devotees and is now still guiding the whole world toward the future. Though your service to Kṛṣṇa is beyond comparison, you still humbly gave all the credit to your Guru Mahārāja, and you also gave him all you accomplished.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so great and humble. As followers of yours, what should we learn from you? Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said that your books will be the spiritual guide for the world for the next ten thousand years. Who else ever said such a thing before? You said this not out of pride but out of your realization of the Absolute Truth and due to your being recognized by Lord Caitanya. Yet, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can we understand your books with our tiny brains? We are prone to misinterpret the teachings in your books. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, that is why we beg you to protect us from stepping on the wrong path of misinterpreting your books. If we do, we will spoil our good fortune for countless births. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have built a house in which the world can live. We hope and pray that we can all find shelter in it.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have given incomparable gifts to the whole world!

Your immature children at New Govardhana, Khasadeśa.

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which are the shelter for innumerable devotees and which remove all the obstacles on the path of devotional service.

Appreciation and gratitude for the causeless mercy you’ve bestowed upon us flood our minds and hearts, especially on this Vyāsa-pūjā day.

When Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu described the glories of His devotees, it appeared as if He had many mouths. For those who met you, this description comes to life. Glorification of your sublime qualities and eternal preaching activities fill an ever-increasing volume of literature. Every day thousands of devotees throughout the world recite and hear your glories.

It is said that one pure devotee of Lord Caitanya can deliver the whole universe. As we watch your movement spread all over the globe, expanding to greater and greater proportions, we realize how true this is. It is happening before our eyes!

Your mercy, like the sunshine, is available to everyone. The more we surrender to your instructions and serve you without duplicity, the more your mercy increases. Because you are Kṛṣṇa consciousness personified, you make devotional service so simple. Our greatest misfortune is that we complicate the whole process with our material desires.

Our prayer to you, today and every day, is for you to give us the intelligence, faith, and strength to always accept that which is favorable for taking shelter of you, your teachings and movement, and to reject anything or anyone that might divert us from Your Divine Grace.

Please bless us so we can become qualified servants of your devotees and your mission to inundate the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your aspiring servants,  
The devotees of New Govardhana-dhāma, San Diego, California, USA.

## New Jagannātha Purī

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your wonderful lotus feet and pray that you will permit us to offer some reflections on the gifts given to us by you—you who are so dear to Kṛṣṇa and your own sublime preceptor, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. You came ever so briefly to these Western countries with his mission to offer the sublime alternative to impersonalism and voidism, namely the all-inclusive shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

We often walk through the U.C. Berkeley campus, going the same way you sometimes walked early in the morning with your disciples. We sometimes imagine one or another conversation you might have with us, prompted by something in current events—just as you would so often comment on concerns of the times more than two decades ago.

As we walk by the main library, we imagine mentioning to you that the building is now undergoing retrofitting renovation work, to strengthen it against the danger of an earthquake. You might have challenged, “Why didn’t they think to build it strong enough when they originally built the building?” We might feebly try to defend the well-meaning but ignorant architects, after which you would probably make your point, the lesson to be learned: “First they spend millions of dollars to build it, then again more millions to save it from falling down. What is the use? Simply a waste of time. Sooner or later, they will be finished—earthquake, war, the effect of time. This is the law of nature” And then with a penetrating look you would conclude, “Who can stop it? Nobody can stop it.”

Keeping with the library theme and relishing your indictments of the “modern so-called civilization,” one of us (trying to keep up with your quick stride) might have then commented, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I just read that there are over eight million books in this library...” And you would have made scathing remarks about modern “so-called” higher education, concluding, “So many books, so much knowledge, but still, *na te viduḥ svārtha-gatiṁ hi viṣṇuṁ*—they do not know that their own benefit is Viṣṇu, Kṛṣṇa.” We imagine a third devotee piping up, “But Śrīla Prabhupāda, at least they have a set of your books here in the library, so now the library has been made perfect!” To which you might have smiled as the devotees chorused, “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!” Then you would ask, “But are they reading our books? That we want. It is one thing to have the books, but you must inspire them to read them; otherwise what is the use?”

We hardly need to imagine conversations with you, as there are thousands of printed pages of your conversations available for us to read. But the thought of hearing personally from you, again and again, your scathing comments about the matters of the day, as we go about our activities encountering this world, is spiritually invigorating. And at the end of the day we need to ask ourselves, What is the basic theme of all your comments—your critiques of these current events? Of course, we hardly need reflect: your point is always “Kṛṣṇa consciousness”—the lack of it in this world, the necessity of it, the logic of it, the urgency of it. But always your conclusion is “Therefore take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and somehow or other give it to others.” In one conversation (not an imaginary one) you even accommodated those who may not accept Kṛṣṇa as God. After quoting *Bhagavad-gītā* 7.7 (*mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat*), you said, “Every word here is God’s word. You may accept Kṛṣṇa as God or not, but the words are God’s words. That is *Bhagavad-gītā*.” Behind your insistent critique is your genuine love for all beings, that all may be uplifted.

Let us be reminded not to take your words simply as entertainment (although true pleasure is to hear from you), nor as a means to bolster our resentments due to our own frustrations with life in the material world (although your words are indeed our final shelter, bruised and battered as we are by our own folly). Behind each and every one of your words—words we have heard and heard again, and which therefore become part of our lives, more and more the center of our lives—is a call from the depth and breadth of your molten-gold heart—a call to dismantle the massive, steel-framed structures piled about our own hearts, to enter into the supreme, everlasting conversation that is *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. There are earthquakes coming sooner or later in this shaking world, that is sure. But you are here, your heart trembling and quaking in devotion to the lotus-eyed Rādhā-Gokulānanda, as you guide us over the great “fault-line” of material waywardness to the Lord’s lotus feet. Let us follow you, and call your attention to the events of the day, and hear your scathing replies.

Your servants at New Jagannātha Purī, Berkeley, California.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## New Kusuma Sarovara

**Your Family**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your sincere followers!

By the blessing of your dear son His Holiness Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami, our Guru Mahārāja, we have a daughter, Śālinī Rādhā. My wife always tries to teach her to call you Grandfather, although the baby cannot speak yet. Her attempt seems pointless, apparently, but as her Godbrother and husband, I understand her thinking and feeling.

Four years ago, my wife left her family to follow the spiritual path. Now she has her own small family and her own daughter, and she wants to connect her small family with another great soul-connected family. That means your family, Śrīla Prabhupāda. She wants to stay in your family forever.

Just as fish feel at home living in the sea, she feels blissful and free in your family. Besides her husband and daughter, she has a father, and she has uncles, aunts, brothers, sisters, and cousins. She has all varieties of relationships in your family, and not only in her own country but also in other countries such as America, Britain, or Portugal. Actually, she has identified with your family.

Many times, I have noticed that whenever she mentions the members of your family, her face turns bright and gleeful, so I have come to the conclusion that she takes pride in your family.

Maybe from the viewpoint of mundane tradition her behavior and thought are strange or even crazy, but I can appreciate it, or rather, I like it because both of us have no shelter other than your lotus feet and your divine family.

The name of your family is called ISKCON. For us, you are the grandfather and our spiritual master’s dear father. In your family, there is no envy, animosity, birth, death, old age, or disease. There is only transcendental service and love of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the origin of this family. By serving in your family, we are touched, protected, and blessed by the hand of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who appeared in the form of Lord Caitanya to deliver the fallen souls in this Age of Kali. Your family is so attractive that Lord Kṛṣṇa is living in your family as the supreme master, so actually your family is the real and only family for souls in this dark Age of Kali because Lord Kṛṣṇa is the supreme father of all living entities.

Only in your family do I realize the real and perfect meaning of service to the family. By serving my spiritual father, I have been blessed by you and Lord Kṛṣṇa to have such a wonderful family and to engage in service to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my dear Grandfather, for letting us take pride in your family. We pray for your permission to stay in your family forever.

Your insignificant grandchildren in New Kusuma Sarovara, Khasadeśa.  
(written by Siddhadeha dāsa)

## New Orleans

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

We are once again called together by this auspicious occasion to renew our relationship with you and rekindle our dedication to your mission.

Thank you for allowing us to spend another year serving your mission here in New Orleans (New Sankirtan Puri). When this time comes around each year, it gives us the opportunity to pause and reflect on what you have given the world. Being immersed in so many day-to-day problems and conflicts, even in the course of service, we can easily lose sight of the “forest for the trees” and forget that we are actually proceeding on the final great journey back to the supreme abode. Your mission is ecstatic and liberating, and you have often shown your matchlessly superior capacity to transmit devotional service to the conditioned souls.

Here in this temple you personally installed the Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhākānta, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā and named this temple as New Sankirtan Puri due to its tremendous book distribution potential. Since then lots of ups and downs have occurred in this temple, but due to your kind blessings we are now back on the track of book distribution every day, and we also have a Food for Life program.

You always said that wherever there is Kṛṣṇa’s book distribution and *prasādam* distribution, that temple will never go down, and we have personally realized that. As soon as we concentrated more on distributing books and *prasādam* on a regular basis, you started showering more and more blessings and the temple started developing like a wild creeper. You teach by your example. But we are so ignorant that we get diverted from your main instructions and do something else. We know that success in spiritual life and preaching will surely come if we follow your teachings without change. The world may go on changing, but our goal and dedication will not change.

Of all your transcendental qualities, it is your compassion and understanding that touch us the most. You understand how fallen we are, and you are compassionate and patient in dealing with us. You are truly *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī*. Just because you are not physically present before us does not mean that you have forgotten about us, or that you are no longer making sacrifices for us. You are forever alive and preaching through your books, and you mercifully appear in the minds, hearts, and dreams of your devotees. We hope to associate with you more and more as time goes on.

Our progress in pushing on this movement for the highest welfare of humanity is simply due to your spiritual potency and superexcellent devotion to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Just as Lord Caitanya made even the animals chant the holy name of the Lord, you came to preach to us although our consciousness was lower than that of the animal kingdom. Indeed you have given us the greatest treasure, and it would be extremely foolish not to accept this gift of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

As your servants in New Sankirtan Puri, we all pray that you will inspire us to render pleasing, favorable service and to assist you in carrying out you mission. We pray to be able to maintain and increase our life-giving *sādhana* so as to continue to become purified from our material conceptions of life. As an offering to you, let us work together to benefit everyone through preaching and true community! Please save us from creating offenses to devotees and to sincere spiritual aspirants, and free us from the “us versus them” mentality. We also pray to remain faithful to you and to ISKCON and to not become bewildered. We all express our gratitude to you for giving us the most precious gem of devotional service and the knowledge that can take us out of the material world.

A pure devotee is a resident of Goloka Vṛndāvana; therefore the place where he descends and performs his *līlā* must be like Vṛndāvana. The *Bhāgavatam* (1.13.10) says,

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“My lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.”**

We pray to you on this auspicious day that we may imbibe a fraction of your spirit, your humility, your simplicity, and your mercy for all the conditioned souls.

Hoping to remain eternally at your lotus feet,  
Your servants in New Orleans, Louisiana, USA.  
(written by Sukhada dāsa)

## New Pāṇihāṭī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. By your mercy we have been allowed to continue serving you for another year. Every day we feel thankful for the opportunity to engage in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

All the devotees here in the Atlanta temple community are becoming more and more excited about the idea of preserving the memory of your visit here. The constant thought of your visit here in 1975 is the source of our inspiration, and we want to develop your project so that generations in the future will have the blessing of being able to visit one of the actual locations of your divine pastimes.

In India people travel for many miles and with great hardship to visit one of the locations of the pastimes of the great *ācāryas*. Are you not one of the greatest *ācāryas* in the history of Vaiṣṇavism? Therefore we should hold places of your pastimes as sacred and as worthy of our greatest efforts to restore and maintain for years to come. Just see the opportunity that we, as members of your ISKCON, have before us! But we must act quickly because the years are passing by so rapidly and our time on this planet is limited.

We can just imagine that in the distant future Vaiṣṇavas will be going back in the past, our present, searching for significant periods in their history. In their search they will undoubtedly pass over many devotees who did not perform any significant activities. They will be looking for important places connected to the great devotees that they will be reading about, great devotees such as Your Divine Grace. We hope that the Atlanta temple, New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, will be one of those places they will find still intact and not lost to antiquity.

The Vaiṣṇavas of the future will indeed be so thankful to those of us who made the sacrifice to preserve your memory and pastime places. We do not want to subject them to the same troubles that the Christians are experiencing in their efforts to establish the factual existence of Lord Jesus Christ and the places where he performed his activities. What a wonderful blessing we have at this time in history!

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be kind upon us and give us the spiritual strength and vision to do something wonderful for your glorification. With faith in you, that which is normally impossible becomes possible and easily performed. The task before us in establishing your pastime place here at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma is certainly very great, but with your blessings we are sure that we will be able to accomplish it in our lifetime.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your humble servants at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, ISKCON Atlanta, Georgia, USA.

## New Ramaṇa-reti

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this day we make a special effort to glorify you. Of course, we show our appreciation for your divine mercy every day of the year by engaging in Kṛṣṇa’s service according to your instructions. There are 314 adult devotees on the most recent list of Vaiṣṇavas in the New Ramaṇa-reti community. Each of these devotees, with you as the center and transparent via medium to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is performing devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead according to the individual abilities and gifts bestowed by Kṛṣṇa.

Devotees in New Ramaṇa-reti are carefully managing Śrī-Śrī-Gaura-Nitāi’s temple according to your desires. Some are engaged in Deity worship, some in maintaining temple facilities, and others in distributing your books or performing *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. We have Vaiṣṇavas cooking and distributing *prasādam*, regularly attending *maṅgala-ārati,* decorating Kṛṣṇa’s temple, and studying your books and sharing the knowledge in classes on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. Hundreds of devotees in the community are overcoming the struggle with the mind and senses to fulfill their agreement with you for chanting *japa* and following the regulative principles that you delineated as essential for advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Devotees in the community are protecting cows and bulls, as you wanted, and some are farming for Kṛṣṇa, and others are sweeping the temple room floor. Devotees are arranging many festivals here, with transcendental dramas, ecstatic *kīrtanas,* singing, dancing, and feasting. Also, New Ramaṇa-reti serves as a hub of national and international ISKCON projects, where Kṛṣṇa conscious magazines and newspapers are produced, the scientific basis of Vedic knowledge is firmly established, and management of your movement is performed, refined, and improved.

Many devotees, in the course of earning their livelihood, are preaching Kṛṣṇa’s glories according to time and circumstance, to practically every sector of the local community. Your devotees are spreading Kṛṣṇa’s glories to businesspeople, auto mechanics, politicians, real estate agents, accountants, hair stylists, musicians, counselors, professors, electricians, students, plumbers, technologists, secretaries, computer technicians, nurses, doctors, lawyers, carpenters, artists, architects, educators, medical practitioners, writers, cleaners, social workers, construction workers, and numerous other elements of society. Many devotees preach in their homes, and some preach about Kṛṣṇa on the street. We offer you this meager attempt at engaging fallen souls in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Of course, there are the children, who are also busily absorbed in remembering and serving you. An observer might perceive the children to be the center of the community, and this is so, as the devotees are dedicated to raising Kṛṣṇa conscious children who will be the cornerstone of your brilliantly conceived spiritual house, which will shelter millions for thousands of years to come. Many devoted Vaiṣṇavas here in Alachua are serving as parents, teachers, school administrators, tutors, foster parents, adoptive parents, and mentors for these children, who are the world’s hope for salvation at your lotus feet.

We are caring not only for the children but for each other. This is the material world, and we have all found ourselves in difficult times. At such times your devotees help each other with material necessities, emotional and physical support, and spiritual guidance. More than the humble attempts at service of so many individuals, this cooperative spirit is our most valuable offering to you, for it is this team effort to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness by which we can most genuinely show our love for you. Just as political parties share the aim of improving the condition of the country, our diverse Vaiṣṇava community is united in pleasing you and expanding your glories. Differing opinions abound, but by your mercy we are feeling like a secure family of devotees, happily and peacefully serving Your Divine Grace, our loving father.

You are the unifying force, and by the force of your purity the New Ramaṇa-reti community possesses the seed of a progressive, spiritual social structure, integrating traditional Vaiṣṇava culture with your fervent desire to promote Kṛṣṇa consciousness in all corners of the world. There exists here a developing model for a Kṛṣṇa conscious community that is momentous not only for the current boundaries of ISKCON but for the entire population of the world, as it spirals deeper into the abyss of Kali-yuga. Some may question, “Are you sure that you’re referring to the community in Alachua, Florida?” Our response is a resounding yes, and with this affirmative we beg your mercy to intensify our efforts at making this a devotional community that will attract thousands of souls to the path leading to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Your aspiring servants at New Ramaṇa-reti, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Śāntipura (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Our dear lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is your appearance day. It is the most unusual and most auspicious moment for the otherwise most unfortunate souls in the darkest land outside India. Your transcendental descent into and travels through this material world are like a lotus flower that grew up from the mud without being contaminated by even a bit of the filthy mundane elements. Rather, it is the fallen souls, once connected with you, either directly or indirectly, who experience eternal spiritual transformation. This truth also applies to Khasadeśa. The transcendental message brought into this country by your faithful servants has ignited many sparks here and there in this isolated land.

This is a country you never personally visited. Still, there is never a moment we cannot feel your transcendental presence. Rather, we are bathing in your unbounded mercy. We are enlightened by your transcendental intelligence resounding in your books and lectures. That is your unparalleled position as the eternal *śikṣā-guru* of your movement. From the association of your bona fide servants who faithfully serve your lotus feet, we can feel the rays of your effulgent spiritual qualities. From the beautiful and refined Vedic culture, we can smell a fragrance of your saintly disposition, which gives us a hint of the inhabitants in the spiritual world. You are with us, our dear lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Most significant of all, though we encounter various difficulties both internal and external, by your mercy we persevere to maintain ourselves in this transcendental family by following the spiritual processes day by day. Meanwhile, under the guidance of our spiritual masters and senior Vaiṣṇavas we are endeavoring to enlarge and invigorate this branch of your transcendental family. And in our hearts we cherish a hopeful vision of an ever-brightening future. All these miraculous happenings—and those going to happen even more gloriously—are made possible totally by your will, your unshakable will! You are with us all the time, our dear lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Never can we move an inch without the blessings emanating from your lotus feet.

As you know, when it comes to glorifying Your Divine Grace during *kīrtana,* there is a burst of call—“Jaya Prabhupāda!”—spontaneously cried out by everyone in unison. It is the expression of our repentance and endless gratitude to you, a call by the most fallen souls for their most compassionate and magnificent savior. It is our condensed prayer from the core of our heart, the full version of which might go like this: “Śrīla Prabhupāda, please pour your mercy upon us so that soon the day will come when we can be engaged fully in your dynamic *saṅkīrtana* movement and distribute the holy name to the vast suffering mass of people in Khasadeśa, when we can be more closely connected with your body, ISKCON. We are intensely eager to join in the dynamic activities of your transcendental family and serve you cooperatively as an integral branch.”

We have full confidence that this day will surely come, because it is your will to fulfill the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “The holy name shall be spread in every corner of every town and village.”

Again and again let us bow down humbly at the sacred dust of your lotus feet and beg for your oceanlike mercy.

Your eternal servants in New Śāntipura, Khasadeśa.

## New Śrīvāsa Aṅgam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The appearance of the Lord’s pure devotee in this material world is the most rare and auspicious event for this otherwise desolate place. Indeed, the Lord manifests his mercy toward the conditioned souls through the activities of his pure devotees. Yet even among pure devotees, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you occupy a unique position. No one else spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. No one else rendered the powerful *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and other devotional literatures into English for the benefit of millions of Westerners and, through translations, everyone in the world. And not only did you render these devotional literatures into English, but you elaborately explained them in a nectarean way that is specifically designed to awaken love of Kṛṣṇa in the hearts of the readers. Before you did this, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who knew that the Supreme Absolute Truth is a person with a transcendental body characterized by eternity, knowledge and bliss? And who knew that the Supreme Person takes great pleasure in engaging in a variety of transcendental loving relationships with his eternal associates? Who knew that there is a method for dissolving our false identification with and strong attachment to the material world?

For this and many other reasons, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are greatly indebted to you. We know that this debt can never be repaid, but we are trying to do so by distributing your books as much as we can and preaching intensely in the colleges and universities. We know that these activities give you great pleasure, and so we want to go on doing them for the rest of our lives.

Your servants at New Śrīvāsa Aṅgam, the ISKCON farm community in Denmark.

## New Śyāmakuṇḍa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your auspicious appearance day!

Time is flying and a year has again gone. Now your children and grandchildren all over the world are reading aloud their prayers to celebrate your appearance day. For those who want to make progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, today is most joyful and worth celebrating. Every one of us finds him- or herself so fortunate that we could become devotees and servants of Kṛṣṇa, so that we can really live healthily, nicely, and happily. All this is because of your advent and mercy. You give love of God to the conditioned souls, just out of your causeless mercy.

While moving toward the end of this century, we are finding that people’s greedy desires are increasing everywhere around the world. Most people have given up the idea of living as human beings. They just want to enjoy and gratify their senses unlimitedly. That is why there are so many conflicts and wars among people, races, religions, and countries. People fight for things that actually do no belong to them. Nowadays the so-called modern civilization, which is based upon atheism, is leading the innocent public toward a dead end, and there is no way out except Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

At first people are hopeful, then they become disappointed, and then desperate. They do not know how to face the future. Some them ask themselves, “Now we have created what we can create; we have traveled where we can travel, we have tasted what we can taste, we have enjoyed what we can enjoy. So what should we do now? What should we do now?” The only thing we should do is to give them Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the consciousness of serving God. I think that was why you went to the West. And that is also what we, children delivered by you, should endeavor to accomplish with our whole life.

As your grandchildren at New Śyāmakuṇḍa, we feel ashamed, because the other Khasadeśa *yātrās* are growing and increasing their preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but we have not done anything brilliant. Spiritual weaknesses are what we possess. We are actually very eager to become spiritually mature. The people around us are under the spell of the threefold miseries and are living like moving corpses. They are in need of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You have given us the seeds of devotional service, but we do not want to be so selfish as to keep them ourselves. We want to give them to more people. We are sincerely praying for your blessings. Please let us become perfect tools in the hands of you and your servants. The *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are ours weapons. With them, we can enable those who have lost their vision in the ignorance of darkness to see light again. May we be able to see by the turn of this century what was predicted by Lord Caitanya, that the holy names are being chanted in every town and village—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Your grandchildren at New Śyāmakuṇḍa, Khasadeṣa.

## New Tālavan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. We aspire to be your eternally surrendered disciples and followers and are very pleased with your directions; we strive to fulfill your desires, and we feel so fortunate to have the opportunity to serve your lotus feet. Every day, every moment, is like a lifetime of divine service. We feel like the luckiest people alive.

Most embodied souls are forced to toil for economic or political stability. Others are serving humanity in various ways, and some are heeding and preaching the word of God according to different cultural heritages. Yet you have given us the opportunity to serve Lord Nityānanda and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu who are freely distributing *kṛṣṇa-prema*. What could be more refreshing and joyful then to give others their hearts’ greatest desire? Conditioned souls are crippled by uncontrolled senses and bound by material desires and views. Consequently, they are not good candidates for pure *kṛṣṇa-prema*. However, by meeting Your Divine Grace they become relieved of multitudes of sins and become fit to receive the greatest benefit. You are so kind that you selflessly work to relieve them, and your mercy extends to engage whoever wants to help. We can not imagine ever working again for someone or something unrelated to your mission.

O Gurudeva, you are Lord Kṛṣṇa’s representative, and you are propagating his mission on this earth. You are making love for Kṛṣṇa available to as many as possible. We are so fortunate to have the opportunity to act as your instruments in this divine task.

Without you our connection with the Supreme Personality of Godhead remains lost in the dark caverns of ignorance. Because of you, ample opportunity awaits us to understand, to give, and to love the Supreme Personality in the highest way. By your mercy, our occupation becomes illuminated with *bhakti,* and through you we can perform holy works reserved for priestly men of the highest stations of life. You are our support and refuge. We pray to never forget our good fortune occasioned by your divine intervention in our lives.

People think and act in terms of temporary estimations, but by your grace we can think and act beyond the boundaries of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s time feature. However, our works within time are essential to His plans to relieve the sufferings of all living entities and bestow the greatest benediction—pure *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

For this reason you have created the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and engaged your disciples in serving its purposes. By so doing we may please you and cross over the pain of material existence to reach the stage of absolute happiness. Better yet, we may assist others in reaching it. Can there be any greater goal? We think not!

O Gurudeva, your teachings bestow all perfection, both materially and spiritually. Can we ever repay you? We certainly cannot, but what do you ask for? Isn’t it just for us to continue distributing this mercy to others? How ideal, and yet we wane; but you and Lord Kṛṣṇa never wane, but always remain bright like the sun or the full moon. It is our good fortune and the good fortune of others that we got caught in the net of *bhakti* you have woven.

O Gurudeva, you are an ocean of mercy. Please allow us to always serve and listen to your nectarean words.

We remain  
*Your humble servants at New Tālavan, ISKCON’s farm community in Mississippi,* USA.  
(written by Yogīndra Vandana dāsa)

## New Varṣāṇā (New Zealand)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious day of your appearance we are struck by your dedication to your spiritual master and your determination to fulfill his order. Everyone thought that *pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma* was a myth or an allegory or something else. Only you could understand the true meaning. \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\]

At the advanced age of sixty-nine you left India on a steamship accompanied only by your *Bhāgavatams*. You carried only forty rupees and a sack of dry cereal. Only Sumati Morarji’s secretary was there to see you off. He thought you were going to America to die. No one knew that history was in the making. No one knew that the pure devotee was about to awaken the Golden Age.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you often commented that at your age everyone is generally retired. The *bābājīs* of Vṛndāvana advised you not to leave the holy *dhāma,* and in America you saw the elderly playing shuffle-board and lavishing their affection on dogs.

Once when you were sick, several devotees—stalwart *sannyāsīs—*requested you to stop traveling and simply retire and translate. After a long silence you said to them: “I want the benediction to die on the battlefield, just like Bhīṣma.” Your sober response struck every devotee to the core of his heart.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us that we may never be affected by the retirement disease. You never retired, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so there is not a single one among us who can say that we have done enough for ISKCON. Rather, let us always remember your example and strive to satisfy you by cooperating to maintain and push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Let us never forget the sacrifices you made for us, and let us always remember that whatever troubles and difficulties we may have are simply a mere trifle compared to what you experienced in establishing ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your servants in New Varṣāṇā, the ISKCON farm community near Auckland, New Zealand

## New Vraja (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear grandfather, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We are your fallen grandchildren.

A few years ago, as a fulfillment of your desire, some of your excellent senior disciples came to this place to deliver us and lead us on the brilliant path back to Godhead. Years have passed, and we have become your grand-disciples, one by one, fallen though we may be.

If we may say that we have made a little progress in our spiritual life, it is all because of your blessings, which we received by distributing your books, even though at the time we knew little about the essence of what was in them.

Today, our family has expanded, and more and more people here are sincerely chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. The message is spreading. We have never compromised with the impersonalists, but rather, scores of them have stepped into our family.

Last month on Gaura Pūrṇimā, we had a festival, and everyone was overwhelmed with ecstasy. Our spiritual master, Umāpati Swami, said that it was all due to your mercy.

Our merciful grandfather, although we have never seen you physically, we can feel your love and concern for your grandchildren at every moment. It seems that you have never been separated from us, but still, our tears flowed when we saw you leave this material world on the videotape. Have you seen us, these little grandchildren of yours?

On the day that your own Gurudeva left, you wrote, “My spiritual master! Tears burst into my eyes. I can’t tolerate losing you, my spiritual master.”

Our grandfather, we miss you so much. When will you bestow on us this kind of lamentation for you? When will you bestow such mercy on us?

You are never really away from us. You are with us through the timeless instructions in your books; you make your beloved disciples, our beloved Gurudevas, guide us at every step; and your splendid smile has appeared in our dreams many times.

We know that we can never love or understand you without the mercy of our Gurudevas. We beg for your benediction so we can render better service to them. This will be sure to please you.

Dear grandfather, please give us your blessings forever.

Your grandchildren in New Vraja, Khasadeśa.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*ei-bāro karuṇā koro vaiṣṇava gosāi*  
*patita-pāvana tomā vine keho nāi*

**“O Vaiṣṇava Gosvāmī, please be merciful to me now. There is no one except you who can purify the fallen souls.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration! All glories to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda for sending such a wonderful devotee as Your Divine Grace to deliver us from nescience and give us the gift of *kṛṣṇa-prema*!

Being the faithful, dedicated, and devout servant of His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you always exemplify the highest standard of all divine qualities, and dedication, humility, and gratitude are especially exemplified by the *śuddha-bhakti* you render at your beloved master’s lotus feet.

You have taught us that we must always dedicate ourselves to attempting to repay that which can never be fully repaid, our eternal debt to the spiritual master’s lotus feet. If we can even attain 1% of such a magnanimous humble attitude of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you displayed in the execution of your pure devotional service to Guru and Lord Kṛṣṇa, then we will succeed on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because 1% of your exalted state of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is an ocean of Kṛṣṇa conscious devotion for us, your worthless, insignificant, menial beggars.

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.80.41) states:

*etad eva hi sac-chiṣyaiḥ kartavyaṁ guru-niṣkṛtam*  
*yad vai viśuddha-bhāvena sarvārthātmārpaṇaṁ gurau*

**“This indeed is the duty of all true disciples: to repay the debt to their spiritual master by offering him, with pure hearts, their wealth, and even their very lives.”**

We know we can never repay you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us such wonderful Vaiṣṇava association, for giving us menial *seva* at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Vṛndāvana-dhāma, Māyāpura-dhāma, and Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, Śrīmatī Subhadrā, and Jagannātha Purī *dhāma,* or those absolutely great classics of Kṛṣṇa consciousness: the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Kṛṣṇa* book, and more. Who could ever repay Your Divine Grace for all the bliss of those ecstatic Sunday Hare Kṛṣṇa Love Feasts or festivals like Janmāṣṭamī, Rādhāṣṭamī, Gaura-pūrṇimā, Govardhana-pūjā, etc.? Bathing in the Yamunā and Gaṅgā, or the sea at Jagannātha Purī, where Lord Gaurāṅga bathed daily, are for us a divine source of inspiration for the Kṛṣṇa conscious spirit of *bhakti*.

Without these matchless gifts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would be bankrupt, devoid of real life, miserable, and possibly lying comatose in one of Māyā’s many godforsaken gutters. You saved us then, you save us now, and you will save us time and time again, always adding to our unlimited debt, which we will happily owe and make payments on forever.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have so nicely taught us, your insignificant students, about the proper relationship between the guru and his disciples:

\[Kṛṣṇa said to Sudāmā Vipra:\] “With great compassion our gurudeva said, ‘My dear boys, it is very wonderful that you have suffered so much trouble for me. Everyone likes to take care of his body as the first consideration, but you are so good and faithful to your guru that without caring for bodily comforts you have taken so much trouble for me. I am also glad to see that bona fide students like you will undergo any kind of trouble for the satisfaction of the spiritual master. That is the way for a bona fide disciple to become free from his debt to the spiritual master. It is the duty of the disciple to dedicate his life to the service of the spiritual master.’”

We can proceed toward the understanding of our eternal relationship of love for Lord Govinda only by fulfilling the commitment we have made to Your Divine Grace at the time of initiation and by continuously striving to fulfill that same commitment in the course of our lives. Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us your mercy so we may always attempt to fulfill the essential details of that commitment in the course of the development of our relationship with Your Divine Grace.

Your Divine Grace guarantees that you will bestow on us the priceless gift of *prema-bhakti*, love of Kṛṣṇa, and our return home, back to Godhead, the highest paradise, Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. The value of the treasure of love of Kṛṣṇa can’t be compared to any amount of material or spiritual opulence. So we therefore owe you a very great debt, Śrīla Prabhupāda, an eternal debt for sure. The purifying association available through ISKCON, which you so wisely planned, founded, and developed, can’t be measured in terms of pounds, shillings, and pence. We simply have to respect that high-class association of Vaiṣṇavas and beg for their mercy again and again so we don’t lose this great opportunity of Kṛṣṇa conscious *sādhu-saṅga*.

Our commitment is simple, Śrīla Prabhupāda: eternal, loving, menial devotional service rendered in ways that please you. What pleases you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You told us many times: “To see that my disciple is learning to love Kṛṣṇa....” “Distribute books, distribute books, distribute books!” “Chant the holy names of Kṛṣṇa and be happy and your life will be sublime!—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.” “Distribute this knowledge of the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the benefit of the unfortunate suffering conditioned parts and parcels of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.” And “You can show your love for me by how you cooperate after I depart.” By these simple and sublime instructions of Kṛṣṇa consciousness Your Divine Grace has blessed us, your fallen insignificant menial servants, with the highest happiness eternal.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your blessings have removed our fears, our illusions, and our sufferings forever, and for this mercy from Kṛṣṇa’s really pure devotee, Your Divine Grace, we make an unflinching commitment to try to repay our debt to you forever, Śrīla Prabhupāda, birth after birth, for as long you allow us that merciful opportunity to serve you. Indeed, we are eager to repay Your Divine Grace with a dedication that no amount of criticism or false evaluation can weaken.

Your Divine Grace has repeatedly and emphatically expressed the great importance of maintaining and supporting ISKCON:

We have to turn our attachment from material things to a *sādhu. ‘Sādhu-saṅga’, ‘sādhu-saṅga’—sarva-śāstre kaya:* this is the advice of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. All *śāstras* advise us to associate with a *sādhu.* Even Cāṇakya Paṇḍita, the great politician, recommended: *tyaja durjana-saṁsargaṁ bhaja sādhu-samāgamam.* One Vaiṣṇava householder asked Caitanya Mahāprabhu what the duty of a householder is, and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu immediately replied, *asat-saṅga-tyāga—ei vaiṣṇava-ācāra:* “Don’t associate with nondevotees, but search out a *sādhu.*” (Cc. *Madhya* 22.87)

At the present moment it is very difficult to avoid the company of *asādhus,* those who are not *sādhus.* It is very difficult to find a *sādhu* for association. We have therefore started this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement to create an association of *sādhus* so that people may take advantage and become liberated. There is no other purpose for this society. \[*Teachings of Lord Kapila,* Verse 21\]

In Śrīla Rāmānanda Rāya’s discussions with Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the most essential devotional service accepted by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu was to hear and chant *kṛṣṇa-kathā* in the association of pure devotees; that was considered the highest utilization of our precious time.

When Lord Caitanya was talking with Rāmānanda Rāya, there were varieties of suggestions offered by Rāmānanda regarding spiritual realization, but Lord Caitanya rejected all but one-that one should hear the glories of the Lord in association with pure devotees. That is acceptable for everyone, especially in this age. One should engage himself in hearing from pure devotees about the activities of the Lord. That is considered the supreme benediction for mankind. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.15.49, purport\]

Does the institution of ISKCON offer facility for such *saṅga* and *kṛṣṇa-kathā*? We say it does, thanks to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Does the institution of ISKCON work toward fulfilling the desires of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and pure devotees such as Śrīla Rāmānanda Rāya, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and Your Divine Grace? We say it does, thanks again to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made the importance of Vaiṣṇava association within ISKCON crystal clear:

If you do not understand Kṛṣṇa, if you do not surrender to Kṛṣṇa, then you are simply wasting your time. There is no meaning of writing big, big comments on *Bhagavad-gītā,* uselessly waste you time and waste others’ time. This is the real point, *śraddhā.* Kṛṣṇa said, by surrendering to Kṛṣṇa everything will be done. *Ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi.* So if one has no *śraddhā,* faith, on this instruction of Kṛṣṇa, *man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī,* then there is no use, simply waste of time.

If we come to this point, *ādau śraddhā*…This is called *śraddhā.* Then the next stage is *ādau śraddhā tataḥ sādhu-saṅgaḥ.* If one has…one is convinced that “Surrendering to Kṛṣṇa, my all business will be perfect,” this is called *śraddhā.* Then we have to seek out who are engaged in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, *sādhu-saṅga. Sādhu* means Kṛṣṇa conscious person. *Sādhu* does not mean having a big beard and nice dress or saffron-colored dress. That is not *sādhu. Sādhu* is described in the *Bhagavad-gītā, api cet su-durācāro bhajate mām ananya-bhāk, sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ.* That is *sādhu,* who has no other business than to serve Kṛṣṇa. He is *sādhu*. He is *sādhu* because he has accepted the principle, *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekam. Sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ samyag vyavasito hi saḥ.* You cannot say, “Oh, he has got some bad principle in his life. Although he is worshiping Kṛṣṇa wholeheartedly, but he has got some defect.” Therefore Kṛṣṇa says, *api cet su-durācāraḥ.* Even if he has got so many defects of his life, because he has dedicated his life for Kṛṣṇa’s service, *sādhur eva sa mantavyaḥ.* So if you have got *śraddhā* that by surrendering to Kṛṣṇa your all purpose of life will be practical or fruitful, then you have to associate with such persons who are engaged in the service of Kṛṣṇa, *sādhu-saṅga. Ādau śraddhā tataḥ sādhu-saṅgaḥ.* Therefore our this movement is called International Society for Krishna Consciousness. \[Lecture in New Delhi on 14 November 1973\]

Many thanks again and again to Your Divine Grace for keeping us awake and straight on the path of perfection: service to the Vaiṣṇava devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa with an unswerving determination to remain your menial assistants in pushing on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We are always being reminded of this by your words of wisdom, and we would like to thank you very much for inspiring us with such potent words, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* it is said: *vyavasayātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* “Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose, and their aim is one.” Our only business is to be fixed up in devotional service by pleasing the spiritual master. Those who are not fixed up they have various lines of action. (*eka* means “one” and *bahu* means “many”).

The real ocean of mercy is Krsna, and it is the duty of the spiritual master to tell his disciple to come to the ocean and be happy. The spiritual master’s duty is to lead the disciple to this ocean. I am trying my best, and if you try to follow surely you will benefit.

On this most blessed, auspicious occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please be merciful to us. Please never forget us, please don’t leave us alone to drown in an ocean of sorrow. Please uplift us by your all-powerful mercy and allow us to serve Your Divine Grace purely, even in some very insignificant way. Please be merciful to all of us, your fallen, insignificant beggars, your sons and daughters who aspire to always associate together by hearing *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, and dancing in ecstasy, in this life and the next, with those followers of yours who are dedicated, sincere servants, who strive to maintain, preserve, and expand this International Society for Krishna Consciousness that you have so mercifully and wisely founded.

All glories to the topmost swanlike *paramahaṁsa* founder-*ācārya,* His Divine Grace Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda! We beg to remain your servants forever.

Your fallen, insignificant children at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir, Brooklyn, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## Pandarapur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

ISKCON Pandarapur is slowly growing, emerging out of the sugarcane fields. This year we inaugurated the new guesthouse. Before there was only one building, containing the temple of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva and Subhadrā, the kitchen, and two small rooms. We now have quarters for guests, space for setting up an office and new rooms for the resident devotees.

Our efforts year after year have been toward the acquisition of land, in view of developing an attractive project in this holy town, where Lord Caitanya’s brother, Viśvarūpa, entered *samādhi* and where Caitanya Mahāprabhu himself spent eleven days. This year we made good progress, getting several pieces of land on the banks of river Candrabhaga. The site is beautiful, overlooking the town on the opposite side of the river.

Pandarapur is one of the largest places of pilgrimage in India. On every Ekādaśī thousands throng for *darśana* of Lord Viṭhṭhala, Śrī Dvārakādhīśa Himself. For the last few years a steady group of devotees from Pune, Pandarapur, and some other places have taken part in the Dindi-yātrā—the main pilgrimage to Pandarapur—in which 200,000 people walk together for two weeks. With all the pilgrims that come by bus, train, and other means, close to a million people take *darśana* of the Lord on Śayanā Ekādaśī. It is a sight to behold—and a grand opportunity to perform *harināma* and flood the crowd with your books.

After the renovation of the goshala a couple of years ago, it is now much easier to take care of the cows. We let them graze in different fields all over the property, and they provide us with their milk. We also managed to develop a vegetable garden. In this way we are gradually working at developing self-sufficiency, which is one of our goals.

We are incompetent, but we’re trying our best. We need plenty of your mercy to spread the glories of Lord Caitanya and the holy name in Pandarapur Dhāma. Please give us your shelter. We are eager to please you.

Your servants in Pandarapur, India.  
(written by Praśāntā-devī dāsī)

## Paris

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear spiritual master and grand-spiritual-master,

Please accept our devoted obeisances unto your lotus feet.

We love to remember your visits to Paris. Our first temple was a small house in the suburbs. We had hardly finished painting the walls and cleaning the house when you came in, appreciating the fresh paint smell, and rewarded the devotees who stayed back preparing for your arrival with your broad, “oceanic” smile. You had to share the toilets with all of us, and you did so with elegance and humor. We remember your walks in the parks. Stopping in front of a famous statue, you laughed and said, “Poor man, he has worked so hard all his life to become famous, and what is his reward? Once a year there is a celebration and one man comes with a broom to sweep off the bird stool accumulated for the whole year.” You were always so eager to preach to anyone desirous to hear. You were extremely attentive to the persons in front of you and dealt differently according to time and circumstances. In a huge hall, la Salle Pleyel, you started your lecture by quoting a verse from the Bible—“In the beginning was the word …”—knowing France to be a predominantly Catholic country. You said that the French devotees were very sincere and the people here are very expert in sense gratification, noticing how nicely they set up their homes. Let us follow in your footsteps in our preaching activities, and thus they will become successful.

Once during the celebration of one of your Vyāsa-pūjās, you told us that on this occasion the disciple should meditate on the progress he has accomplished on the path of self-realization on behalf of the guru. Today, as a whole body, including the devotees of the Paris temple and the congregation of devotees and friends, we would like to thank you endlessly for the matchless gift you gave us, the gift you boldly called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Living in Paris, which ironically was known in past centuries as La Ville Lumiṝre (The City of Light), we witness every day how Kali-yuga’s progress is deepening the *jīvas’* sufferings and how relationships are deteriorating between parents and children, wives and husbands, citizens and leaders. We sadly remember the Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira’s questions to his dear brother Arjuna at the time of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s departure, as Yudhiṣṭhira observed so many bad omens. So today we would like to make a promise: We promise to endeavor to fulfill our preaching mission, to maintain a heaven of spiritual peace that can ease and comfort the pains of the conditioned souls. In order to do so, we pray to be able to daily respect and treat each other with the utmost care, in a very personal way, thus creating a spiritual atmosphere of love and trust.

We will pray at your lotus feet to send us all the sincere seekers of the Absolute Truth. Fascinated by Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Parisīśvara’s brilliant and compassionate beauty, and by you clear and sharp philosophical message coming through the *paramparā* from time immemorial, they will come forward. Our only desire is to become your transparent via medium and your insignificant servants, so that we can help them grow the priceless seed of the devotional creeper and make it bloom into a wonderful tree of *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Remembering Lord Caitanya’s great devotee Vāsudeva Datta, we would like all conditioned souls of the city to be delivered before us and to remain eternally your unconditional servants, wherever you like, life after life.

Your devotees at the ISKCON temple in Paris, France.  
(written by Mandākinī-devī dāsī)

## Philadelphia

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. During your Vyāsa-pūjā lecture on September 5th, 1969, in Hamburg, Germany, you implored us all to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The excerpt is as follows:
