I am a *sannyāsī,* you know. I came here empty-handed. So you are providing me. What can I do for you? I shall simply pray to Kṛṣṇa. Another thing, that don’t be satisfied that you have understood. That’s all. No. This should be distributed. Just like in my old age I have come to your country carrying the order of my spiritual master to distribute it. You are all young boys and girls; take this message and distribute it. The whole suffering humanity will be happy. That is our mission.

You lived your life in an exemplary manner as demonstrated by the following incident that took place on September 22, 1974.

During the previous six weeks, while staying in Vṛndāvana, you had been very ill, with an intermittent temperature of 103 to 105 degrees. For weeks you daily ate only some pieces of orange and a few grapes. Twenty-four-hour *kīrtanas* were being performed by your concerned disciples worldwide. You said your ailment could not be cured by medicine because your illness was due to 80% of the leaders in ISKCON who were not strictly following the rules and regulations. In spite of your condition, you had one overwhelming desire, to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness

As soon as you began to recover, you left Vṛndāvana to visit your other centers in India. Your first stop was Delhi, where you planned to rest for one day before continuing to Calcutta and on to Māyāpur. The devotees were always enthusiastic to receive the blessing of your presence. Soon after arriving in Delhi, you were offered the chance to speak at a *Bhagavad-gītā* discourse in Kurukṣetra.

You replied, “These meetings are useless. These people will never accept Kṛṣṇa. What is the use of going out there and talking. It would be a waste of my time.” We were relieved that you did not want to take such a difficult ride in the hot summer, so soon after a serious illness.

As the conversation progressed you said you would consider accepting the invitation and thus take the opportunity to preach. You very sweetly told us to deliberate on the matter and advise you what we thought was the best course of action. Of course, taking into consideration your fragile condition, we had all previously thought the long trip would be too stressful.

You often gave us the chance to serve in this way. It was very sweet how you engaged us. You always remained independent, but you allowed us to meditate on taking care of you. Now your secretary discussed the situation with other GBC men and *sannyāsīs*. They again concluded that you were still too weak to take a long automobile drive to and from Kurukṣetra. Initially, you agreed with them.

Around 2:00 p.m., Śrutakīrti heard your bell ring. He went into your room and offered obeisances. You said, “Get everyone.” Your disciples entered the room, eager to hear from their spiritual master. You said, “I think we shall go to Kurukṣetra. This is my duty. I can not give up this opportunity to preach. It won’t be so difficult. We can leave in the morning before sunrise. The ride will be about two hours.” Then, looking at everyone with the compassion of the pure devotee, you asked rhetorically, “Is that all right?” Your secretary, Brahmānanda Mahārāja, said, “If that is what you want, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can arrange everything.”

You arrived at Kurukṣetra in the scorching heat with a few disciples. Your host took you on a short tour to visit some nearby temples and then to your guest quarters. Later you were escorted to the pandal and seated in the midst of several so-called *sādhus,* many with beards and long hair. They were eager for you to be present, for they all knew you were famous all over the world.

They appreciated your *vapu* manifestation but feared your *vāṇī,* so they weren’t eager to have you speak, knowing your reputation as a no-nonsense *kṛṣṇa-bhakta*. You looked glorious sitting among them, like the full moon among so many twinkling stars.

As the various speakers pontificated, you sat impatiently listening to their rhetoric. They talked in Hindi and English about unity, understanding, and religious tolerance. With emotions heightened, you sat in a chair with your jaw clenched, your foot tapping on the stage. You became angrier with each passing minute as the speakers talked of peace and *dharma*. The members of the mutual admiration society of pseudo *sādhus* continued praising one another with delight. They reveled in the gathering of so many holy persons to inaugurate the undertaking of a new temple there. You sat still like a lion about to pounce.

Finally, without any introduction you stood and began to roar in Hindi. We, your disciples, could understand some of what you were saying—“*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja / ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi mā śucaḥ*.” Your lecture was short, fiery, and conclusive. At the end of your speech you walked back to the car with your disciples. Everyone scattered and began leaving the assembly.

After getting into the car you turned to us and said enthusiastically, “So, you could understand the lecture? I spoke in Hindi, but, you could understand some?” We all smiled broadly, feeling your pleasure and said, “We understood the purport of what you were saying.” “Yes!” you said, “This is all nonsense. These people, they will never get anywhere because they do not accept Kṛṣṇa. This is their business. This is their idea. Here in India, they try to make unity, but they never accept Kṛṣṇa. Why they speak of *Bhagavad-gītā*? You shouldn’t speak of *Bhagavad-gītā* unless you are accepting Kṛṣṇa.” Chuckling, you continued, “How you are in the land of Kṛṣṇa, Kurukṣetra, where Kṛṣṇa spoke on the battlefield to Arjuna, and not one person has mentioned Kṛṣṇa? You are talking about *Bhagavad-gītā*…talking about Kurukṣetra…Kṛṣṇa spoke *Bhagavad-gītā*…Kṛṣṇa was here in Kurukṣetra…and not one of you mentioned anything about Kṛṣṇa.” You enthusiastically defended your dearmost Lord, and we were proud to follow in your transcendental footsteps.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you accepted so much inconvenience to spread the glories of Kṛṣṇa. In spite of illness, advanced age, and extreme heat you pushed on. Your incredible feat at Kurukṣetra was punctuated by a severe typhoon. As our car drove, huge uprooted Eucalyptus trees fell in front of us. Kṛṣṇa safely guided you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, through torrential rains and maneuvered your car through many obstacles on the road. It was a dramatic conclusion to a successful day.

It was your pleasure to triumphantly fight for Kṛṣṇa on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra. You always relished speaking about Kṛṣṇa, your dearmost friend. In this way, you are most dear to the Lord.

We pray that our only desire is to serve you with the same eagerness with which you have served your Guru Mahārāja. For you have spread the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa under any and all circumstances, up to your last breath. We pray to serve your mission birth after birth.

Your servants in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Śrutakīrti dāsa and Kuśa-devī dāsī)

## Port Harcourt

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Today is the auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā of Your Divine Grace, a day we should offer our love to you, a day we should offer our devotion and determination to spread your message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, neither love, devotion, nor determination do we have, and above all we are faithless.

Our prayer to you today, Śrīla Prabhupāda is this: Please do not withdraw this rare opportunity you have given us. It is very obvious that we are taking your instructions for granted, and the result is that we are diminishing in all aspects of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We know that if we sincerely desire to preach your message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you will allow us. But your conditions are: purity is the force, books are the basis, preaching is the essence, and utility the principle. Please, it is said that the grandfather is more merciful than the father. So forgive us all our offenses in all aspects and still give us the strength to be able to participate in preaching and distributing your transcendental books.

All glories to your Divine Grace! Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your grandsons and granddaughters in Port Harcourt, Nigeria.

## Prabhupāda-deśa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The temple here has been named Prabhupāda-deśa in glorification of you, so we are all very fortunate that we can always remember you and how you have saved all of us. We worship you daily on your *vyāsāsana,* and we can go into your room, so nicely constructed by the devotees for your pleasure, and feel your presence. We are going out regularly to distribute your books, depending on this for our sustenance. In so many ways we are remembering you at every step of our lives.

Our lives are only meant for pleasing you. The only criteria in navigating through life is if our decisions will please you. This can be confirmed by the approval of your other devotees, your presence in your teachings, or our own sense of satisfaction or happiness.

Recently I was giving a talk about the various movements of Kṛṣṇa when He was present in this world, as you describe in the *Kṛṣṇa* book, and we came to the discussion of Kṛṣṇa in Dvārakā marrying 16,108 wives. A guest was present who has been coming for years but doesn’t practice the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Upon hearing these descriptions he immediately became agitated. “How can I believe this? Is there any scientific proof?” We had some discussion, but being faithless, he couldn’t understand. I had noted before the lecture and now during our discussion that he had an expression of great suffering on his face.

Then the lecture ended and I went downstairs to the temple, and upon entering I saw you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so beautiful and majestic on your *vyāsāsana,* so clearly personally present there. All the devotees were so happily chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and dancing. I thought how fortunate we are: *nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*. You have saved us from a world of impersonalism, voidism, and faithlessness, full of suffering. Just by following your instructions with faith, we can enter into the all-blissful world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But one who doesn’t have faith in you is lost and can never approach transcendence. What is the problem for Kṛṣṇa to have 16,108 wives? He can have 16 *billion* wives or an unlimited number. We can gain faith in this by following your instructions and thus associating with you. Every day that I follow your instructions and hear from you I become a little more convinced, despite my seemingly never-ending attraction for sense gratification. You asked the devotees “Are you convinced? Only if you are convinced can you help me.” Let us become ever more convinced. You are supremely convinced, and by following your instructions we will become convinced, no matter what else may happen, that Kṛṣṇa is reality and He has unlimited potencies.

You said that ISKCON is your body. How could anyone think to do anything to hurt your body? I feel your presence here in your movement even if there may be difficulties. For me there is no question of Kṛṣṇa consciousness without you. When an ocean liner is plying across the sea and some storm comes and the ship rocks, the solution is certainly not to jump off into the turbulent ocean. If I were ever separated from your body, ISKCON, which is like a great boat crossing the ocean of Kali, I know I would drown in the ocean of material existence in a very short time. Sometimes on a big ship one might see some small islands in the distance or some small speedboats, but I have never felt attracted to any other vessel or shelter, since you won’t be there. There have certainly been many difficulties, and there will be many more, but abandoning ship is certainly not an alternative. You would want us to cooperate to make things better. “If you want to show your love for me, then cooperate together.” Despite all that has happened in these long and difficult twenty-one years since your departure from our physical sight, I more than ever want to cooperate and serve in your mission, ISKCON, because I know you would be pleased with me.

Therefore we are all praying to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance that you please allow us to continue, even in an insignificant way, to serve within your mission, ISKCON, and never to lose faith or give up trying to participate and help it. Let us become free from the tendency to criticize others, which is so destructive, and cooperate to spread your DIVINE mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your eternal servants at Prabhupāda-deśa, in Vicenza, Italy.  
(written by Trai dāsa)

## Pretoria

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on his most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

South Africa is proving itself to be a real testing ground of people’s faith—faith in the economy, faith in security, faith in negotiation and reconciliation, faith in man’s abilities of logic and reason. And since ISKCON is working within the social milieu of Western society, it cannot be said that the devotees are not being affected by it.

The other day, two of your *saṅkīrtana mātājīs* were mugged while returning to the temple. Nothing serious, but scary. Some of your congregation are emigrating to “greener fields” overseas. Some of your *gṛhasthas* are taking risks in making quick money and becoming severely entangled. Devotees risk their lives now on a daily basis to preach in the townships. Temple managers do not really know where the money will come from as the Indian congregation suffers retrenchments and poverty.

So certainly there is also a test of faith for the devotees: a test of meeting the need of the times. Not only within the temples, on a day-to-day basis, but in the broader society of devotees and South Africa as a whole.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* was written for *rājarṣis,* and certainly many of your pre-1966 purports were aimed at the leaders of society. ISKCON is meant to be more than just a crash-base for society’s drop-outs. You wanted ISKCON to change the face of the world. “All questions answered, all problems solved.”

Pretoria is South Africa’s capital, and it is apt that on Rāma-navamī I was praying to Lord Rāma for guidance as to how to be a better leader and servant in ISKCON. The next day you answered my prayer in my *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* reading (9.10.50, purport):

Simply enforcing laws and ordinances cannot make the citizens obedient and lawful. That is impossible. Throughout the entire world there are so many states, legislative assemblies and parliaments, but still the citizens are rogues and thieves. Good citizenship, therefore, cannot be enforced; the citizens must be trained. As there are schools and colleges to train students to become chemical engineers, lawyers, or specialists in many other departments of knowledge, there must be schools and colleges to train students to become *brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas, śūdras, brahmacārīs, gṛhasthas, vānaprasthas,* and *sannyāsīs*. This will provide the preliminary condition for good citizenship (*varṇāśrama-guṇān-vitāḥ*).

Of course, I was astounded that you specifically mentioned “chemical engineer.” This was the only place you ever wrote those words, and that was my field of study at the university. But although I took it that you were preaching to me personally through your purport, I still felt that your words were meant for the whole Pretoria temple, indeed, for any temple in any capital city of the world.

After I read those words, it seemed as though you were presenting a challenge for ISKCON in South Africa—how you want us to grasp the opportunity to show how Kṛṣṇa consciousness can save the world by providing the vision for a misdirected civilization. You wanted *varṇāśrama-dharma,* and we pray for the mercy of Your Divine Grace to be instruments in bringing about such social change in South Africa. The people of South Africa are desperately placing their faith before unqualified leaders, and when the people are thoroughly frustrated, there is a good chance that they may pursue less conventional avenues for guidance. Please guide and bless your GBCs and other authorized agents to allow ISKCON to reach its full potential so that when that day comes, Kṛṣṇa consciousness can thus be seen as a genuine solution to the current state of affairs. And please allow us to be their humble servants.

Your hopeful servants at ISKCON Pretoria, South Africa.  
(written by Jīvānanda dāsa)

## Puncak

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Parama Guru Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are an ocean of mercy. Permit us to worship and glorify you. We aspire to always reside under the shelter of your divine lotus feet.

For us, your spiritual grandchildren, it is very special good fortune to be able to offer our homage unto you, whose glories are spread all over the three worlds. At the same time, we feel that all the language in the universe is not enough to sufficiently glorify you.

No person other than Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself is capable of reciprocating the service you have performed in saving fallen souls like us, to the point that gradually we are being delivered from this horrible ocean of material suffering. So many souls have incurred such a deep debt of gratitude to you that they will never be able to repay that debt.

There is nothing higher or more profound than your glories, to the point that even Kṛṣṇa Himself follows your requests to accept us, who are full of sins, unworthy, and incompetent to worship His Lordship. It is your glories and your glories alone that have influenced Kṛṣṇa to accept us in this way.

At this point, we who have this unpayable debt of gratitude offer prayers from the depths of our hearts that it will be Śrī Kṛṣṇa who will pay those debts to you.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! The entire world is indebted to you. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is famous as being more merciful than even Kṛṣṇa Himself, and you are even more merciful than Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, for you have distributed the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. No intelligent person can deny this fact. May your glories be sung all over this universe.

There is nothing I can offer to reciprocate for what you have done for us. I simply surrender unconditionally to you.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please always be merciful to us. Please always distribute your mercy, so that Śrī Kṛṣṇa will be pleased to reinstate us as His eternal servants

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant spiritual grandchildren at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Temple in Puncak, Bogor, Indonesia.  
(written by Matsya dāsa)

## Rāḍhadeśa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet, which have traversed this globe, blessing all who were able to take advantage with the understanding that the goal of life is to serve Kṛṣṇa and to love Him. Without your causeless mercy, this would have remained a secret and we would have been doomed to get lost in the world of illusion.

I’m writing you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day on behalf of all the devotees of Rāḍhadeśa, one of the many homes you have created for your followers. From your humble start in New York in 1966, we have seen big projects develop, and even bigger projects are developing now: Bhubanesvar, Bangalore, New Delhi. All this is being done for your pleasure and by your potency. Devotees have been working hard achieving apparently impossible tasks by your mercy. Trying to analyze where the foundation of that mercy lays, I come to the conclusion that it’s found in your basic instructions: chant sixteen rounds, attend *maṅgala-ārati,* worship the guru and Kṛṣṇa, observe the four regulative principles, and hear *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the association of devotees. This is not a new invention; you modeled them on the instructions of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and you mentioned that if we do not follow this we will have to fall down. There is so much potency in this simple morning program! I can see that devotees who faithfully follow this process can take on so much to help spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I get a glimpse of it myself whenever I actively engage in this *sādhana* process and make it a meditation…

…to glorify you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by singing the *Gurv-aṣṭakam* prayers, and realizing that Nṛsiṁha-deva is here and wherever I go to protect me, and by praying to Śrīmatī Tulasī-devī, who is so dear to Lord Keśava, to engage me in devotional service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in the groves of Vraja-dhāma,to engage me in chanting the *mahā-mantra* with rapt attention, trying to avoid the ten offenses, and to help me absorb my mind in the beautiful forms of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, Lalitā, Viśākhā, Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā; and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, remembering that I am their eternal servant, praying for some service at Their lotus feet. Then offering myself at your divine lotus feet in submission and surrender, begging that you will accept me. Then joining together in a blissful *kīrtana,* chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, remembering that Kṛṣṇa’s name is Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa is His name, and that Kṛṣṇa appeared to us in His Deity form by your grace so we can serve Him. Then hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* with care and attention and holding on to some instructions to inspire us to remember Kṛṣṇa during our daily duties.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us so much to help us on our path back to Godhead. Many times you even told different devotees that if they follow this process of observing the four regulative principles, chanting sixteen good rounds daily, and following your instructions, that you will personally take them back to Godhead in this very lifetime. This was your promise. Knowing I am not qualified, but hearing that promise from you, and knowing who you are—you who are so dear to Kṛṣṇa—and thus knowing that Kṛṣṇa will keep your promise intact, gives me the conviction that it is possible. I just have to take care of my part of the deal, which is to follow you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We have seen, in many special cases, how devotees who had drifted away from devotional service were drawn back to your lotus feet at the time of death and were able to remember you and Kṛṣṇa while leaving the body. Therefore we have faith that others, who have left due to unfortunate circumstances or by our mismanagement, will not be forgotten by you and therefore also not by Kṛṣṇa, who will give them special mercy because you are that special pure devotee who keeps Kṛṣṇa under your control by your love.

I would like to make a pledge on this auspicious day on behalf of this small Rāḍhadeśa *yātrā,* of which I am just an insignificant member. Following the mood of our temple president, who is very strict in his *sādhana,* I would like to pledge that by maintaining and increasing our individual attendance of your morning program, we would like to please you and serve you. And we have faith that by our combined effort all our *anarthas*, misunderstandings, and deficiencies in running the *yātrā* will disappear. We will be blissful in maintaining and increasing our relationships with you and with one another, creating such a Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere in the temple that the guests will never forget their visit to Rāḍhadeśa, and they will want to join us somehow or other.

This humble servant of yours is offering this prayer to you, that you may please allow her to be of some service to you in this nice Rāḍhadeśa Dhāma.

The devotees in Rāḍhadeśa, Belgium.  
(written by Nikuñjavāsinī-devī dāsī)

## Rome

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your humble servants at the Rome *yātrā* pray for your blessings, that we may feel a part of your great, worldwide family of Vaiṣṇavas and never fail to bring it satisfaction.

The serenity and simplicity found in the rural lifestyle of Śrī Gaura Mandala (near Rome) facilitate spiritual life and daily regenerate your *saṅkīrtana* preachers for their unflinching propagation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in one of the most materialistic cities in the world. At the same time, many university students and other young people visit the Hare Kṛṣṇa Forum, a preaching center in the heart of Rome, and in this way approach Kṛṣṇa consciousness on friendly terms. They are gradually taking up the practice of *bhakti-yoga,* forming, on their own, cell groups animated by the dynamism of your ever-expanding movement.

The fruits of your unconditional mercy invested in your disciples continue to reveal themselves, and you personally continue to offer this mercy to all through your books. Just as all devotees felt completely happy in your presence and fully satisfied in their aspirations, similarly devotees and others in contact with ISKCON realize their true identities to be spiritual and full of bliss.

The spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness generates an infinity of spiritual activity, which the whole of humanity benefits from. Finally true brotherhood, peace, and prosperity can be known.

With the promotion of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* in all its cultural manifestations, we may attract people from all levels of society, inviting them to glorify the holy name of the Lord.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu entrusted to you, who are free of all egoism, a treasure, which you in turn distributed without reservation or discrimination to the Western world, terminally ill with egoism.

With your heart constantly absorbed in the desire of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you realized all your spiritual aspirations, giving the example to humanity of how to achieve the perfection of life. We pray on this auspicious day that we may come to know a fraction of your divine sentiment.

“O Lord and master! When will this devotee be blessed by obtaining your mercy? I am low, fallen, and devoid of all strength and intelligence. Please make me your beloved servant.” (*Gurudeva* \[from *Śaraṇāgati*\])

Your aspiring servants in Rome, Italy.

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to the Paramahaṁsa Ṭhākura who came to the Western world on behalf of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura! You were thinking of going to Trinidad, but there was some restriction. So, being just like Nārada Muni, who can go anywhere he wants, you came to America.

By your presentation of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* we get an immediate glimpse of the spiritual world. By your building a house in which the whole world can live we have a chance to increase that glimpse.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to you that we may never fall from the onward path of spiritual perfection, and that we may always see that you are personally still guiding us back home, back to Godhead.

Nara-Nārāyaṇa Prabhu once asked you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, is it possible that I can become a pure devotee.” You answer. “Why not? You’re following the process. But it may take some time.” Please help us be patient, knowing that you gave us the full process for complete perfection.

Once in Chicago just after finishing class, you looked at Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī and asked, “Can I go now?” Also at that time in Chicago a devotee was thinking he was going to die. He asked for *bābājī* initiation. At the initiation you said, “Because he was thinking he was going to die…” I always wondered why, while you were giving the initiation, you used the word “was.” The devotee didn’t die after all.

*yāṅra citte kṛṣṇa-premā karaye udaya*  
*tāṅra vākya, kriyā, mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya*

**“Even the most learned man cannot understand the words, activities, and symptoms of a person situated in love of Godhead.” (*****Madhya*** **23.39)**

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to always have firm faith in your words and activities, even if we don’t fully understand them.

Your devotees in New Kṛṣṇaloka, St. Louis, Missouri, USA.  
(written by Cakrī dāsa)

## San Jose

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

ISKCON was established for our benefit and for the benefit of others as well. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* describes reality distinguished from illusion for the welfare of all.

Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā:* “The self-realized souls can impart knowledge to you because they have seen the truth.” You could clearly see the truth and talk to Him as well. Knowing His desire and empowered by the *paramparā,* you set out to preach in the West.

The word *tīrtha,* which is generally translated as “holy place,” also means “ford,” or a shallow place where one can cross a river. The *sādhu* is considered a holy place because he carries the Lord in his heart. The *sādhu* is also known as a *tīrtha* because he can help people cross over the material ocean to the spiritual world. You gave everybody you met and hundreds of future generations the opportunity to cross over the material ocean by letting them know what was on the other side.

You arrived in the United States with the First Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is considered to be the literary incarnation of the Lord; the First Canto is compared to the Lord’s lotus feet. Lord Brahmā said that “for those who have accepted the boat of the lotus feet of the Lord…the ocean of material existence is like the water contained in a calf’s hoof-print.”

Similarly, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings, “The lotus feet of the spiritual master are the only way by which we can obtain pure devotional service. I bow down to his lotus feet with great awe and reverence. By his grace, one can cross the ocean of material suffering.” Your lotus feet are also a boat that can carry us across the ocean of material existence.

When you came to America, you were surprised to find the children of wealthy men disenchanted and living in the streets like beggars. Material success had not brought their parents happiness, so they sought something higher in an effort to escape the same fate. Unfortunately, there are no material means to escape the ocean of the material world, in which every materialist is drowning. One has to cross the material ocean with the help of the Lord and His devotees. As Rāma had Nala build a bridge across the Indian Ocean, Lord Caitanya had you build a bridge across the material ocean.

For your pleasure, we have opened the doors to a new branch of ISKCON in San Jose, California. Many Indian people have moved to San Jose to be part of the computer industry. Because of the great success of ISKCON in India, they know ISKCON already. They are very happy to hear that ISKCON has opened a new center in San Jose.

We are going to the universities in the area to give students an opportunity to engage in devotional service. We distribute *prasādam* and books at Stanford, where Chelsea Clinton goes to school. We also go to San Jose State University, UC Santa Cruz, and a few community colleges.

Please give us your blessings and allow us to expand our center and to expand people’s minds beyond the needs of their senses. We are your servants, and we have taken shelter of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet are the bridge that spans the ocean of material existence. Please allow us to be the bridge to your lotus feet.

Your servants at ISKCON San Jose, California, USA.

## Scotland

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. We are lame and ill advised, so please be our constant director.

This year has brought about more changes, which we hope will bring pleasure to you in the future. More properties have been acquired to help accommodate the devotees, and each of these properties is fully paid for and in the name of your ISKCON.

The Deity worship has increased, more devotees are taking part, and Their Lordships now have more than thirty day outfits.

Many devotees had the opportunity to visit Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* this year also. It has made a great impression on them and has connected them more with the truth that Kṛṣṇa really exists. You made these wonderful places available to the entire world. All glories to you!

A new fifty-foot-long Tulasī house was erected for the pleasure of Tulasī-devī and yourself. Tulasī is growing nicely. Some are seven feet high and about the same distance wide. She has now mercifully manifested in about 150 forms.

Your books are still being distributed, although, sad to say, not quite in the same quantity as before. But we hope to change that soon.

The Nama-haṭṭa meetings are being maintained, and the members are becoming more active.

One congregation member has taken responsibility for cultivating the 2 1/2 acres of land round the temple, and we hope that by the end of the year our worshipful Lordships will be receiving offerings made from produce grown on the land.

We thank you for providing so much for all of us here, Śrīla Prabhupāda. By your mercy Karuna Bhavan has become a very safe Kṛṣṇa conscious place to stay and serve. We hope that we can increase the preaching here in Scotland and in northern England for your pleasure. We will try. We want to see many souls come and take the priceless gifts that you are offering. To become your instruments would be the perfection of our lives, and hopefully we will surrender to being your willing instruments.

We are hoping to be able to please you in the coming year, and in that way get your great mercy and the mercy of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Khoda Nitāi-Māyāpura-sasha. Please continue to be merciful upon us; otherwise we are useless specimens. We have no good qualities, but we do want to develop the desire to continually please you.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servants in Scotland.

## Seattle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, the devotees of Seattle ISKCON, offer our most humble obeisances to you on your appearance day.

Because we are few in number, we are limited in the magnitude of service we can offer you. Our best offering to you is to facilitate anyone who wants to serve in the temple you started twenty-five years ago. Trying to give up our false ego, we are rendering service to you in this temple. We always wish that many of your disciples who have left the movement will come back to help us. The few devotees who remain understand that by continuing the effort you started here, our legacy will be part of the solution rather than the problem. So our offering to you is the sum of the cooperation we show one another as Godbrothers and -sisters and the totality of our efforts to push on the movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your humble servants of the Seattle ISKCON Temple, Washington, USA.  
(written by Śacīsuta dāsa)

## Singapore

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

On this auspicious occasion, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we are very much honored to be given this great opportunity to glorify you, our divine spiritual master, Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Firstly we were told not to have *harināma* on the streets. That we tolerated, as you often stressed that book distribution was like the big *mṛdaṅga*. Then book distribution too was banned. Again we tolerated, trying to reach out to whomever we could meet. *Yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa / āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*.

Then we were told we could no longer have association from any outside Vaiṣṇavas. What more can we tolerate, Śrīla Prabhupāda! But just to uphold your divine missionary spirit, we are still hanging on. We know this strength that we are experiencing is only dripping into our hearts from your divine lotus feet. There is no other means of mercy for us to go on except you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your divine *vāṇī* is the only solace for all of us. Kindly bestow onto us some intelligence so we can impart your teachings to all the conditioned souls here, to save them once and for all.

Praying for your causeless mercy,  
Your servants in Singapore.

## Slovenia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which are the shelter for all the three worlds. All glories to this glorious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

By reading your books, we are again and again struck by the importance of your pure Kṛṣṇa conscious instructions. They are the real medicine for the hearts of materially infected persons. And because you know the bliss of service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, you went through so many pains to introduce it to the forgetful conditioned souls. By following even a few of your instructions, one can return to Kṛṣṇa. He who sincerely follows your instructions will sooner or later see Kṛṣṇa face to face and feel your presence, because you are nondifferent from your books and instructions.

You are giving Kṛṣṇa to the world—a matchless gift—as it was written on the first temple that you opened in New York.

How can we repay you for what you have done for us? We can’t, of course. Still, every one of us knows well how to satisfy you: by helping to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name will be sung in every town and village. And we can help in this effort only because you gave us the big *mṛdaṅga*—distribution of your wonderful books, the holy name, and *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this glorious day our prayer is that we may serve you with full enthusiasm and in that way reach perfection.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us everything and allowing us to serve you within your ISKCON movement.

Your eternally indebted servants in Ljubljana, Slovenia.  
(written by Udāra-mānasa dāsa)

## Sofia

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the transcendental dust of Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance in this blessed world!

The pure devotee’s character is unfathomable. There is no limit to the volumes of literature that one could write describing his glories. That’s how it is possible to continue year after year compiling your Vyāsa-pūjā book. Every year the devotees contribute to spreading your glories by adding more profound realizations about your divine personality. And yet there is no end to this process. There is no such thing as running the risk of repeating ourselves, for any aspect of your wonderful personality becomes even sweeter as we repeat it.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in this year’s offering to you we’ll try to reflect on the culture you have given us. One meaning of the word “culture” in the Oxford dictionary is “the customs, civilization, and achievements of a particular time or people.” And what is this culture you have given us? It is the culture of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In the introduction to your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you say that we must propagate the cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. From your direct representatives, your disciples, we’ve heard that by cult you mean the culture. And what is the culture of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu?

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*****Madhya*** **7.128)**

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we got to know Kṛṣṇa. Our real life began from the moment we first got in touch with you through your wonderful books. You taught, teach, and will continue to teach us about even the smallest detail of the real culture, the eternal culture of the spirit soul. From you we learn how preach, how to eat, how to sleep, how to speak, how to dress, how to read, how to cook, how to sing, how to dance, how to play instruments, how to paint, how to teach, how to write, how to study, and so on. You teach us how to do everything in such a way as to please Kṛṣṇa. There is no sphere of life you didn’t instruct us on. By your mercy our lives have been completely transformed. Instead of being materialists completely engrossed in the confines of matter, we are now spiritually cultured devotees of Kṛṣṇa, who in turn can attract others to Kṛṣṇa’s culture.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we ask you for your blessings to spread this culture you’ve given us to every nook and corner of the world.

Jagat-guru, savior of the whole universe, Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!!

Your helpless servants in the Sofia temple, Bulgaria.

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Born in complete darkness, declared dead shortly thereafter, I barely survived. How did it happen that I could ever meet Your Divine Grace? I was dreaming my youth away, so how could it ever happen that you finally sent me out to distribute your books? I have seen my life change all these years, so how could it ever happen that I managed to stay under the shelter of your lotus feet? Some come, some go—how is it possible for me to still enjoy the safety of your movement? As I stand in front of you, embarrassed by material nature, how come you still accept my service?

I fail to understand the greatness of Your Divine Grace. With the bright light of your instructions you illuminate the darkness of this age, providing a steady hold for those who take them in an uncompromised way. Concepts come and concepts go. The endless speeches of confused ones looking to satisfy their desire to be distinct fade away as your strong voice resounds with clarity and depth, carrying the most sublime message of the *śāstras*. No place for doubts, no place for personal speculation is left as you establish the true position of the soul as the eternal servant at Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me be more patient. My life in this body is shortened day by day by time, and I would like to see your words coming true. I would like to see a society where *brāhmaṇas* can preach under the protection of *kṣatriyas* while *vaiśyas* are charitably assisted by loyal *śūdras,* all engaged in devotional service. It hurts to hear the talk of the *śūdras* while *kṣatriyas* quarrel, and to see the greed of the *vaiśyas* while *brāhmaṇas* retire in resignation. I would like to walk through a city where all citizens bow down in your temple, praising the glories of your books for the next ten thousand years.

I would like to walk through this city hearing the sound of your walking stick as you take your morning walk, following behind you and your senior disciples, hearing you explain how life comes from life and how we should serve Śrī Kṛṣṇa and not money. The last walk on which I followed behind you was 26 years ago. When will I be able to walk behind you again? How simple life was then! Your presence kept the sound of the speculators far away, filling the atmosphere with the Holy Name. In your presence even the most difficult obstacles appeared like small pebbles on the road. Even people kicking at our *saṅkīrtana* van, a dark man pointing his gun at me, a hideous priest shouting at us along with his followers, trying to stop our *saṅkīrtana*—all these figures were just mere pictures from Māyā’s dream world, simply giving us more strength to go on with greater determination. As you wrote to one of your disciples, “With the kick of my foot I can remove all the obstacles from you.”

Now Māyā tries to make the unimportant seem important and the important unimportant. But when we open your books, Māyā dwindles away. When we close your books, she again comes close. Outside of your books, madness prevails. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me become patient, help me remember Your Divine Grace walking in the heat of New Delhi distributing *Back to Godhead* magazines to busy *karmīs*. You kept on walking, tolerating the heat, so please help me keep on walking, tolerating the cruel, cold impersonalism surrounding us on all sides.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for keeping me engaged in your service. And thank you so much for giving me the association of my very special Godbrothers, who all reflect different parts of your amazing personality. Whatever this spiderlike soul can do to assist you by pushing a few grains of sand into the ocean of illusion to help build the bridge for the conditioned souls kept imprisoned under Rāvaṇa-like leaders, so that they can cross this ocean of nescience—this alone has some value, with your blessings only. Please allow me to be part of this wonderful mission you gave to us.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servants in Stockholm, Sweden.  
(written by Maṇidhara dāsa)

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all the faithful devotees in this *yātrā,* I would, in no particular order, like to thank you for everything.

Thank you for giving us the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā, Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Kṛṣṇa* book, *Nectar of Devotion, Nectar of Instruction, Back to Godhead* magazine, and all your other books, letters, lectures, morning walks, *darśanas,* etc.

Thank you for *prasādam: halavah*, *purīs, subjī, kicherī, pakorās, samosās, sandeśa, rasagullā,* sweet rice, *gulab-jāmuns, laddu, lāssi,* etc.

Thank you for the temple: the altar, the Deities, the *vyāsāsana,* Tulasī, the smell of incense, the *caraṇāmṛta,* the sound of the conch, the beautiful *cāmara* and peacock fan, etc.

Thank you for dhotis, saris, *śikhās, tilaka, japa-mālā,* neck beads, *śuci*-ness, etc.

Thank you for the *mahā-mantra*, for *japa, kīrtana, harināma, bhajana, mṛdaṅga, karatālas,* etc.

Thank you for festivals: Gaura-pūrṇimā, Janmāṣṭamī, Rādhāṣṭamī, Govardhana-pūjā, the appearance days of Lord Nṛsiṁha, Lord Rāma, Vāmanadeva, Varāhadeva, etc.

Thank you for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and all Her associates, for Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the other members of the Pañca-tattva—Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, Śrī Advaita Ācārya, Śrī Gadādhara Paṇḍita, and Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura.

Thank you for all the *ācāryas:* Lord Brahmā, Nārada Muni, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Mādhavendra Purī, the six Gosvāmīs, Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Śyāmānanda Prabhu, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Śrīla Gaurakiśora dāsa Bābājī, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, etc.

Thank you for the devotees, the devotional family, and their all important association: the gurus, the *sannyāsīs,* the *brahmacārīs, brahmacāriṇīs,* the *gṛhasthas,* Godbrothers, Godsisters, Godcousins, Goduncles; older, younger, the neophytes and the advanced.

Thank you for the *vratas* and the *yātrās:* Ekādaśī, Cāturmāsya, Parikrama, Padayātrā, Ratha-yātrā, etc.

Thank you for the *āśramas,* temples, farms, restaurants, shops, schools, communities, etc.

Thank you for the temple program: *maṅgala-ārati, tulasī-pūjā, japa,* class, *darśana, guru-pūjā, kīrtana,* etc.

Thank you for the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the opportunity for service in His mission: from book publication to book distribution, from *harināma* to Deity worship, from washing the pots to managing the temple, from *prasādam* distribution to membership development, from farms to *gurukulas,* in all *varṇas* and through all *āśramas*.

Thank you for the cars, computers, printers, fax machines, TVs, videos, desks, chairs, etc.

Thank you for the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, the Bhaktivedanta Archives, ITV, the Bhaktivedanta Institute, the Bhaktivedanta Charity Trust, IFR, Food for Life, etc.

Thank you for all the Deities you established all around the world: Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Rādhā-Rasabihārī, Rādhā-Govinda, Rādhā-Londonīśvara, Rādhā-Parisīśvara, Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa, Rādhā-Gopīnātha, Rādhā-Vallabha, Rādhā-Gokulānanda, Rādhā-Giridhārī, Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Sītā-Rāma, Pañca-Tattva, Gaura-Nitāi, etc.

Thank you for the holy *dhāmas:* Śrī Māyāpur *dhāma,* Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma*, Śrī Jagannātha Purī *dhāma,* etc.

Thank you for the Yamunā, the Gaṅgā, the Jalaṅgī, Rādhā-kuṇḍa, Śyāma-kuṇḍa, and Govardhana Hill.

Thank you for the Māyāpur project, the Māyāpur Festival, and the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium.

Thank you for ISKCON: the society, the institution, the GBC, the temple presidents, the organizational structure and managerial authority.

Thank you for the *guru-paramparā* and its continuation: the disciplic succession of *śikṣā* and *dīkṣā*.

Thank you for all these things I have mentioned, as well as all for the many things I have overlooked.

Thank you, above all, for yourself, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and all the mercy that you have brought, all the mercy of which we, and the whole world, are forever made and will remain forever grateful.

Thank you.

Yours sincerely,  
The devotees of New Gokula, Newcastle, and the Sydney yātrā, Australia.  
(written by Ātmārāma dāsa)

## Taiwan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances at your feet. All glories, all glories to Your Divine Grace!

One day when I was out distributing books, a little boy was standing next to me looking on in amazement. After finishing my talk with the man I had met, I turned to the boy.

“Hello!” I said in Chinese.

“Are you Jesus?” he asked me.

“Well, not exactly,” I said. “But we are good friends with him and are doing similar service. You see \[I showed him a picture of you in which you are sitting in meditation, softly glowing\], this gentleman is our teacher. He and Jesus are very good friends. We are together serving God.”

Understanding that he must have heard about Jesus from school or friends, I was careful not to break the appreciation he might have. In the course of our talk we said “Hare Kṛṣṇa!” a few times together and then I left him, wishing him good luck. Just imagine, I thought, if that same boy would have run into a “Christian” and the boy would have asked him, “Are you Kṛṣṇa?” I wonder what reply he would have gotten. We, your followers, can reply to questions people confront us with in a mature way because we have learned from you what to say. You are making it very clear. You are not propagating and defending some personal needs or concoctions.

You have no competitor. You are teaching and living the truth, for the glorification of the truth. You give us *rāja-vidyā,* the culmination of all that there is to know, in plain terms. “Ignorance is the disease,” you explained, “and knowledge the medicine.” Sharing your knowledge by word and example for the benefit of all is your mission.

By the mercy of you and your disciples, this is also going on here in the wilderness of modern-day Taiwan, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It would be too early to shout victory, but there is some encouraging progress. “Hare Kṛṣṇa” is becoming more and more favorably known among the local people. A group of about twenty youngsters regularly visit your center. All of them are nice and gentle people who find what they have always been looking for in your books and your movement. The same is true for some Indian friends who live and work here. We are all benefited eternally by dint of your causeless mercy. More of your books are being translated into Chinese and are circulating. We have successfully published a magazine in Chinese and will soon have a regular newsletter for our many expanding contacts. The preaching here is starting to take shape.

You have made the design, and we aspire to execute it. It is looking beautiful as the plan unfurls slowly but steadily. On our own we are undeserving and not qualified to achieve anything substantial. There is no hope for us apart from begging you and all other devotees to please keep us somehow or other engaged in your service. We apologize for sometimes making havoc.

Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Taiwan.  
(written by Dayāl Nitāi dāsa)

## Tampa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you on your most auspicious appearance day!

We are jubilantly celebrating with great ecstasy your divine appearance. You have saved our lives from a perpetual miserable existence of birth and death.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have single-handedly enveloped the entire world in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Though you gave up your mortal body twenty-one years ago, your heart, The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, goes on beating like a drum getting louder and stronger. One day every person in the world will say, “What is that wonderful sound, so divine? It is Śrīla Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda still playing his *mṛdaṅga*.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the transcendental Pied Piper leading everyone into the carefree spiritual world, beyond birth and death. Kṛṣṇa’s flute-song is emanating from your books, enchanting everyone the world over. You are forcefully pulling all souls, willing or not, out of illusion so they may never suffer again in this miserable material life.

“If they read just one line of my books, their life will be perfected” you told us.

“There is no other literature in the world that compares to these books,” you said.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we bow down before you and ask you for the exclusive transcendental privilege to sell your books. Please give us this blessing.

Should I dare ask the devotees if there is anything as important as your books and their distribution? Can anyone say?

I don’t think so.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very, very small and insignificant, but if we preach your message our lives become glorious. Let us challenge ourselves, disciples and grand-disciples, to see how many books we can read and distribute throughout this short lifetime. Certainly we will then be worthwhile disciples and not worthless!

Your devotees in Tampa, Florida, USA.  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tauranga

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the poor and meek. By his mercy, a lame man can cross over mountains and a dumb man can compose eloquent poetry.”**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and repeated obeisances at your soft, conquering lotus feet.

We all want happiness and success, so you gave us the formula. Chant at least sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* a day, avoiding offenses, follow the four regulative principles, and always be busy in devotional service in the association of Vaiṣṇavas.

We may struggle or pretend or hope to be devotees, but we only have spiritual life as much as we apply your *vāṇī,* your words, in practice! We are guaranteed protection, security, relief, happiness, and complete realized knowledge—all desirable things—by becoming very simple: simply sold-out servants of Your Divine Loving Grace’s instructions. You likened our neck beads to collars signifying us as Kṛṣṇa’s dogs. (“A dog,” you said, “has one good quality: he is loyal to his master.”) We aspire for such loyalty to you and faith in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You are the epitome and embodiment of all good qualities and auspicious potencies; you manifest all the twenty-six qualities of a perfect servant of the Supreme Person. You lived Kṛṣṇa consciousness absolutely! You were with Kṛṣṇa totally, exchanging with Him, at every moment, though to our eyes you seemed to be in the material world. How can we possibly understand?

How was it possible that you tolerated our shameful condition? How could you, who were constantly enjoying the unlimited ecstasy of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s perfect personal association and mercy, stand to be here with us, even for a moment? It seems impossible, certainly impossible, to understand. It is because of Kṛṣṇa’s inconceivable and unlimited mercy and potency, manifested in and through your person. By getting a slight inkling of the personality of the pure devotee, one can begin to understand the qualities and nature of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself.

“By the mercy of the spiritual master, one gets the Mercy of Kṛṣṇa, but without his mercy no one can make even a little spiritual advancement.” You offer us unconditional compassion—which has to be understood as real love—and we desire to become desperate for even a reflection of that mercy! You are the most trustworthy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so please, please, please keep helping us place our complete trust, faith, and loyalty in you!

So many of your trusting and faithful followers are following your basic, all-powerful instructions, and thus they can give others more and more access to your association through their examples and shared potency. Those who are gaining your mercy through serving you selflessly and unreservedly are most important for all of us. Their association is absolutely necessary for our advancement, our ability to take up your “call to arms.”

We have to admit our need for advanced association to be able to stand and fight, to become victorious in the vicious battle with the material energy. We cannot think that you want us to serve you alone, or that we are independently able to do so! You never taught us to ignore, reject, or neglect the association that you created. Of course, you emphasized the importance of discrimination, even in association, but not as an excuse to avoid humbling ourselves to see and serve the greater good.

You created a society because that’s how it is in Kṛṣṇa’s place: everyone serving together, helping and serving each other in devotional service! (Lord Caitanya personally taught that perfection is to serve the devotees of the devotees.) Wherever you went, Śrīla Prabhupāda, during your manifest sojourn here, you automatically caused such an atmosphere of the greater family: you lived in the spiritual world, so naturally it manifested in your presence. I remember being with you and feeling that there wasn’t a problem in the world. Every sincere soul who had your association will attest to this phenomenon. So, by admitting our dependence on the appreciation of and service to our fellow devotees, we can start to become heroes and heroines in your army, in your mission to inundate the universe with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The association of your followers who are successful is essential for our success in pleasing you. We need to humble ourselves to appreciate them, to learn from them, and to serve them as we desire to serve you, and that mood of co-operation will certainly make you most pleased. You made that clear throughout the time of your physical presence, and especially in your final instructions. You were very happy when we worked together respectfully, co-operatively, without criticism or envy. You gave the example of the two groups of children on each side of the father, massaging him. When they co-operated, it benefited the father, but when they bickered about the massaging, inadvertently they struck the father, causing him harm. You knew perfectly well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that our humble co-operation would speed the spreading of your ISKCON *saṅkīrtana* movement, and conversely that only by dissension within could it be stopped.

If we’re foolishly unwilling to dismantle the obstruction of our own pride and false ego, than that becomes an external impediment to the progressive expansion of your mission. Do we really want to risk inciting your displeasure? What could be more detrimental for us? In 1970, you “excommunicated” a small group of he leading devotees because you saw that their preaching was a threat to the movement!

Your glories are spreading more and more, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We know that whatever good manifests, it’s a sign of your qualities shining through, directly or indirectly. Whether it’s favorable media coverage, like a National Radio program about ISKCON’s cow protection program and farm projects in New Zealand, or a TV program in which a devotee and nondevotee trade places for a day, or a national news broadcast about the high-tech Hare Kṛṣṇa building, the wondrous Glory of India Temple in Delhi, and using everything in the service of God, we see you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your awesome qualities, personality, perfect philosophy, and your living of it, shining through.

You have given us everything. You have made the impossible possible, so it is in fact possible to humble ourselves. We beg that you continue to bless us to become more deeply grateful, and thus serious about pleasing you with our every thought, word, and deed.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories to you!

Your lowly disciples and grand-disciples in Tauranga, Bay of Plenty, New Zealand

## Tenerife

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Some years ago we came to this island with the hope of establishing a temple, and now by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and your blessings our dream is about to become reality.

First we had a small-scale preaching in a rented house, then we rented a big flat downtown, and last year we purchased a small house near the city.

Next we want to sell this house and move the temple to a place downtown, realizing that that will be the best thing for the preaching (as you recommend always in your letters).

These years have sometimes been very hard, full of problems arising because we forgot that you are our real shelter. But we have also relished the sweet nectar of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We are trying to put you in the center of our efforts; then everything will surely unfold nicely.

Eager to receive your special mercy,

Your servants in Tenerife.  
(written by Jagannātha Miśra dāsa)

## Toronto

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our humble obeisances on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The Glory of ISKCON**

Having been asked to write your Vyāsa-pūjā offering this year for Toronto, the temple that you prayed for, I wanted to share with the members of your ISKCON my realization of your incredible mercy in bestowing and installing the presiding Deities of our movement, which you strategically placed in each major city of the world. They are truly the glory of ISKCON.

Your ISKCON has grown to more than three hundred temples and forty farms, which are really the homes of our installed Lordships and Their devotees. Each day the devotees around the world engage in Deity worship, dressing and preparing Their Lordships to receive Their guests, both Eastern and Western, who are arriving because of the preaching efforts.

One day the populations of these cities will, by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and the preaching of the devotees, become lovers of Kṛṣṇa and surrender at His lotus feet.

Please accept this poem, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as an offering of our appreciation of your great effort to save us all.

Rādhā–Kṣīracora-Gopinātha, New Remuṇā Dhāma (Toronto)

**An Appreciation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Mercy**

*O Gopīnātha!*  
You are our Deity fully installed,  
Presiding with Rādhārāṇī by Your side.  
You give Your blessings to all who come,  
To receive Your mercy within their lives.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Six times per day we offer you food,  
Six times per day *ārati* is performed,  
In the morning You are bathed and dressed,  
And in the evening You are put to rest.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Lord of the *gopīs,* You have come  
To melt our hearts made out of stone,  
You simply stand, flute in hand,  
Destroying the sins of the fallen souls.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Some bow down before You awkwardly,  
Some close their eyes and join their palms,  
While You and Rādhārāṇī stand,  
Bestowing the pleasure of Your *darśan*.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
To manifest right here like this  
By the grace and mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda—  
How kind You are, O Lord of Love!  
They can finally rest their eyes on God.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
They look and see the miracle  
Of your appearance now beheld.  
Devotees pray they will not forget You,  
Blessed Pair of their remembrance.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
In bitter cold and driving rain,  
In sunlight or in dark,  
They come in awe and reverence,  
To have You melt their hearts.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
All day and night for twenty-six years,  
Your *pūjārīs* have worked hard  
To keep Your schedule on time  
And dress You in the finest cloth.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Fresh flower garlands, jewels, and crowns,  
Fresh sandalwood with Tulasī,  
Grace Your lotus feet with ankle bells,  
Bestowing Your love upon Your devotees.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
The devotees dance and chant Your names,  
The devotees sing Your charms,  
The *mṛdaṅga* and the *kartāls* combine  
In the ecstasy of *kīrtana’s* mercy.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
O cowherd boy and cowherd maiden,  
You bring such gladness to this world;  
You enrich us all with song and dance,  
And Your *mahā-prasādam* remnants.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Divine Supreme Personality of Godhead,  
Our dearest friend and well-wisher too,  
Even poetry can unlock  
Only a little of the glory that is You.

*O Gopīnātha!*  
Lord of our hearts,  
Bestow Your kindness upon  
This fool, who attempts to worship You  
By the writing of this simple song.

The devotees at the ISKCON temple in Toronto, Canada.  
(written by Padyāvalī-devī dāsī)

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You gave many instructions we still need to fulfill,  
schools, farms, and *āśramas—*quite a tall bill.  
We’ve accomplished a few, yet so many remain.  
Empower us to move forward and no longer refrain.

Two temples are open and still going strong;  
following your example, we cannot go wrong;  
serving the Lord of the senses instead of our own;  
Vaiṣṇava *saṅga,* no longer alone.

What have we given, what do we lack?  
Desires may come, but we’re fighting back.  
To push on your movement we must remain pure;  
Māyā’s temptations cannot endure.

In all our actions let us glorify you,  
for the public eye sees all that we do.  
Straightforward and honest, no need to hide,  
we must be aboveboard, no longer connive.

Now we’re attempting to purchase a farm—  
dreams of crops, tilling land, and our own cow barn.  
Pushing away from these cities of greed,  
*ugra-karma* dependence, no longer in need.

You said in the future there would be a change,  
a place to take shelter, we should arrange.  
Despite any endeavors we might contrive,  
the world as we know it will no longer survive.

So it’s off to the forest and into the hills,  
no more artificial living and all of its thrills.  
Straw bale and solar power, you are insisting,  
the Lord’s natural gifts, we’re no longer resisting.

We’ll teach by example, as you have shown,  
to offer to Kṛṣṇa what we have grown.  
We’ll prepare for the masses, who you said would come,  
take shelter of ISKCON when there’s nowhere to run.

We’ll not stop endeavoring in any case,  
trying to save this misguided race.  
We beg for your mercy as this project begins,  
for under your guidance everyone wins.

Your servants in Tucson, Arizona.  
(written by Sandāminī-devī dāsī)

## Turkey

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We earnestly offer our most humble obeisances to the lotus feet of you, our dearest *parama-guru,* His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have mercifully saved millions of fallen souls from the hopeless, fruitless, and frustrating struggle for flickering material gain. Somehow or other, by the causeless mercy of Kṛṣṇa, we have become blessed with the association of you, the purest of souls, through your books.

On this occasion of your appearance day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we desire to glorify your merciful pastime of delivering us from the deepest dark well of material existence. Of course, at present it is impossible for us to begin to understand even the smallest fraction of your great mercy, what to speak of writing some appropriate offering that does not fall short of expressing the full extent of your mercy. Nevertheless, we request that, as faulty as this attempt is, you may please excuse us and accept this Vyāsa-pūjā offering with all its shortcomings.

When Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya what he considered the greatest suffering, Rāmānanda replied that separation from a pure devotee constitutes the most painful existence. So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you forewent all personal considerations and came to give people your personal association so they would be relieved of the sufferings of material life. Now we experience this pain of separation from you. Please come again and deliver us from this painful existence.

Your instructions come to us through your books, the production of which you have tirelessly supervised. You have left the distribution of your literature up to us. These transcendental instructions are our eternal link with you and are the only way to relieve society as a whole of its problems, which have arisen due to a lack of education in spiritual science.

You have mercifully expanded yourself into your books; everyone, including us, can have your association simply by reading your books. Lord Caitanya inquired further from Rāmānanda Rāya: “What is the most profitable thing in the world, the essence of all auspicious events?” Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “There is nothing as profitable as the association of pure devotees.”

Therefore, we are simply trying to follow in your footsteps, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and relish the nectar of your kind association, by which we can someday develop love of God.

In the *Vilāpa-kusumāñjali,* Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī says, “I was unwilling to drink the nectar of devotional service possessed by renunciation, but Sanātana Gosvāmī, out of his causeless mercy, made me drink it even though I was otherwise unwilling to do so.” Similarly, by the causeless mercy of your divine influence, those who were completely uninterested in devotional service are now inspired and induced to taste the sweet nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thus, all over the world, *mlecchas, yavanas,* and other unqualified persons are becoming great devotees of the Lord by your influence only.

Cāṇakya Paṇḍita teaches that a liberal person is one who can take gold even from an unclean place. We are all rotten and fallen, but you are so liberal that you are uncovering *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* even from our dirty hearts.

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī says that Lord Caitanya is the most munificent of all incarnations because He is giving freely what no other incarnation has ever given before—*kṛṣṇa-prema*. Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your are so kindly delivering that same love of God to conditioned souls all over the universe, and therefore you are the most munificent of all *jīvas*.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the champion of personalism in the age of impersonalism, voidism, and self-made gods, and sober-minded intellectuals are appreciating more and more the works of Your Divine Grace.

The modern educational system, as you have explained, is a slaughterhouse. The students are devoid of all knowledge of the self as soul, the importance of self-realization, or the glory and opulences of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Thus when a student or professor has the opportunity to receive your books and hear directly from Kṛṣṇa or His representative, Your Divine Grace, about real knowledge—that which frees one from *saṁsāra*—we can directly see the compassion you are showing by distributing the mercy of Lord Caitanya to those who are willing to accept it.

Now in Turkey we are concentrating on preaching to the university students and professors. We are organizing seminars and courses in different colleges and universities. After the seminars, many students and teachers express their desire to have your books. It is very difficult to transport your books from Europe to Turkey; therefore we are establishing a BBT branch right in Turkey. We already have a few translators, who are translating *The Perfection of Yoga*. It will be the first book translated and printed in Turkey. It is our Vyāsa-pūjā offering to you, O Śrīla Prabhupāda. May you bless us that we will be able to translate and print all your books in the Turkish language.

Some devotees are working as teachers in various fields and in this way introducing Kṛṣṇa consciousness and your books to the young intellectuals. These devotees are begging you for your blessings and protection so that they can continue to perform this difficult service for your pleasure, O Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We are also planning to open a restaurant in Istanbul to increase *prasādam* distribution. Our preachers have expressed their desire to have Deities in Istanbul. They feel that this will help increase their preaching. At the same time, Kṛṣṇa is already sending some facilities for establishing Deity worship here. We are begging Kṛṣṇa to allow us to fulfill your long-cherished desire to establish the worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Istanbul-īśvara for the benefit of all Turkish people.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glorification is heard throughout the three worlds, for you are truly the *jagad-guru*. We cannot adequately glorify you on this most auspicious day, being bereft of spiritual vision. Nevertheless, we can reverently appreciate your qualities and example. By your instructions you have placed yourself in our hearts and are constantly guiding us.

We are eternally indebted to Your Divine Grace and cannot repay your mercy.

Please let us take shelter of your divine instructions, making them our very life and soul. Let us pledge ourselves to your service.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories unto you!

Your insignificant servants in Turkey.  
(written by Airāvata dāsa)

## Uddhava-kuṇḍa

Dear Grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to the pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa! All glories to the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON!

You are the one who blessed the whole world. In this material world the conditioned souls cannot know the Absolute Truth. Especially in this Kali-yuga, people are extremely fallen, being blind to the need for self-realization and seeing only sense gratification as their aim in life. In this hellish place, Śrīla Prabhupāda, people are suffering so much because of illusion; they are completely under the control of the material energy.

The most merciful Lord Caitanya and His intimate associates have appeared at this very special time. They came with the wonderful gift for all fallen souls—the *saṅkīrtana* movement. The Lord gave Your Divine Grace the glorious task of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the whole world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our dear Grandfather, it is only by your mercy that we people in Khasadeśa have gotten the chance to perform Kṛṣṇa consciousness, devotional service. We are so fortunate to receive the guidance of your sincere and cheerful servants. Through your intimate servants we have gotten the chance to start devotional service and get connected with the disciplic succession coming from Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself. What we have received till now is all due to your mercy, and what we will receive tomorrow all depends only on your causeless mercy and protection. Please always give us your mercy. With your mercy we can be liberated from this mad world. Also by your mercy there will be more people who will join us and will find the true harmony in life.

Once you said that for you a little straw hut is enough to live in, but if we only have a straw hut, then who will come to join us? In this age people are so maddened by ignorance, but you are so soft-hearted that you give them transcendental vision. You told us that we should give people a very good example. All the devotees are trying to follow in your footsteps to show people a perfect lifestyle—“simple living but high thinking.”

Our devotees in Uddhava-kuṇḍa are all eager to follow in your footsteps, although we are weak in our ability and very inexperienced, and we are under very tight government control. Please bless your grandchildren in Uddhava-kuṇḍa so we can have stronger faith and confidence and can thus help each other. Please feed us with spiritual consciousness, which is already there in our heart. Please bless us to have more association of our spiritual uncles and our Godbrothers and -sisters. By that association we can enjoy spiritual happiness. Please bestow special mercy on the people of Khasadeśa so they can understand the need of going back to our original home in the association of our dear Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Let us always follow your divine instruction and always remember the Lord by chanting His holy names: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Your grandchildren in Uddhava-kuṇḍa, Khasadeśa.

## Uppsala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Ray of Hope**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I have come before you empty-handed, with nothing to offer, no great achievements. No success. No real spiritual advancements. No love of God. I have failed. Still I have come to ask for your grace. What audacity!

Forgive me, Śrīla Prabhupāda—I should not have come before you at all, embarrassed and ashamed like this, a foolish pretender, an imitator. Fools rush in where angels fear to tread.

Showing your infinite kindness and compassion, you came to me in a dream one night. You were sitting on a cushion in a small room in front of a low table, and I sat close beside you. As you calmly spoke, you showed me a paper with some instructions regarding traveling and preaching. I had the feeling that you were encouraging me to take up that service, but I was somewhat confused and emotionally overwhelmed by this very intimate association with you, and so I did not clearly understand what was on your mind. Finally, my voice choking with tears, I said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you had not come, everything would have been meaningless!” You did not say anything: you just sat there peacefully looking down.

You really made me feel like I was a candidate for serving you, and that gave me hope.

Like a lotus flower with no pond  
being scorched by the blazing sun—  
That is my life without you!

Your aspiring servants in Uppsala, Sweden.
