(written by Prapūjaka dāsa)

## Vancouver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda;  
Please accept our humble obeisances on the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Here in Vancouver there are so many different programs going on for your pleasure and for the pleasure of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanamohana:

Sunday Feasts with wonderful *kīrtanas* and classes, Life Membership, youth happenings, Deity worship, gardening, Govinda’s Restaurant restoration, *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* festivals, Ratha-yātrā, college preaching with books and *prasādam* distribution, Food for Life, Nāma-haṭṭa. There is always some wonderful program going on here to glorify Guru and Gaurāṅga. On this auspicious day, the devotees of this *yātrā* would like to offer you a poem expressing their appreciation for all you have done for us.

**Your Giving Ways**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You planted the seeds  
In each of our hearts,  
That we should develop  
Pure love of God.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Then through your books,  
Deities, temples, and your love,  
Your gave us the service  
To water those seeds.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You have looked after our every need.

Comforted, protected  
From Kali-yuga’s storms,  
We live in your ISKCON  
With the Deities of the Lord.

How can we forget you,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Your place in our lives  
Is permanently kept  
Deep in our hearts,  
Without any regret.

Through Guru and Gaurāṅga  
We have all met;  
You followed your guru,  
We follow you.

Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
We would not know the Truth: Kṛṣṇa.

We swing Him on swings,  
He dances on our tongues,  
We eat His Mahā remnants  
And chant in *kīrtan*.

That blue cowherd boy  
Who you love so much,  
By your causeless mercy  
Is now known to us.

Happiness and joy  
Accompany our days,  
Love and devotion  
Decorate our ways.

What words can express?  
What can we say?  
We love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
For your giving ways.

Selflessly you engaged us,  
Total strangers at first,  
Cleaning our hearts,  
Which were dirty and dark.

You saw within us a tiny spark,  
Which you fanned with your caring into a flame,  
Now we sing, dance, and chant  
Kṛṣṇa’s Holy Names.

We pray that we will do this  
Until the end of our days.

Thank you again, Your Divine Grace.

The devotees of ISKCON Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Padyāvalī-devī dāsī)

## Villa Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“Dear …”. Most letters begin with this formula, but in no case is it as proper as when addressed to you. Many people will glorify you today in very knowledgeable ways, citing many scriptural references, and we have also done this in the past. But on this occasion we want to express one simple feeling: that you are for us the most dear because you have given us a real, purposeful, fruitful life, not the routine repetition of chained activity.

You gave us knowledge of the goal, and you gave us the organization to allow us to strive toward that goal. In short, you give us everything of real value that we possess today.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, to thank you properly is not possible.

*Your servants at Villa Vṛndāvana, Italy.*

## Vilnius

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The appearance of a magnanimous personality in this material world and his presence here changes the way of thinking of many living entities and enlightens their lives with a new light.

The people are worn out by material existence, yet moved by your unselfish, pure love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you sincerely endeavored to awaken them by teaching them to seek refuge in the Absolute Truth. In this way you fulfilled the mission of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. But before the people could take refuge in he Absolute Truth, the first of all looked to take shelter in you—your teachings and your conduct.

To come in contact with the spiritual master is possible by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, and only by the mercy of the spiritual master does Kṛṣṇa become understandable.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gathered around yourself people who had a desire for an eternal, blissful life serving Kṛṣṇa. You imbued them with the spiritual knowledge you had received via the *paramparā,* and you also taught them the chanting of the holy names of the Lord. Then you enjoined them to distribute all this treasure to others. Those extraordinary personalities, our spiritual masters, are executing your instructions, and thus the message of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is spreading throughout the material world.

You are the eternal *śikṣā* guru for all people who come to your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Your disciples, our spiritual masters, are like parents to us. They gather us together into one family and guide us on the way to spiritual perfection. Śrīla Prabhupāda, they get their strength from devotion and love for your instructions. They conduct their lives in this mood and pass this mood on to their disciples.

Although now you are not physically present in this world, everyone can still meet you. When people who are reading your books and performing devotional service are tumbled over by trials, they look for the refuge of your lotus feet.

To carry out the mission of preaching is the way to diminish our debt to you, at least by one little drop. Although all of us are full of faults and are so conditioned by material nature, we humbly ask you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your mercy and shelter.

Your servants in Vilnius, Lithuania.

## Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

You told us Vṛndāvana is your home and your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple are your eternal residence. This year, to fulfill your desire and to honor you as the supreme preacher of Lord Caitanya’s mission, devotees ceremoniously installed your *mūrti* in your quarters at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple.

Vṛndāvana is also the home of all your devotees, and we are trying our best to create a welcoming atmosphere for everyone who comes to the temple. We have now carefully researched your instructions for the Vṛndāvana project and are systematically implementing each of your visions for Vṛndāvana. You left us a treasure house of directions for developing the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple, and we are cooperating as a community of mature devotees to expand the Vṛndāvana project according to your plans.

Twice in Vṛndāvana you quoted a little poem to the devotees:

Big monkey,  
Big, big belly;  
Laṇkā jumping,  
Melancholy.

The first incident was just after you opened the temple in April 1975 and then left Vṛndāvana for three months of preaching. A huge amount of work remained to complete the project. The devotees struggled throughout the summer months to prepare the temple, guesthouse, and your house for your return in August. On your arrival, the temple managers proudly showed you all the improvements. On your first morning walk they gave you a tour of the grounds and temple building. A Life Member preaching room had been established at the entrance of the temple, with book displays, etc. It was very attractive, and as you entered the room the devotees expected your appreciation of their efforts. You looked around and saw what everyone else failed to notice. You pointed to some scattered twigs and broken pigeon eggs on the floor. Above, pigeons were nesting on a ledge in the room and had created a mess on the floor during the night. The devotees were speechless and hung their heads in disappointment. You then said, Feed a monkey a lot of chapatis, he gets a big belly. Ask him to jump over the wall, and he just puts his head down. Their reaction of despair elicited from you this Vṛndāvana monkey story to bring them out of their state of inertia.

Then again you quoted this poem when you were describing the *bābājīs* of Vṛndāvana who collect a lot of chapatis throughout the day by begging. But when they are asked to preach the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu—no, that they cannot do.

You used this example in reference to two essential services in Vṛndāvana. First, we must be very enthusiastic to bring the temple development up to your exemplary expectations. And we must also be prepared to spread the glories of Vṛndāvana all over the world, as you did. No melancholy. We have your orders to carry out, and your perfect example to inspire us.

When you were too ill to walk or even eat, you felt you were rotting just sitting in Vṛndāvana. But when plans were made for a preaching tour to London, New York, etc., you again became enlivened. How your face lit up when you spoke to us of going to preach. Even though you had to be carried to the car, you still undertook your last tour in August 1977.

You gave us two fields in Vṛndāvana—your home to maintain and develop, and your mission to preach the glories of Vṛndāvana worldwide. If we can just always remember your determination to please your spiritual master and try to fulfill your orders as successfully as you did his, then our lives will be successful. Please bless us with unfailing enthusiasm to serve your lotus feet.

Your humble servants in Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma, India.

## Washington, D.C.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

We are humbled by this auspicious opportunity to speak a little something on the occasion of your glorious Vyāsa-pūjā. And how glorious it is, Śrīla Prabhupāda! It’s glorious that you have made so many contributions to the planet. It’s hard to imagine what this planet would have been like without your transcendental influence. It’s also hard to imagine the great number of souls worldwide that would yet be suffering in this material existence. And even harder to imagine is how lost these souls would have been without your mercy. How glorious it is that this global house you have built has given shelter to so many who would otherwise have been without spiritual homes and the protection of the Holy Name. How glorious, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you have left a living legacy of your transcendental mission by following the instruction of your spiritual master, writing books in the language of the heart that could be understood by all. How glorious it is that you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness beyond the shores of the Eastern Hemisphere to touch us here in the West and make indelible impressions on us, showing us our true mission. How glorious, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you helped us remember our true identities and began stripping away the layers and lifetimes of amnesia.

And most of all, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how very, very glorious are your spiritual warriors, whom you personally trained and empowered. It is truly glorious that they have remained steadfast in their commitment to you. Every day these warriors don spiritual armor and take up their transcendental weapons of humility, bravery, and compassion to carry on your mission of spreading the glories of the Holy Name. Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is glorious that we have benefited from the mercy of these spiritual warriors, who are like touchstones of inspiration and guidance. And it will be glorious, Śrīla Prabhupāda, when we can serve in the same manner and offer inspiration and guidance to others.

Happy Appearance Day!

Your servants at ISKCON Washington, D.C.

## Yogyakarta

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I humbly offer my obeisances under your most exalted lotus feet.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I feel thankful that I could come in contact with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, of which you are the founder-*ācārya*. This has enabled me to gradually understand what the meaning of this life is. You are so kind to have showered your mercy upon me, who was truly fallen because my mind, words, and actions were full of sin. I only pray that through your messengers you will always continue to guide me. I hope that by this guidance, my thoughts, words, and actions will gradually change so that they are one day in tune with your desires.

The devotees of ISKCON Yogyakarta, Indonesia.  
(written by Śubhānanda Caitanya dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers (1)

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Indonesia)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we, your servants at the Indonesian BBT, offer you our most humble obeisances.

Your books are the only light that can dissipate the darkness of the dark Age of Kali. Through your very lips the Holy Name of Kṛṣṇa is spreading to every town and village in the world, fulfilling the prophecy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. All the devotees of ISKCON, both now and in the future, are Prabhupādānugas—followers in your footsteps.

All knowledge needed by human beings can be found in your Bhaktivedanta purports. Even such things as the current economic crisis Indonesia and many other Asian countries are experiencing is clearly described in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.17.39. You have even suggested a practical solution to this world economic problem, one based on the formulas given in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*! If only people would read your books, they could save the lifetimes of time they waste in useless mental speculation.

Though you left our material vision for Goloka Vṛndāvana in 1977, we know that as long as we sincerely follow your instructions, you are eternally with us, watching over us and guiding our devotional service. There is no need for us to seek guidance elsewhere.

This year we will try to publish several new books in Indonesian for your pleasure. We know how you remarked that when you see one of your books newly published in a foreign language, you feel “as if I had conquered an empire.” Although we are incompetent and full of faults, please bless us that we can always continue to serve you in this way.

Your servants at the Indonesian BBT.  
(written by Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi dāsa)

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books, being the sound incarnation of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, are all-powerful. Thus inevitably they are bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of people in the misdirected so-called civilization here in North America. Every day we see this revolution manifested in the letters that come into our offices in Los Angeles. Enclosed are a few of the letters out of many hundreds we received recently from everyday people who greatly appreciate you, your books, and your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

\* \* \*

Dear Bhaktivedanta Book Trust,

I want to thank you for being a critical part of my life! Without the distribution of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’s books, I would have never been ignited with the fire of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. My first contact with Kṛṣṇa was at my friend’s house. I saw this book, *The Science of Self-Realization,* lying on the floor, and at that moment I felt an overwhelming, irresistible attraction to borrow the book. I read a few pages and knew that I had found the truth finally! It just shows that these books are gifts that just keep on giving. I’ve now read about half of your books, but had little association with devotees. My goal right now is to join an ISKCON farm community. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jimi M.  
Island, Washington

\* \* \*

To whom it may concern,

Being a Westerner blinded by modern inventions and science, I welcomed A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’s book *Easy Journey to Other Planets* as a breath of fresh air. Not only am I interested in the subject matter, but I seek to practice it. My girlfriend introduced me to the book, and reading it was pure catharsis. If you could send me further information about the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, it would be greatly appreciated. Also, would you please relay to His Divine Grace my thanks and prayers. He proved to me that all my inner dreams and wisdom are not in vain, but are instead connected to the Universal Mind, where goodness and God (Kṛṣṇa) reign. Thank you again.

Sincerely,  
Jeremy D.  
Lansdale, Pennsylvania

\* \* \*

Dear Secretary,

I would like to say “Thank you.” I had just arrived at Denver International Airport when a young man gave me a book called *Bhagavad-gita*. I gave him a donation and took the book. I read from its pages. I could not stop reading. I agree with all that I have read. I see the need and the joy in becoming free from material addiction. I am 22 years old and just moved to Daytona Beach from England. I have thought about this a lot and know that I am ready to embrace this truth. I would give all that I have to be free from the misery of materialism. What you teach is true, and I genuinely would like to thank you.

Jonathan B.  
Daytona Beach, Florida

\* \* \*

Hello,

About two months ago my plane was delayed at the Los Angeles Airport for 5 hours. I was sitting in the terminal wondering what I was going to do for 5 hours and wishing I had a book to read when I heard a voice behind me. I turned around and was given a *Srimad Bhagavatam.* I was so surprised. I never really got a chance to thank the person who gave me this book. But I would like to say that my life has radically changed. Thank you.

Anita S.  
Honolulu, Hawaii

\* \* \*

Dear Friend,

Thank you very much for sending me the new books of His Divine Grace Srila Prabhupada. I can’t express my feeling how much I am happy to read these interesting books, and it is a great honor to be your friend. I appreciate you. And forgive me if I make any mistake because my language is Arabic. Again thank you very much.

Your servant and friend,  
Saud A.  
Richmond, Virginia

\* \* \*

Dear BBT,

I am writing to express my deepest and profound gratitude for your kindness in sending me the book *Your Ever Well-Wisher*. I am in turn humbled, brought to tears, dipped in melancholy, and then finally I find myself saying Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa. This book is one of the dearest and most valuable gifts I have ever received. As you suggest: I am regularly chanting every morning (4 rounds) and reading Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books every day. I have made a commitment to increase my rounds every two weeks and increase my support to BBT. Thank you again for this wonderful kindness

Scott Pemaquid, Maine

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your servants at the North American BBT.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your sacred lotus feet.

It is 6:00 A.M. Over the forest east of Korsnas Gard, the sun is rising, its rays entering the temple room, softly coloring your divine form and your *vyāsāsana* in shades of saffron and gold. Devotees are chanting *japa,* pacing back and forth or sitting cross-legged along the walls. From the altar room faint sounds of small bells are heard as the *pūjārīs* perform the daily *pūjā* for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Gāndharvikā-Giridhārī. At 7:15 the Deity greeting begins, followed by your *guru-pūjā*.

It is a fascinating thought that this worship of Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee is performed around the world continuously, every day, without fail. Gradually, as the sun moves across the globe, basically the same morning program takes place in all the different ISKCON temples in each time zone, one after another. This worship goes on twenty-four hours a day, in a never-ending cycle! You set this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in motion, and by your power it is still going on.

ISKCON is actually uniting the world in several different ways. You often criticized the United Nations for its inability to accomplish anything tangible. ISKCON is the real United Nations, and Korsnas Gard is a miniature U.N. in itself. Here your BBT is producing your books in more than forty languages, many of which are rather exotic: Chuvash, Bashkir, Tajik, Icelandic, to name a few. Devotees of thirty nationalities are working together here, both in book production and maintenance of the temple. At the time of writing, one of the *p\\ujārīs*comes from Armenia and another from Azerbaijan, and although their countries are in conflict, these two devotees serve peacefully together. Similarly, the devotees from the different republics of the former Yugoslavia have no difficulties serving together or associating with one another. Kṛṣṇa consciousness, your formula for uniting the people and nations of this world, actually works.

Among many other titles, we are producing a two-volume *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* in English. To read about your pastimes is most relishable; nothing is more absorbing than this. Even the chapter titles are fascinating: “On the Bowery,” “Planting the Seed,” “Breaking Ground,” “Swami Invites the Hippies,” “Jet-age Parivrājakācārya”—these titles tell stories in themselves, stories of the pure devotee’s total dedication, determination, compassion. You are the most extraordinary person, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and it is simply logical that a biography of your life is the most fascinating of all books.

The BBT has dominated the activities in this temple for most of the twenty-one years it has been your property. You never visited Korsnas Gard while you were personally present in this world, but we feel that you are always here. Please bless us so that we can continue producing your books. There are numerous problems that hamper book production, but we beg for the strength and intelligence to solve such problems and simply go on serving you and your mission. Please help us work conjointly for your pleasure.

*Your humble servants at the Northern European BBT production center in Korsnas Gard, Sweden.*

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Portuguese)

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet.

Your divine mission is progressing more and more and saving thousands of people from the clutches of Māyā. In almost every place on earth, the glories of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa are being chanted as more and more people gather to chant the Holy Name. Thousands of your divine books are being sold, and lots of *prasādam* is being distributed every year. All of this is due to your inconceivable, causeless mercy upon us and your immaculate devotion to the Lord.

On this most blessed day, let us try to glorify you properly, rededicating ourselves to your mission with a pure heart totally free from envy and anger. With a straw in our mouth, we offer our lives to you and humbly request you to give us shelter under your lotus feet.

Your eternal servants in the Portuguese BBT (Brazil).

## The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Spanish)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

One more year, and here I am, again looking at you, trying to understand my relationship with you.

I love you; and you love me, that’s obvious. And I know this love for you and your love for me make up the only meaning of my life.

I know you’re patient, but I don’t want to be slow. I know you’re tolerant, but I don’t want to repeat my mistakes again and again. It’s difficult for me, but I want to work hard for you. And I need your help.

I know your desire. You want me to publish and print in Spanish all of your books and teachings and help make them available to every single Spanish-speaking soul in the world. A clear enough mission statement.

But for this I need you to give me the necessary intelligence to organize the little resources I have (or to realize how many resources I actually have). I need you to help me to be more pure and not become distracted by everything else going on inside and outside. I need your help.

I miss so much your personal training, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I know there’s a way. I know you’ve left us with your teachings, your example. If I seriously study all these and treasure them in my heart, and if I serve you with sincerity, you’ll empower me and give me all the necessary resources to fulfill what you want from me. So I’ll do that.

And as your ISKCON becomes more supportive, I’ll get from you the necessary strength to face new obstacles and difficulties. I’m still somewhat afraid of not being protected. But I see you’re making adjustments; by your mercy I didn’t leave when it was so painful, and now I’m getting new hope that we all can come to share our love for you and your love for us, together serving you in a loving association.

So: mission, empowerment, intelligence, resources, support…You’re giving me everything I need to grow, everything I need to be happy.

You’re my leader, you’re my master, I’m your servant.

You’ve done your part; please give me the courage to do my part.

I want to be your eternal servant.

Your servants at the Spanish BBT.  
(written by Hanumān dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers (2)

## Back to Godhead Magazine

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

When I became your disciple, ISKCON was expanding without limit. As you gracefully sat upon the red crushed-velvet *vyāsāsana* in New Dvārakā, you were uniquely regal, yet grave. One could see you as a conquering monarch, a noble prophet, a simple saint, and a loving father, all at once.

From this kaleidoscope of different appearances came a single, unmistakable truth: “Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are His eternal servant. Lord Caitanya said to chant His name, serve Him, and love Him. You’ll be happy.” Afloat on a sea of intelligent young disciples ready to spend their lives in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you gracefully steered the ISKCON ship over limitless horizons to unimaginably wonderful destinations.

I was so blessed to encounter you in your magnificent Dvārakā-līlā. Yet as my life goes on, I often think of you in your Vṛndāvana-līlā, when there were no opulent ISKCON temples, no eager disciples. Poverty and obscurity accompanied you, and you never cursed or praised them. You chuckled at a list of your relatives’ names. You noted the widow, bringing water to the temple early each morning, content serving the Lord in a most helpless and humble existence. You relished your own simple existence of study, *japa,* and meditation on the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta.

You could have complained about crushing, mysterious, undeserved personal setbacks, institutional politics, suffocating heat, and a disinterested, uncaring world. But you never did. Much loftier matters entertained your thoughts. You wrote them in *Back to Godhead,* and later *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, for the benefit of unknown eyes.

Today I meditate upon you in your unfathomable, undisturbed, utterly Kṛṣṇa conscious Vṛndāvana-līlā. You always exuded the sweet taste and the fresh, earthy fragrance of Vṛndāvana-līlā, even as you immersed yourself in the state affairs of ISKCON and the world-wide business of saving the fallen souls. You were always ready to return to Vṛndāvana at a moment’s notice.

May I one day join you in that *līlā*.

Your servants at Back to Godhead Magazine.  
(written by Kalakaṇṭha dāsa)

## Bhakta Program Institute

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

In 1965 you came to America. After many years of struggle in India you were now faced with the challenge of turning the minds and hearts of the most materialistic nation of the world to Kṛṣṇa. This was by no means an easy undertaking, as you humbly stated in a poem written upon your arrival. One year passed alone; your determination did not waver. Then it happened: suddenly devotees appeared. As if by magic. Sent by your spiritual master, you once said. Sincere souls, willing to sacrifice—young, intelligent, resourceful boys and girls who joyfully allowed you to chip away at their rough edges.

Sometimes we get an inkling of the immensity of the mission you began, as if we were also arriving at Boston Harbor, seeing before us what seems to be an impossible task. What can we do? How will we ever be able to carry on your work? That you have answered in a very profound way: Never lose faith in the instructions of your spiritual master or the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Never forget that everyone in the material world is suffering and that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the only solution. Give up sense gratification, false pride. Be humble. Always remember Kṛṣṇa. Preach, preach, preach. And above all, cooperate. These are just a few of the many jewellike instructions a disciple can glean from your example.

Are we to understand that now the magic of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is no longer available? That there was an expiry date on the process you gave us? Obviously not. You declared war on illusion, and the war is not over. You have pinpointed the enemy and given us the weapons. Now we just have to rally behind you and push forward. The nectar of remaining faithful to the order of our spiritual master is still available. The bliss of struggling to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness has not gone sour. The storehouse of love of God is not depleted. Fallen souls are everywhere. Preaching opportunities abound. More surrender is asked of us—total dedication to your mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness for your pleasure. Please bless us and guide us so that we may be successful in pleasing you.

Your servants at the Bhakta Program Institute, USA.

## Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Before coming to Kṛṣṇa consciousness we were told, by our religion, or faith, that there was an ineffable “something” with the appellation “God.” Of course, we hoped that this was true, but certainly we had no other understanding of Godhead. Nor was any further elucidation proffered, with the exception of the examples of a loud voice from the sky and a representative burning bush—both difficult to reconcile with a loving, personal understanding of God. However, through your exacting and uncompromising translation of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*, and your Bhaktivedanta purports, the generic title, “God,” was transformed into the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

And so we’ve come to understand that not only is Kṛṣṇa wondrous and merciful, but His pure devotee is similarly so. In *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.7–8, we read that Lord Kṛṣṇa comes anywhere and everywhere to deliver the pious, annihilate the miscreants, and reestablish the principles of religion. His servants, the pure devotees, acting as His agents, perform the self-same function by targeting the fallen *jīvas* from every conceivable angle of vision in their presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is implemented in a way just suitable to soften the *jīvas’* hearts so that they may gradually make progress back to their birthright, the revival of their eternal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are perfectly acting on Kṛṣṇa’s behalf.

You delivered those attempting to be pious by explaining to them that the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa produces plain, or simple, living and high thinking. Notwithstanding modern society’s policy of high living and poor thinking, you continually pressed the spiritual issue. Unfortunately, we were trained up to practice barely living and hardly thinking, and it’s counterpart, hardly living and barely thinking. However, because of your purity, patience, and compassion, your message cut through the fog we’d surrounded ourselves with and touched a chord. To clarify this point regarding chanting, you further explained that “this sound vibration, and Kṛṣṇa, the original Personality of Godhead \[are\] the same. By sound vibration, you can have Kṛṣṇa associated with you.…\[When\] you are walking on the street, if you are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, that means Kṛṣṇa is going with you.…While walking on the street you see up on the horizon…\[that\] the moon is going with you.” And so you gave the example from your life, from when you were a *gṛhastha,* approximately fifty years earlier: “My second son…was walking with me on the street, and he \[asked\] me, ‘Father, why the moon is going with us?’” You explained that “As you find that the moon is going with you, similarly, by chanting, if the moon has got such power—it is a material thing; it can go with you—don’t you think the Supreme Lord, who is all-powerful, He cannot remain with you? Yes. He can. By His potency, He can remain with you, *provided* you are also qualified to keep His company.”

To the miscreants—who hoped to avoid your shoes—you addressed these words in no uncertain terms: “So I have protested this false God-consciousness in all my purports. This is my business, to punish all these rascals. You see? I become very much angry \[if\] anyone says before me, ‘I am God, he is God, everyone.…’ I cannot control myself. I am so obstinate enemy of these rascals. I want to kick them on their face, but it is incivility.…But I want to kick them. It will be my pleasure. Never mind I go to hell.”

And to all the *vikarmīs, jñānīs,* and *yogīs* you challenged, “We are going door to door of all rascals. I tell it, declare it publicly. Anyone who is not Kṛṣṇa conscious, he is a rascal number one. I declare it. Come, any intelligent man. I shall talk with him. I shall prove that he is rascal number one if he has no Kṛṣṇa consciousness. That’s a fact.”

Upon your arrival in London in 1969, you were asked, “What do you try and teach, sir?” Whereupon you immediately replied, “I am trying to teach what you have forgotten.…That is my mission. Any nonsense can come to me, I shall prove that there is God.…Unfortunately, we are trying to forget God; therefore we are embracing so many miseries of life. So I am simply preaching that you have Kṛṣṇa consciousness and be happy. Don’t be swayed by these nonsense waves of *māyā,* or illusion. That is my request.” Thus as the chaste servitor of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, and on Lord Kṛṣṇa’s behalf, you continued to reestablish and perpetuate actual religious principles.

When asked by a disciple how he could repay you, you humbly instructed, “You don’t require to repay. \[chuckles\] I am not giving you anything. It is Kṛṣṇa’s property. You repay to Kṛṣṇa. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and He will be repaid.…Nobody can repay.…Therefore it is better to remain always obliged.” So we fall at your feet, thanking you for eternally obliging us, and for delivering the pious, annihilating the miscreants, and reestablishing the principles of religion.

Always hoping for a place in your lotus shadow, we remain,  
Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Center in Charlotte, Virginia.  
(written by Amit Ācāra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In the enumeration of the twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee is the quality “fixed in the Absolute Truth.” When we examine Your Divine Grace, we find this quality abundantly evident. In the more than two thousand hours of tape recordings, in the thousands of pages of books, nowhere do we find the inconsistencies and contradictions that are the hallmark of the conditioned soul. We never see the bewilderment caused by the temporary comings and goings, ups and downs, of this material world. You are always seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead and His energies, and realizing their true nature. At no time is there any hint that you might have been mistaken about something and needed to change it, or that you needed a vacation, or that you were bored, or that your life was being wasted and perhaps there was a better career out there for you. This quality of being so fixed up shows that you have attained to the topmost stages of *bhakti*. In your article “Spontaneous Love of Godhead,” in one of the 1960 *Back to Godhead* magazines, you state:

By attainment of bhakti, the devotee:

1\. Does not wish for any other engagement.  
2\. Does not fear from anything.  
3\. Does not envy any object.  
4\. Does not enjoy sensually.  
5\. Does not endeavor for anything mundane.

When we examine your person, we find all these qualities. Especially the first quality is most evident. You wanted only the engagement of preaching. There was never any other desire, nor did you engage in any activity that did not contribute to this overriding urge. On many occasions you would quote:

jīvera svarūpa hoy, nitya kṛṣṇa dāsa

This eternal engagement of service to Kṛṣṇa in the preaching of His message is your legacy to us, your incompetent servants. By your mercy we have been given the opportunity to try to follow this example of constant engagement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In fact it is the only hope for this entire planet of misery and death. Although this statement may sound exaggerated and fanatical, the living entities are hopelessly entangled in the misidentification with the material body, and no one other than yourself and your servants are practically conveying the message that we are not these bodies but spirit soul. So it is no exaggeration. It is backed by *śāstra* and a philosophy so sound that there is no serious challenge from any quarter.

And yet we vacillate, procrastinate, and somehow avoid the surrender that we know will bring us true happiness. Fortunately you are always reminding us and bringing us back to your lotus feet by having instituted the festivals of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and especially this Vyāsa-pūjā festival. Please bless us that we may never forget these festivals and the opportunity to worship your lotus feet and serve your mission.

Your humble servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Only by smearing our bodies with the dust of your lotus feet can we get free from our *anarthas*. No other process is as effective. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who out of great compassion brought this Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West in spite of so many difficulties!

We humbly pray that you will continue to engage us in what you called the most important preaching arm of ISKCON, the Bhaktivedanta Institute. We humbly pray to be but an instrument in the fight against the materialistic attitude infecting the minds of scholars and scientists.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you built a house in which the whole world can live. We pray that you engage us in building a nice conference room in that house. Please engage us in that nice room to meet with scientists and glorify you as well as Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya. You taught us that scientists and all learned circles should engage in the glorification of Lord Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22). You have said, “Philosophy and science should be engaged to establish the glory of the Lord. Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of God on a scientific basis” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22, purport). You want us to defeat the materialistic mentality of the scientists but not bar their entrance into your house. That divine house is held together by Lord Caitanya’s all-encompassing mercy.

We fervently pray that you will continue to reveal to us the way in which scientists can be purified of their materialistic mentality. By your inspiration and instruction, we are developing many programs to engage the scientists: a graduate program, conferences, publication of books, research programs, research fellowships with scientists, lectureships, etc. Please inspire us to make these programs fully blossom, so we can offer them at your lotus feet.

Your Bhaktivedanta Institute seems to be poised to fulfill the central desire you have for it, namely, to lead the development of the next phase of material science in a way that takes them closer to *Bhāgavata* science “according to their own way.”

Apart from *Purāṇas* and Vedic literatures, scientifically it is the modern science, according to their way, we can convince. *Purāṇas* you may not believe the authority. I may not believe. But scientific truth has to be accepted. \[Room Conversation, Mumbai, 31 March 1977\]

We feel that you are ever so mercifully revealing the very first but absolutely great steps of your master plan for preaching in this room. Kindly give us all the sincerity and humility to understand how assisting you in your service is always greater than doing our own individual service within the Bhaktivedanta Institute. That will be the best way, it seems to us, to ensure that we all carry the spirit of co-operation and serve you. We pray to you on this auspicious day to realize how important it is to keep the focus of scientific preaching within Bhaktivedanta Institute.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai, India, and Berkeley, California, USA.

## College TSKP

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Hare Kṛṣṇa monks, on the road,  
Traveling in a caravan,  
mṛdaṅga, books, kṛṣṇa-prasād, ready to unload,  
being inspired to serve Gaura-Nitāi’s plan.

A nectarean instruction from a letter  
of February 2nd , 1972,  
the following we are inspired to do:  
“The traveling SKP and teaching in  
the schools and colleges which you are  
organizing is excellent proposal.…Simply  
traveling and preaching, and infiltrating  
the schools where young boys and girls  
are interested to learn something which  
will satisfy them. In both activities you  
may distribute many of our literatures,  
and that will please me very much.”

You have given the essence,  
Rāja-Vidyā, “the king of education,”  
your Bhaktivedanta purports,  
comforting us with your presence.  
May we distribute the “most secret of  
all secrets” throughout the nation.

You are the true, transcendental professor,  
releasing sincere seekers from Māyā’s maze,  
counteracting the demoniac ways  
of the Kali-yuga aggressor.  
This is truly worthy of praise,  
You have given us the weapons:  
*mahā-mantra, śāstra, sādhu-saṅga,*  
and *kṛṣṇa-prasād—*  
essential life-saving gems.

Offering the students some real solace,  
Kṛṣṇa’s mercy from Las Vegas to Minneapolis,  
Self-realization, not sense gratification.

“To make the mind austere is  
to detach it from sense gratification.  
It should be so trained that it can be  
always thinking of doing good for others.”

To see everyone as brothers and mothers,  
the spiritual vision  
that we may eternally serve  
the *saṅkīrtan* mission.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us shelter in the house you built for the whole world. May we express our love to you by working together in inviting everyone to participate in Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi’s *saṅkīrtana-yajña—*“everlasting and joyfully performed.”

Your servants of the College TSKP (Traveling SanKirtan Party).

## Festival of India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At an advanced age you traveled to America on a ship, and later you traveled all over the world, underwent many hardships, translated volumes of transcendental literature—all to deliver Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message to the English-speaking people, and then to the rest of the world, on the order of your guru. You have done what no one has ever done before.

Walking through a Kṛṣṇa consciousness festival, conditioned souls get a glimpse of the spiritual world: the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* resounds all over; they taste *kṛṣṇa-prasādam;* they read the exhibit panels (which are like giant pages from your books) with great attention. It appears that you are directly preaching to them. When they leave a festival, they usually have at least one of your books in their hands.

You are still enlightening the world with your transcendental message. To us, you are fully present at every festival.

Every day, and especially today, on your holy appearance day, we sincerely pray that you give us the determination to understand and follow your instructions.

The Festival of India crew.

## Food for Life Global

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of the Food for Life devotees around the world, please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have a vision that one day the world will stand up and praise the devotees for the incredible service they have done for humanity. I foresee that the day will come when all the people of the world will get a chance to relish *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and spontaneously dance and sing in ecstasy. I can see them now—old men, young men, women, children, blacks, whites, Muslims, Jews, Catholics, Hindus, Buddhists—people of all walks of life—smiling, singing, and feasting together without inhibition. As Lord Caitanya’s vision unfolds, a wave of love and respect drowns them in the excitement and relief of peace and prosperity.

Central to that vision is the understanding that food and children are the heart and soul of every home, community, and nation. Both food and children hold similar values. They represent new life, energy, sustenance, growth, and change.

Anyone who has worked the fields and planted seedlings knows what miracles Mother Nature unfolds from the soil—bringing forth billions of tons of new life every day as her selfless offering to her dependents. The energy produced far outweighs the meager investments of man and animal. The fruits and vegetables she provides form the basic sustenance and the most important commodity of every community on the planet, from insects to humans. For what is the use of massive wealth if one has no food to eat? That food, when ingested, is quickly transformed into bodily strength, physical growth, movement, and action. And when man eats according to nature’s arrangement, that food fuels and nourishes the intellect as well, leading to clarity, self-realization, and proper discrimination. *Kṛṣṇa-prasādam* is the ultimate expression of nature’s divine arrangement.

The same miraculous episodes unfold in the life of a child. The newborn child emerges from the mother into the world, bringing forth a new generation, energy, and fresh idealism. Like food, children are the vitality of the family, community and nation—sustaining tradition and the purpose of life itself: education, happiness, and love. Children also represent growth and continuity—essential to all species. And they also symbolize change and the need to constantly adjust and adapt to the never-ending transition of the material world.

It is with this understanding that we believe that *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* will play an integral role in the respiritualization of the planet. Even more so if somehow or other we can convince the children of the world to cook, eat, and distribute *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*! To this end we believe that Hare Krishna Food for Life needs to be introduced into schools, colleges, and all other fields of youth education. Indeed, for our vision to unfold, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* needs to become a household word, a global institution in its own right. For until the people of the world understand the necessity and benefits of eating sanctified vegetarian food, they will never understand the basics of self-realization. As the emblem of “new life, energy, sustenance, growth, and change,” the youth are an effective channel to the heart of society. We believe, therefore, that our own ISKCON youth should lead us in meeting challenge to manifest this *prasādam* revolution!

You told us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that “spiritual life begins with the tongue” and that a “meat-eater can never understand God.” These two statements confirm your commitment to massive *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* distribution. To further elucidate your commitment, you said, “Everyone should be given a chance to take *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and thus be induced to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.” And if that was not clear enough, you explicitly told your temple presidents to “invite the world. We \[ISKCON\] can feed them.” All these jewels from your mouth inspire us in our humble efforts to purify the conditioned souls with *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*, knowing that by distributing *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* we are pleasing you immensely and paving the way for the success of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Our confusion and disappointment, however, is that our faith and enthusiasm for this service does not always meet with acceptance among the ISKCON community. As a result, Food for Life is often understaffed, under-funded, and misrepresented. It is a crying shame, especially when we consider that *prasādam* distribution is one of ISKCON’s *aṅgas* and that “the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is based on this principle: chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* at every moment, as much as possible, both inside and outside of the temples, and, as far as possible, distribute *prasādam*.”

Our prayer to you today, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that you will please acknowledge our sincere efforts and empower us to fulfill your desire to “distribute *prasādam* as far as possible.”

Begging your mercy and praying to realize and unify our vision with yours.

Your prasādam distributors around the world.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Quiet woods.  
Listen carefully.  
The stream can be heard splashing on the rocks;  
It is the stream the cows drink from.

Grassy meadows.  
Listen carefully.  
Grass high as our knees, swaying in the breeze,  
Delicacy for the cows.

Fields of grain.  
Watch carefully.  
Glistening in the sunlight like ocean waves,  
The land tilled by the oxen.

Down by the barn.  
Listen carefully.  
Cows taking shelter,  
Mooing in greeting.

Pristine beauty, is this land.  
Land, man, and cow.  
Formula for happiness,  
All under Your shelter.

What is that sound?  
Listen carefully.  
The cow is moaning,  
Moaning in distress

What is that sight?  
Watch carefully.  
The cow is distressed.  
Gone are the grassy meadows, the cool stream, the shelter.

What is that sight?  
Watch carefully.  
Slaughterhouses, feedlots.  
Neglect where protection was guaranteed.

Pristine beauty, is this land.  
Land, man, and cow,  
Formula for happiness,  
All under Your shelter.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have instructed us.  
Listen carefully.  
“Protect the cows, bulls, and land.  
If they are happy, everyone is happy.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have instructed us.  
Listen carefully.  
“Harm to the cows, bulls, and calves  
Creates all of society’s troubles.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have instructed us.  
Listen carefully.  
“The cow is the representative of the earth, the bull, the emblem of religiosity,  
The land, the provider of all wealth.”

Pristine beauty, is this land.  
Land, man, and cow,  
Formula for happiness,  
All under Your shelter.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have blessed us with service.  
Let us listen carefully.  
First ISCOWP and now the Ministry of Cow Protection and Agriculture.  
Protection agencies for the cow, bull, and land.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have blessed us with service.  
Let us watch carefully  
The development of cow protection world-wide,  
Humankind living simply from the land.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Let us listen and watch carefully.  
Bless us with the ability to fulfill your desire,  
A society based on Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cow and land.

Pristine beauty, is this land.  
Land, man, and cow.  
Formula for happiness,  
All under Your shelter.

Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and the Ministry of Cow Protection and Agriculture.

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories unto you for spreading the glories of the holy name throughout the world!

In 1936 you wrote a letter to your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda, requesting him to give you a particular service to perform. Just before passing away, he wrote you back requesting you “to preach in English the cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu as I had heard it from him.” You took this order to be the purpose of your life, and against all odds you successfully accomplished your mission.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 9.18) you explain that ISKCON is one of the branches of the Caitanya tree:

*viśa viśa śākhā kari’ eka eka maṇḍala*  
*mahā-mahā-śākhā chāila brahmāṇḍa sakala*

**“Thus the branches of the Caitanya tree formed a cluster or society, with great branches covering all the universe.”**

“\[Purport:\] Our International Society for Krishna Consciousness is one of the branches of the Caitanya tree.”

By the mercy of Your Divine Grace we have seen this branch spread all over the world, saturating every town and village with the glories of the holy name of Lord Gaurāṅga. Wait, what about those souls rotting in the deep dark dungeons of our society, placed in isolation away from the general populace? Is there any mercy for those forgotten cursed individuals shackled like animals, who never once heard the glories of the holy name or tasted the sweet nectarean ambrosia of *prasādam*? Who have never associated with *paramahaṁsas,* whose hearts do not remember the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa even once, and whose heads do not bow down even once before Lord Kṛṣṇa?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your infinite compassion you have desired that a twig sprout from this branch of the Caitanya tree; this twig has manifested as the ISKCON Prison Ministry.

As menial servants of this twig of compassion, we humbly follow in the footsteps of you, our beloved spiritual master. Our goal is to increase the number of books distributed to inmates until every correctional facility is inundated with love of Kṛṣṇa. The dedicated servants of the ISKCON Prison Ministry will feel themselves unsuccessful until the majority of inmates are wearing Tulasī beads around there necks and holding *japa* beads in their hands as they chant the holy name. To accomplish this purpose, we have vowed, “If we ever get money, we will distribute books.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful upon us monkeylike servants; we have no qualifications, nor any good qualities. All we have is the desire to assist you in carrying out the order of your beloved spiritual master. Please engage us fifth-class men and women in this most important mission of delivering the fallen souls from the clutches of Māyā. If Lord Caitanya came to save the most fallen, then who is more fallen than those inmates locked behind prison walls?

On behalf of the devotee inmates from all over the Unite States and the dedicated staff of your ISKCON Prison Ministry, we express our most sincere gratitude unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for opening our darkened eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. Please bless this ministry and use our bodies like puppets; make us dance as you desire. We place our heads eternally under you lotus feet.

Your menial servants at the ISKCON Prison Ministry.  
(written by Rādhākānta dāsa)

## ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to your Divine Grace!

You have a vision of an educational system where the students will be guided to become devotees of Kṛṣṇa and be of ideal character. Such a system means a total revolution in our modern world. Yet, when a pure devotee such as you desires something, surely Lord Kṛṣṇa makes all the arrangements. We beg, therefore, to stay within the shelter of your vision and desire for these children. Then we can hope and pray for Kṛṣṇa to bring us success beyond mundane expectations. Please shower your blessings upon our school and curriculum project. May we always serve you within the limits of your instructions so as to achieve the unlimited spiritual benefits of devotional service.

Your servants at ISKCON School, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA

## New Vrajamandala Gurukula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

From a lost corner of Spain we are trying to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by taking care of a small group of children here in New Vrajamandala, which is located in the countryside ninety minutes by car from Madrid.

Last December one of our teachers went to visit her family and, while she was talking to a relative, asked him what he thought about her children, who were around them playing and talking. “Do you want to know my opinion?” he said. “Keep them as natural as they are now for as long as you can. Contact with nature is giving them all the best.”

This was the personal opinion of a person who had no knowledge of the influence that daily *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindacandra is having on these children. Nor did he know about the glories of *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* chanting and dancing in the association of devotees. Nor did he know about the result of following the principles of *dharma*, or about the potency of daily honoring *prasādam,* or about receiving knowledge from the line of disciplic succession in the form of your books, or about the unique benefit one receives by being among those who glorify your matchless service to your beloved spiritual master, our *parama-parama-guru,* through this preaching movement you named the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for dedicating your every breath to granting us this most wonderful gift, your beloved ISKCON.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us appreciate your divine glories.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us to surpass all sorts of obstacles that we face while trying to bring forward our humble offering, this small school.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us in such a way that we will find the ability to strengthen the Kṛṣṇa consciousness already within the hearts of these little children, who are your descendants.

And please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us all to eternally remain the servants of your beloved servants.

Your servants at the New Vrajamandala Gurukula, Spain.

## New Vrindavan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to your kind service to Lord Kṛṣṇa!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you for the many gifts you’ve bestowed upon us.

First and foremost of these gifts is the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. As your movement continues on, we get more and more realization of just how much we need to thank you for giving us the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa. The devotees who have continued to chant are always making nice contributions to your society, and through their enthusiasm they are inducing others to chant. Our biggest critics are those who used to chant and no longer do. Please bless us that we will never forget that spreading the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* was the whole purpose for which you dedicated your life, and that we will not forget the taste you gave us for chanting, and the desire you gave us to get out of the material world once and for all.

Also, thank you for giving us a pure spiritual mission and direction for our lives.

Thank you for your gift of transcendental knowledge and realization, which you so kindly bestowed upon us.

Thank you for giving us a spiritual society in which to live that is worldwide, with so many temples and farms.

Thank you for giving us book distribution and *prasādam* distribution. In these ways we have a way to try to help the world at large become purified.

Finally, thank you for so many wonderful spiritual associates with whom we can have loving exchanges and grow spiritually together.

All glories to your ever-increasing mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your loving kindness upon us.

Your servants at the New Vrindavan Community in West Virginia, USA.

## Padayātrā England

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

1990, 4:00 a.m. I met Ravi carrying a bucket, looking for cold water in the dark, outside of Dublin. Rain is falling, winds are strong. “We must be mad.”

I blow the conch, open the curtains for *maṅgala-ārati.* The Deities glow enchantingly in the candle-light. You sit in front, grave as ever. To my amazement there are over thirty devotees chanting and dancing joyfully before you and their Lordships! After two solid months of camping in the rain, it didn’t matter what the weather was like. We covered you with two chaddars at night, and one devotee gave you his new oilskin coat. We were young, preaching like real *sannyāsīs,* fearless and joyful. In reality you were the only devotee to complete the full journey from Belfast to Moscow. Some of us fell by the roadside, picked off by Māyā. Others changed services or *āśramas*. But whatever happened, without a shadow of a doubt we will never forget the freedom and bliss of the open road and surrender to your mission. “Where do we sleep? What do we eat?” become the source of laughter, not anxiety. You always supplied, and we were always protected and over-fed.

1998: After eight years we returned to war-torn Ireland. While we chanted, the bombs exploded in the next town. Your peace formula is perfect, and those who get your books and see your *mūrti* are close to the edge of the material world. A few steps will make the difference. A spirit soul returning to the real home. Lord Caitanya’s disciples mainly came from the upper classes. Nityānanda Prabhu could do what Caitanya Mahāprabhu “couldn’t do.”

“Nityānanda Prabhu delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, but a servant of Nityānanda Prabhu, by His grace, can deliver many thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 16.65).

We can get the mercy by being part of your mission, by simply spreading your glories. In Ireland we reached nationwide with appearances on television and newspapers, your peace formula overriding the politics of war. While we were in West Galway, the *padayātrā* attracted the local television station, and for the first time your message was proclaimed nationwide in the Irish language.

Your eternal servants on the English Padayātrā.

## Padayātrā India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our must humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on your most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā!

This is our fourteenth year on the road. We have been walking and preaching on your behalf nonstop. You are always with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are traveling around India with us in your *mūrti* form. You are mercifully giving your *darśana* to millions of people every year, along with the presiding Deities of Padayātrā, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundara.

In December 1976 you wrote a letter to His Holiness Lokanāth Maharaja.

Try to distribute Hindi and other vernacular literature.…Do not depend on outside help. Also, for financial support depend on Krishna. That is preaching success. People’s appreciation will be practical when they help you financially also. Also, book distribution. We are maintaining our huge establishment by selling books. That is wanted. In foreign countries they are distributing huge quantities of books. Money is needed, that you have to collect. You can’t expect money from outside. That is not successful preaching.

You wanted Padayātrā to be an independent program, with no financial help from outside. Although we made some endeavor, at first we did not succeed. We continued to try to please you in this way, and finally you we succeeded. In 1997 we achieved that goal. We did not take any financial help from outside. We were able to support our program mainly through the distribution of your transcendental literature.

This year, 1998, we continue to be self-sufficient, and by your mercy our book scores are increasing more and more. For your pleasure, we would like to share with you the book scores from just one typical month. In August 1997 we distributed 1,532 mahā-big books (mostly your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and *Kṛṣṇa* book), 286 big books, and 2,148 small books, for a total of 3,966 books and 2,906 book points. These books were distributed in all the towns and villages we walked through. We are determined to continue this performance. On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we want to make this news an offering to you.

Thank you for engaging us in your service. Please engage us more and more.

Your walking servants,  
The devotees on Padayātrā, India

## Prabhupāda Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The sun slowly sets over the gently flowing Yamunā River, and the view from Keśīghāṭa is one of profound peace and tranquillity: a higher order of serenity clearly belonging to another world. Even the monkeys seem awed as they stare out over the scene, transfixed. The only sounds are a gentle trickle against the bathing *ghāṭa,* mixed with the quiet prayers of an old Vrajavāsī *brāhmaṇa* reverentially offering ancient prayers to Śrī Yamunā-devī.

Somehow this river seems very wise and very happy as it winds through Vṛndāvana *dhāma*. Could it be that somewhere out there, just off the shoreline, one inconceivably beautiful young boy named Kṛṣṇa, the reservoir of all knowledge and happiness, is right now laughing and playing with His friends within her fortunate waters?

The ability to appreciate all of this is a most generous gift. A gift from one person who struggled very hard to give it to us. Is it possible to ever repay you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for these free matchless gifts to us? Like the gift of awareness of our infinitely rich spiritual culture, and of our eternal place within it?

The answer, of course, is no. We can no more repay you for these spiritual gifts, which came from beyond our consciousness, than an ant can repay the human being who lifts it out of harms way.

Fortunately for us, however, we are not ants, and as humans we can make a repayment attempt. In fact, it is our duty as disciples and grand-disciples to make that attempt. One way we can do this (one way I can do this) is to ask ourselves, “What can I do to please you today, Śrīla Prabhupāda? How can I bring a broad smile to your face today?” And then by doing our best to follow through on the inspired answers we will surely receive to these questions from the Lord within.

I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the strength to steadily follow through, in the hopes of one day really pleasing you. Thank you for your immeasurable gifts, and for your causeless mercy so that I may always be in the association of sincere devotees of the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

From your eternal servants at The Prabhupāda Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

For the past six and a half years, several followers of Your Divine Grace have been coming here, in the foothills of North Carolina, in an attempt to revive or increase their service to your lotus feet. We now have nearly seventy devotees living here, and we decided to name this rural community project after you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We named it Prabhupāda Village.

This name is a daily reminder of our life’s most precious opportunity: to associate with you, our eternal spiritual master. Because it increases our consciousness of you, it is a catalyst in our personal and communal endeavors to please you. It calls on us to constantly introspect and analyze if our desires are in line with yours and the Supreme Lord’s. This name is also a prayer for your mercy to help us do things right.

Because it is Kali-yuga, there is so much confusion in the world, so much quarrel, hypocrisy, cheating, and so on. Even among your well-intended devotees, there are great confusion and disagreements concerning the right understanding on several issues. And we, the residents of Prabhupāda Village, are no exception to this confusion. The devotees who decided to come and live here had many reasons to do so. One fairly common motivation for many was an opportunity to define for ourselves the community’s leadership and management structures. But so far, we’ve been mostly busy building houses and getting established as independent family units, with a rather vague idea of a future community effort toward *varṇāśrama* and self-sufficiency. Our first main community effort is to build a temple. And, trying to benefit from our past experiences, we have embarked upon the difficult task of planning how this temple is going to be run and how it will fit in and contribute to your ISKCON, the house in which the whole world can live. Obviously, a bright, flourishing, and blissful future now depends on our ability to understand your desires, put aside our personal agendas, and show our love for you by cooperating with one another.

Therefore, on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray to you to guide us out of confusion and through this difficult time. We need a clear sense of direction from you, our ever well-wisher, so that our lives may be worth living, and possibly be an example for others to follow. We are helpless. Without your clear, unequivocal guidance, all our endeavors are plagued with doubts and speculations. You instruct us that everything we need to know is in your books, but there again, we too often fail to read or understand them.

We are convinced that a pure devotee of your caliber is never limited by so-called physical appearance and disappearance, though your ways to guide us may be restricted mainly due to our lack of faith. In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.9.8 you write, “The potency of transcendental sound is never minimized because the vibrator is apparently absent.” And your sincere followers daily experience your clear, direct, and specific guidance through this transcendental sound in the form of your lectures, books, etc.

So the problem is with us. And it will end only when we become strict followers and use everything and every situation as an opportunity to please you and the Lord and put our own plans completely aside. In other words, we will have to associate with this transcendental sound more sincerely.

It is said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the mercy of the Lord is always above the devotee’s expectations, and it is also said the pure devotee is more merciful than the Lord. So we beg you to make us realize that our best self-interest lies in a strong *sādhana* and in this two-way-street cooperation you requested from us. In that same purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.9.8, you also write, “Following the order of the bona fide spiritual master is the only duty of the disciple, and this completely faithful execution of the order of the bona fide spiritual master is the secret of success.”

We do not want this place to be Prabhupāda Village in name only. We want it to be a lasting success; we want to make it worthy of you, another project you will be proud of, a place where you will be attracted to stay and where everyone will experience your spiritual presence by the intense Kṛṣṇa conscious atmosphere charged with spiritual bliss and ecstatic *kīrtanas*.

Your aspiring servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina.  
(written by Mukunda dāsa)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. Serving them, I’m alive.

Thank you again and again for letting me remain to play my little part in your big ISKCON orchestra.

You are the amazing composer of the most wonderful symphonies, which you direct us in performing for the pleasure of the Supreme Lord Gaurahari and His loving associates.

In your orchestra some of us are more advanced musicians, some apprentices, some professors and teacher, some new students just learning how to read the musical notation, barely keeping the tempo and remaining in tune.

Still, you are such an expert conductor that you know how to engage each and everyone of us according to our present capacity.

You write the notes in each part for all of us to play.

Your orchestra is ever-expanding.

You started single-handedly, tapping on your little drum and singing with your deep, intense, loving, transcendental voice. Many have been attracted by you, and many are still coming and joining, increasing the size of your orchestra, more and more.

Your orchestra is formed by many sections, with many different musical instruments and choirs.

At different moments, under your direction, different sections emphasize the theme of your musical composition, with some of the best musicians playing their solo parts, supported by the others. Through your big ISKCON orchestra your music expands and extends everywhere, carrying your deep unfathomable feelings of love of Godhead, attracting all the souls from all sections, liberated or not liberated, with full satisfaction of the Supreme Lord.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, when we open our ears and pay attention to your instructions, you enter our hearts. We just want to play your music, not ours. Actually, we want your music to become ours. We do not want to add, subtract, or change anything, for we all know it is perfect just the way you’ve composed it. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, direct us and we’ll play. We do not want crazy solos disrupting the harmony and creating cacophonous effects. You are the master, we are all servants.

Your divine personality, reservoir of the most wonderful qualities, gradually becomes revealed in the heart of your surrendered devotees, the most fortunate souls in the universe.

Your transcendental association cleanses the heart of any tinge of superfluous nondevotional desires, clearing away every sort of fruitive action and empiric philosophical speculation from the pure and natural love for Śrī Krsna and His devotee that eternally resides within.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, my lord and master, please, even if I’m not a good musician or a professor, even if I cannot play properly, sing properly, or properly keep the time and read the notation, I could still hold for you the books of your transcendental musical scores and turn the pages, again and again, at your command.

Whatever my future will be, please let me remain forever engaged in serving your divine lotus feet, my very life and soul.

Your menial servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, in Terni and Florence, Italy  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Śaraṇāgati Dhāma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the guiding force and source of inspiration for all time. You were once asked by a reporter what would happen after you left this planet. “I shall never die, I will live forever in my books!” was your reply as the devotees present erupted in a tumultuous, “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

You have said that the vibration of the spiritual master is living and is actual association. Within your books you remain personally present for the upliftment and deliverance of future generations. In your presence even the greatest problems can be surmounted. Insurmountable obstacles only exist in the absence of your association. Some have said we must go elsewhere for knowledge Your Divine Grace did not give instruction on. Either they have not done their homework, or if you did not comment on a matter, it has no valid relevance. We have seen that those who have gone elsewhere, despite your many warnings, have created havoc for themselves and others. Everything for attaining complete perfection in unalloyed devotional service is within your Bhaktivedanta purports. You are the founder and *ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You went to great lengths to impress this fact into our dull brains simply to protect us from deviations wrought by outside influences.

Your mission was to deliver the world from voidism and impersonalism. Unfortunately, we are so impersonal and in such a void that we cannot realize that you are still with us. Once you heard a criticism by your Godbrothers that you did not visit the *samādhi mandira* of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta in Māyāpur. Your emphatic reply was that you did not consider that your spiritual master was lying within that *samādhi* but that rather wherever you went he was by your side and that there was not a moment he was not in your association. In so many ways, you showed by your practical example what it meant to constantly associate with the spiritual master via his instructions, or *vāṇī*. Thus you are an actual *ācārya*. Many of your contemporaries missed this point, and rather than try to serve the instructions of the *ācārya,* they tried occupy the post of *ācārya*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are eternally sentient, so please protect us from these impersonal calamities. Please allow us to eternally remain your humble servants at Śaraṇāgati Dhāma, which is one very small room within the house you have created for the entire world to live in.

Your servants at Śaraṇāgati Dhāma, Ashcroft, British Columbia, Canada.

## Śrī Rūpānuga Paramārthika Vidyāpīṭha

**The Ambassador**

You sit upon the seat before us,  
the seat of our memory,  
the seat of our heart.

Reaching out to us,  
you touch our heart  
with the tender care  
of the Youthful Couple,  
who sent you.

Sent you  
for us—  
who are unworthy,  
proud, foolish,  
and fallen.

You request us  
to join you—  
and thus Them—  
to be happy again,  
a sweetness long forgotten,  
back in the company of old friends,  
real friends—  
no longer alone;  
once again sheltered,  
once again able to serve,  
free from the pain  
of fear and exploitation,  
safe from the nightmare—  
the painful nightmare  
of material existence.

You offer us a place  
in the glorious mission
